# Android App

密码登录：输入 HTTPS hub 地址后，在系统浏览器输入管理员设置的连接密码。通过一次性授权码和 PKCE 返回 App，密码原文不上传。扫码、配对链接入口保留。

`android/`: the Android client, in Kotlin + Jetpack Compose. It connects to the same hub with the same API as iOS (`docs/protocol/protocol.md`); shared additive fields, such as task-row identity, are available to every client.
minSdk 30 (Android 11), compileSdk 37, targetSdk 37. The UI comes in three languages, 简体中文, English and 日本語. It follows the system by default and can be chosen in Settings; see "Internationalization".

In one sentence: on an Android phone, see the Agents in the engine of each of your computers (herdr, Herdr App, Orca, Tern or Paseo, one per computer, `docs/integrations/engines.md`), send messages, answer questions / approve plans / grant permissions, and press the options of terminal dialogs,
without any push account.

## Current implementation

The body of this page was checked against commit `f3a13fdb7672927197cb8ac38bccb9c65671677b` of 2026-10-31. The current code is the multi-module redesign; per-item status is in [Android P0 status](../archive/android/android-p0-status.md). Verification records of the old version stay further down and do not count as acceptance of this version.

The follow-up branding / input compatibility work for #277 landed in batches: #745 only updates human-visible branding and keeps `AppTab.Persona`, serialized values and old system components; #746 registers and accepts both `persona://` and `persona://`; #758 reads Debug launch metadata from the canonical Activity and parses the full pairing link in one place, before permission checks and networking. Generated notification links still use the old scheme. Build and screenshot evidence for each PR is recorded in its own lane; this note does not rewrite the historical check date above, and does not mean the final audit of all open PRs or the frozen release is done. The shared check for manual entry (#770), the env var / script compatibility (#773) and the static guard (#786) have landed; the full boundary is in [Compatibility migration](../operations/rename.md).

| Entry | Current implementation |
| --- | --- |
| Pairing | QR scan, manual entry and pairing deep links; a temporary token is exchanged for a device token; TLS public-key pinning, candidate probing and re-pairing; when done, goes to the setup checklist |
| Persona | Main chat, New chat's blank page, history drawer and the shared chat page (no home page: Back from an embedded conversation lands on the main chat; an inbox item's pushed conversation returns to the item, #4339 C11); the drawer (left sidebar on wide screens) starts with a main-chat row whose name comes from `ManagerRoster.assistantDisplayName` — one source with the switcher, the top bar and the composer placeholder (#6401/#6969): with several assistants it is the current manager's name, while a single installed assistant (or no roster yet) keeps the persona pack's product title — prefixed by the App icon (`JAppMark` in the design system), with pending count / working / unread status and menu, then a divider. The main chat is not listed anywhere else (shared client convention #2004, #5111); the first message of the main chat can go into the persistent outbox first, and its target is resolved once the connection is back; once the assistant identity is known it stays in the Persona tab. The leader conversations (`assistant.leaders`) come next under the **Pinned** heading (`assistant_directions`; 常驻 in Chinese), in the hub's order, each under its direction's name and saying what it has open (`GET /api/v1/assistant/directions/tree`, `directionsTreeOf`: the current directions in the hub's order without the secretary group, its `counts.needs_you` as 「等你 N」 (nothing when 0) and, while its own conversation is idle and anything under it runs (the tree's `running`: an Agent of its tasks at work, #6626; from an older hub without it, a working task counted), the working spinner as its mark (`ConversationRow` `working`, `JStatusDot` `spinning`), the only way the row says working (no 「在做 N」; its own needs-you / unread dot wins); the title until it answers, and on an older hub that answers 404 the names come from `GET /api/v1/assistant/leaders`, `assistantLeadersOf`, with no counts). A conversation's long-press / `⋯` menu makes it a leader or ends that on the hub (`makeDirection` / `endDirection`, `POST /api/v1/assistant/directions/{id}/make` and `.../end`, #4331), so every device agrees; both open a `JDialog` and run only on its second choice (#6214) — 取消常驻's text says the chat returns to 最近 and its open tasks go back to the main chat's supervision, nothing closed or deleted — and no row draws a pin glyph (the 常驻 heading is the only mark, as on iOS and web); the hub's `directions` message (no payload) makes the page read the tree again at once — order, names and `counts`; the tree is read on every refresh and after a reconnect too, and a direction the list has not met refreshes it. Each lead row with open to-dos also gets a caret before its `⋯`, and every row under it has its caret at its end too (`feature/assistant/DirectionRows.kt`, `JDisclosureButton`: a 48dp button that says expanded / collapsed and what it folds, ("To-dos of …" / "Agents of …")); folded (the default, kept on this phone per lead: `persona-assistant` › `direction_rows_open`) the row is exactly as before. Open, the lead's to-dos follow it (title + state word in its tone, `JDirectionStateLabel`: Needs you / To verify / Stuck / Working / Not started), a tap opens the to-do (`TodoDetailRoute`); a to-do's caret lists its Agents (a tap opens the Agent's session, `ChatRoute` kind Agent; an in-session sub-agent `<parent>@<id>` opens its parent), an Agent with children has its own caret. The secretary group (书记官) has no drawer row (the main chat row stands for it); it stays in the To-dos and Agents groupings. An older hub (404) has no tree, and the rows have no caret. Between 常驻 and All chats, **Recent** (最近, #4922, #5196; web `recentConversations`, iOS): at most 5 of every chat that is not the main one, a leader, archived, the built-in background supervisor or a scheduled task's own chat (`origin == "task"`, #6228: those keep their Scheduled Tasks entry and All chats), newest first, equal times by session id (`recentConversations` in `AssistantData.kt`); no heading when none qualify, none while searching. The heading folds the rows with a caret and the choice is kept on the device across cold starts (`SidebarFoldStore` over SharedPreferences, like the web's localStorage and iOS's @StoredValue; default open, #6228, #6969); the open conversation's rows still show while folded, so folding never hides where a message lands (iOS `holdsCurrent`). A chat Persona opened carries its source's icon after the title (Inbox, Scheduled tasks, Lark /agent with the official Lark mark, Spawned from a chat, Extensions or Other for a new origin), named for TalkBack; the automatic origin takes precedence over the latest message's channel mark, and the same row rule applies in Recent, 常驻, search results and All chats (`AssistantConversationOrigin`). Every other conversation is under one **All chats (N)** row (still everything, Recent's too), which opens the list in place (this app has no all-conversations page and no source filter there); a search lists what it finds at once and still finds every conversation. Local pinning (the old Pinned group with Unpin all), the Recent / Other chats groups and the **Automatic** group are gone (#4331, #3264) |
| Agents | The top of the page is a search box, below it a row of computer chips (all machines + each computer, with online state and counts, scrollable sideways), then the quick filters. When no grouping has been chosen, the default is **By repository** (#2252; iOS #2161 also defaults to it; the web #1671 offers this grouping and defaults to by to-do when none was chosen, #2812): computer → repository → worktree → session. When the node sends `worktree` (#1805, read off git for every session, with `repo_root` and `branch`; `gitPlaceOf`), it groups by `repo_root` (else the worktree itself) and then by `worktree`, labelled with its `branch`; the main checkout is the worktree equal to `repo_root`, and a named branch keeps its heading even when the project has only its main checkout. From an older node, when the session carries `repo_root` (the host knows its repository, #2840: Paseo keeps its worktrees under its own folder, so the path names no repository), it groups by that: a cwd inside it is the main worktree, any other cwd is one of its worktrees. Otherwise the repository is inferred from the session's cwd (`repoOf` / `agentRepos` in `core:domain`, same rules as the web's `web/src/lib/agent-repos.ts`: directories under `/.worktrees/`, `/.claude/worktrees/`, `/.herdr/worktrees/` belong to the repository before the marker, `~/.worktrees/<repo>/<worktree>` belongs to `<repo>`, any other directory is the main worktree of its own repository; sessions without a cwd go under **Other**). When the host place names a linked worktree's sessions carry (`slot_name` without a `slot_id`: a Paseo workspace's title) agree on one, the worktree is headed and sorted by that name instead of its branch or folder, its group id unchanged; the main checkout keeps its name, and so does a worktree with an Orca session in it, which Orca names (as the web's `orcaTrees`). Search matches a session's branch too, which such a heading no longer shows (#4497; screenshots `AgentRepoTitleShotsTest`). Repositories sort by name (natural order, repo2 before repo10, same as iOS); worktrees put the main worktree first, the rest by name. Unlike the web, sessions on offline computers stay in the tree and the computer header says **Offline**, same as by computer and space. A repository with only a main worktree lists its sessions directly, without a worktree level. Repositories with no activity sort after active ones, are collapsed by default and show their session count; expanded, they list all their sessions directly. Worktrees with no activity are also collapsed by default but keep their position. When all of a repository's sessions are in the same herdr space, the repository header carries that space's pin / mute marks and its `⋯` menu. The computer header says "N repositories, M sessions". The list settings also offer **By computer and space** (a three-level tree: computer → space (herdr workspace) → session), by state, by to-do and, from a hub with the direction tree (#4352), **By pinned** (`AgentGrouping.Direction`, never the default: direction › to-do › agent straight from the tree, the secretary group (**Secretary**) last, an agent once where the tree first has it, a spawned agent the tree puts under another as that agent's child; every agent the tree doesn't have goes under the existing **No to-do** group, so none goes missing; `DirectionTreeTest`); the choice is kept (any older stored value maps to **By computer and space**). The tree uses indentation, font size / weight and hairlines to show levels, with no background panel per level (`docs/android/android-design.md` section 2.4); every level can collapse and its header shows counts. By computer and space, idle spaces whose sessions are all seen stay under their computer, after the active spaces, collapsed by default and slightly dimmed, showing the session count when collapsed. Pin / mute, request cards and answering. Renaming a session (the tab name goes through herdr, the session name through Claude's /rename; the entry is in the session row menu, the space page and the chat page menu) and renaming a space (the space header menu) update the list, space page and chat page title immediately after sending, and roll back on failure. Pin, mute, rename and close are wired to the hub by the App's `AgentsWiringModule` Folds are kept on the phone for each grouping (`AgentsPreferences.folds`), through range and grouping changes and relaunches; a search opens them while it runs and gives them back when it clears, as iOS (#4338 A6). |
| Agents | The top of the page is a search box, below it a row of computer chips (all machines + each computer, with online state and counts, scrollable sideways), then the quick filters. | Chat | Continuity gap filling, history paging, live deltas, collapsed work process, Markdown, diffs, to-dos, image cache, reading anchor, pending bubbles and manual review; for the composer see "Composer"; for session controls, origin and capabilities see "Session controls, session origin and capabilities" |
| Inbox | One list for Lark and mail items, persisted filters, AI actions, reply log; Lark has a window cache, event refresh, visible-read marking and basic message rendering; the mail body page and mail read/write are wired to the hub by the App's `InboxModule` (`HttpMailTransport`) |
| Settings | Pairing, setup checklist, background guide, About and updates; in connection diagnostics you can add / edit / delete hub addresses by hand, disable candidates, drag to reorder (list order is the order they are tried) and test each one (see "Connection addresses"); device management and notification preferences are wired to the hub by the App's `DeviceSettingsModule` |
| Extension modules | Terminal, files, machine monitoring, knowledge base, phone tools, voice chat (see "Voice chat"), the cross-app floating capsule (see "Cross-app floating capsule") and demo mode are wired into the App; entries are in the "Shell" section. In demo mode, pages without sample data show an explanation and do not connect to a real hub |
| UI | Three interface styles: Glass (default), Brief and Board, switched in Settings › Interface style; the change applies immediately and is stored on the device. The same page has the theme color (Match interface style (default) / Match avatar / five built-in palettes) and light / dark (Match system (default) / Light / Dark); see "Appearance: theme color and light / dark". Design rules, components and the rules for writing pages are in [Android interface styles and design system](android-design.md) |

## Unified home navigation

The home has only **Assistant** and **Agents**, selected at the top, on phones, tablets and foldables (#2589).
Selecting **Agents from Assistant** opens its list, not an earlier session or secondary page (#5638). Its root entry stays,
so list state is retained; selecting Assistant keeps the current conversation. Task/session entries, `/s/…` links and
notifications still open their requested detail directly. Back from an Agent opened in Assistant returns to its origin.
A conversation opened from an inbox item carries `RouteSource.InboxItem(itemId)`. An assistant conversation is a complete
chat page pushed above the item's event or original page on the Persona stack, rather than a conversation embedded in the
Persona home; Back returns to that item page. An Agent still opens on the Agents stack, and Back selects Persona without
changing that stack, so it also returns to the item. A child conversation or request opened above that chat inherits the
same source, so Back unwinds through the handling conversation to the item; an Agent child still opens under Agents.
Session-metadata repair and hub-group migration retain the sourced page.
Re-tapping an already selected Glass home segment retains its existing menu; that is not a selection from Assistant.
`AgentsTabNavigationTest` checks prior task, app/hub link, notification and restored-detail stacks;
`AgentsTabNavigationShotsTest` clicks the production home control and renders the Agents list and chat through Navigation 3
in three styles, light/dark and default/large text (Native JVM Compose captures, not device/emulator screenshots).
The avatar opens one shared left-side history drawer from either page; it pushes the full-size page aside rather than
rising from the bottom. Settings opens from its bottom gear in a sheet, never a home tab. Root Close returns to the
originating page; child Back pops only the settings sheet's secondary stack.
An open foldable or a tablet lays this out as an open iPhone Duo or an iPad (#2951): in a regular window, at least 600dp
wide and 480dp tall, the sidebar is a column beside the Assistant page that the avatar hides and shows, and the Settings sheet
shows its list beside the page it opens. Phones, folded or not and either way up, keep the drawer and one
column. Details and the threshold's reasoning: `docs/android/android-design.md` section 3.1. Inbox, To-dos, Computers and extension `nav_page` destinations open under
Assistant, following the iPhone sidebar mapping. Scheduled tasks and the knowledge base keep their existing Assistant
entry points. Extension management remains in Settings and is distinct from extension-provided pages.

The Assistant tab's menu (the selected tab on Glass, the title elsewhere) has iOS's items in iOS's order (#4339 C18,
`assistantMenu`): **Connection details** while the hub is out of reach, **Knowledge**, **Details** (the assistant's settings
page, iOS's profile), Android's **Voice chat**, **Back to main chat** (off the main chat only); **Copy directory**, **Copy
info** (iOS `AssistantCopyText`), **Copy session ID** and **Copy diagnostics**; the main chat's **Compact context**,
**Clear context** and **New main chat**; **Session info** and, while it works, **Interrupt**. **Launcher** is reached through Details; iOS's
per-chat launcher pick is the stored pick's (#5055), and its **Screen** needs a live screen Android doesn't have. Tests
`AssistantMenuTest`; screenshots `AssistantDetailsShotsTest`. Other styles retain their existing More section.
**Clear context** (#5100, the web's #5095, hub feature `assistant_main_clear`; none in the demo) posts
`POST /api/v1/assistant/main/clear` with the main chat on screen as `expect`, without asking. The page then stays a
conversation page: an empty middle and the composer at the bottom, not the centered start page; its first message starts
the new main chat. A 409 `main_changed` refreshes and opens the main chat the hub has, with the hub's words, as New main
chat does; any other failure is a toast with the reason ("Couldn't clear the context: …"). In the main chat the composer's
"/" list and "+" sheet offer `/clear` too, which clears the same way (`LocalMainChatClear`); `/clear` sent alone does it,
anything more is a message, a failure puts `/clear` back in the box, and on the cleared page it does nothing. Tests
`ClearContextTest`, `MainChatClearTest`.
It has no native Health destination or archived-chat browser; this change does not invent either feature.
Recent, pinned and automatic conversation history, pinning, archive actions and New conversation are retained.

There is no old-layout selector or migration. Android previously had a fixed four-destination shell, not an equivalent
of the retired Apple layout preference. The old Inbox/Settings tab enum values, their separate stacks, bottom dock,
wide navigation rail, appearance-preview tabs and tab-only strings/tests are removed. Old saved shell positions containing
those enum values are ignored as a whole, without rewriting or migrating them. Interface style, color, theme and Inbox
chat-rendering preferences are independent of home navigation and remain available.

Apple PR #1696 removed the old root-navigation switch; PR #2539 separately removed conversation-presentation settings.
The latter is not a home-layout toggle and is not a reason to remove Android's Inbox rendering preference.

### Simplified Assistant conversation and action trays (#2878)

Since #3387 the actions are two, **Needs you** and **In progress**, and each thing is in one only: the split is
`assistantEntries` in `persona.core.domain.tasks`, checked against `spec/fixtures/entries` with the web app
([unified-entry.md](../assistant/unified-entry.md#needs-you-and-in-progress-each-thing-once-3387)). Needs you takes Pending's
rows, with a task's cards folded into one row for the task (its oldest card first) and the main chat's hand-opened Agents
that ask; In progress takes the open tasks that do not wait on the user and the hand-opened working Agents (the old Agents
entry), keeps the quiet supervision group and gets the board's **All N**. Finished and shelved tasks are in neither. A
task row says **In progress** on the right while one of its Agents works or starts; once they all stopped (unread or
seen) and it waits on nobody, it says **Stopped** (已停下) and draws only a counted bar, never the indeterminate one, as the
web row does (#3576). The list below describes the earlier three entries.

The existing **Assistant / Agents** home tabs do not change. Above the Assistant composer, the old All/task/Agent chip
strip becomes three single-line actions, ordered **Pending**, **Progress**, **Agents**. Tapping opens a bounded, scrollable
tray upward and selects its entry; opening another closes the first. The bar is absent on the Agents page.

The entries are the same short filter chips as the old All / task chips (#2956): `AssistantTrayEntry` draws a `JChip`, so
height (36 dp), the style's chip radius, 12 dp side padding, `labelSmall` text, the `micro` count and the selected fill
all come from `JChipTokens` and `JChip` rather than a second copy. They sit content-width at the leading edge and scroll
sideways when a large font no longer fits them. The open tray is the chip's selected state; there is no arrow.

- **Pending:** orange dot/count; one row per decision, with Approve or Review and an Approve all header. Bulk approval
  takes only explicitly approvable permissions/plans, respects the session's approval capability and never chooses an
  answer to a question. Task plans awaiting Start and task acceptance decisions use their existing review/detail flows.
  Draft replies have a separate **Sent on your behalf · Only after you tap** group, with individual Send actions. An empty
  Pending action becomes gray; data-read failures must not be represented as an empty successful read.
- **Progress:** blue dot and neutral count. Each task has **one row only**, from its planned slot through execution and
  acceptance. Title, PR reference, reported steps, blocked orange state and completed check update in that row; tapping
  opens task details. Scheduled tasks and supervision have a separate quiet group and an inspectable background-record
  entry rather than new conversation turns. A missing numeric progress total is indeterminate, not a guessed percentage.
- **Agents:** green dot and neutral count; one session per row, working green / needs-you orange / completed-unread blue,
  with the computer display name and latest state line. A row opens that session. The old All entry still opens the task
  board, and task/Agent details plus routing controls remain accessible. The old chips were navigation and task routing,
  not an Agent-based message filter; this change does not invent a new conversation filter.

Task plan/task/receipt/review/report cards, including historical ones, are no longer rendered in the conversation. A
small dispatched line names each dispatched task (**Dispatched: <title> · Open**, or every title when there are several) and
opens that task's detail sheet, never the whole tray (#3700); the task row in Progress is the only card. Tools and
intermediate narration remain under a default-collapsed **Checked N places** summary, which can be opened manually.
Housekeeping notices (compaction, restarts, schedule setup) are hidden while dates remain. Explicit message-source
metadata identifies supervision/patrol/follow-up turns; normal user messages are never hidden by their title or emoji.
Original events and background records remain inspectable rather than being deleted from history.

On Glass the conversation draws the hub's task records as iOS does ([chats](../plans/android-ios-alignment/chats.md) C13):
`AssistantTaskCards.isBackgroundEvent(inline = true)` keeps only plans and task cards (the dispatched line stands for them)
behind, except a plan nobody started (`AssistantTaskCards.drawnPlans`, below), and `FeedLine` draws the receipt (its PR button and **View**), the review line, the report as
"task → @Persona" and Persona's hand-off as "Persona → @task" (both in Persona's box, the first three lines and **Expand**,
`ExchangeHeader` / `ExchangeText` in `feature/chat/AgentExchange.kt`), the user's own word to a task as "Me → @task" in the
user's bubble, the hub's `notice` line, centered, and a message card as iOS's `MessageCard` (the reply reads as text until
**Edit** opens it; Send and Edit, Later and Don't handle; Later puts it away to Needs You). A quiet supervision round stays in the conversation as one
**Supervision record** line (`· running` while it runs, iOS `SupervisionRecordLine`) that opens to the round
(`AssistantConversation.project(…, inline = true)` folds it with `BackgroundFolding(rounds = false)` outside the main
conversation's background folding), scheduled and follow-up rounds read as they are unless background rounds fold, and
Persona's work line is the Agent chat's (`WorkGroup` with `workSummary`, ticking while it runs) and opens to its thoughts
too. Brief and Board keep the records behind the trays, the quiet rounds in the background record and **Checked N places**,
as above. Tests: `TaskRecordsTest`, `SupervisionRecordTest` (core/domain), `TaskRecordsShotsTest`, `SupervisionRecordShotsTest`.

A plan nobody started is a line of the Glass conversation too, at the end of the exchange it happened in: an open plan is iOS's
`PlanCard` (`GlassPlanCard` in `feature/chat/PlanCardGlass.kt`: the plan's mark and **Start like this**, or **Split into N tasks,
started like this**; each task with its number when there are several, where it would run as chips that wrap, and why; **Start
these directly**; a task's model chip is its short name ("Opus 4.8", `planModelLabel`), the Edit field keeps the raw id; then **Start** / **Start N tasks**, **Edit** and **Don't start**, in one row while Start's whole label fits and
stacked (Start above the other two) when it would be cut), a dismissed one reads **Not started · N
tasks**. A started plan is the dispatched line in the card's place, and the card follows the store's latest plan, so a sheet that
kept the plan it was opened with (the tray's Review) says **✓ Started N tasks** instead of offering Start again. Tests:
`TaskRecordsTest` (core/domain), `PlanCardConversationTest`, `PlanCardFailureReasonTest`.

#### Background rounds fold into one line (#4314, leaders in #6595)

The main conversation and the leader (direction) conversations fold the rounds they did not start, as web and iOS do
(rules in [assistant.md](../assistant/assistant.md#background-rounds)):

- **Which rounds**: in the main conversation (the tasks store's `mainConversation`, else the config's `main`) or one of
  the hub's leader ids (`ChatChromeBindings.leaderConversations`, from `AssistantSnapshot.leaders`; gate
  `BackgroundFolding.foldsConversation`), and only while the hub's `main_background_folded` is on
  (`AssistantConfig.backgroundFolded`: a hub that omits it counts as on). `AssistantConversation.project(…, BackgroundFolding)`
  (`core/domain/.../tasks/BackgroundRounds.kt`) starts a round
  at a user input whose message source (`sourceOf`) is `conversation`, `followup` or `patrol` and ends it at the next
  genuine input, a queued one included; a system hint inside it does not end it. Such a round stays in the conversation
  rather than going to the background record; supervision and the other quiet rounds keep going there as before.
  A `followup` counts only as a scheduled follow-up: not a usage-limit continuation or a wake-up (injected
  `limit_continue` / `wake`), and not one without a title; those stay shown. Until the hub's config is read, the page
  uses the value it last read this run (in memory, `BackgroundFoldSetting.lastKnown`), else on.
- **Folded**: the round's lines become one `ChatLine.Extra` carrying a `BackgroundRound`, drawn by `BackgroundRoundLine`
  with the work summary's own line (`AssistantFoldLine`): `@<source> · Background round`, `· Running in the background`
  while it runs. A tap opens it and its lines follow in place (the id `bg-<first input>` sits in the chat's open-line set,
  so it survives scrolling and history paging); a search hit or a `?at=` link inside it opens it. A folded line keeps its
  place among the feed's lines and days (`AssistantLines`).
- **Not folded**: `supervisionVisible` (web's rule): a successful `ask_user` / `notify_user` (bare or Persona's
  `mcp__persona__…`), a pending `ask_user` while the round runs, a `task_review` with verdict `accepted` / `needs_user`,
  a `supervision_finish` whose result says `visibility: "user"`, a hub fold with `supervision_user`, or an injected
  supervision message marked `level="user"` (`Injected.level`, the same two marks the web's and iOS's `supervisionVisible` read, #6969).
- **Prompts**: while folding is on, a scheduled task's or follow-up's input shows only the hub's `injected.line` under its
  source label (`SourceMessage(hidePrompt)`), opened or not, and its line opens no prompt sheet; a supervisor message
  shows its text when opened.
- **Needs you**: same entry and count; with folding on and task rows from more than one supervising conversation (a
  to-do's `origin`), `needsYouGroups` groups them under that conversation's title (`JSectionHeader`, "Another
  conversation" without one), this conversation's group first with its cards, plans and drafts. One group: as before.
- **Switch**: Settings › Assistant › **Fold background rounds**, shown when the hub sends the field
  (`AssistantSettingsState.setMainBackgroundFolded`, as `main_tasks_quiet`). Off: the conversation and Needs you are
  exactly as before.
- **Interject note** (#6595): the hub's mid-turn note `<persona-context kind="context" title="插话">`
  (`prompts/assistant/interject.md`) is model instructions, not a picked quote: `MessageContext.isInterjectContext`
  marks it and `messageModel` drops it from the rendered contexts (the text is already stripped from the bubble body),
  while it stays in the model's input.
- **Tests**: `BackgroundRoundsTest` (core/domain), `AssistantSettingsStateTest.backgroundFoldSwitchSavesAndComesBackOnFailure`,
  `HttpSettingsParityWireTest.mainBackgroundFoldedIsSentOnlyWhenSetAndOnWhenMissing`.

#### The background supervisor (#5084)

Where the built-in background supervisor shows (what it is:
[unified-entry.md](../assistant/unified-entry.md#background-supervisor-4927)). Everything here follows fields of
`GET /api/v1/assistant` and is absent against a hub that does not send them.

- **Its conversation**: `AssistantSnapshot.backgroundSupervisor` (`background_supervisor`, `backgroundSupervisorOf`).
  This app's drawer has no group of its own for scheduled tasks' conversations: they wait under **All chats** with
  every other conversation Persona opened itself, and so does this one. It is never listed under 常驻 or Recent
  (`conversationGroups`, `recentConversations`), is named by the app (`assistant_background_supervisor`, in the app's
  language whatever language the hub titled it in; a search finds it by that name), and its row's menu only copies
  its session ID: no Pin, Archive or End chat (`AssistantDrawer`). The hub refuses the first two as well.
  The hub makes the conversation on first use, long after the list was read, and only `GET /api/v1/assistant` says
  which one it is: when a conversation that may be it arrives over the session stream (an assistant conversation with
  `origin: "task"` and no `origin_ref`, new or just retitled, `mayBeNewBackgroundSupervisor`), the repository reads
  the assistant again. Until that answer is in, the row is an ordinary chat's.
- **Switch**: Settings › Assistant › Task follow-up › **Main chat supervises its own tasks**
  (`main_supervises_own_tasks`, `AssistantSettingsState.setMainSupervisesOwnTasks`, as the fold switch: it moves at
  once and comes back on a failure), with one sentence under it. Shown when the hub sends the field.
- **Model**: the same section has **Background supervisor model**, which opens
  `ModelPurposeRoute("assistant.supervise")` (`BackgroundSupervisor.PURPOSE`); the row is there whichever way the
  switch is, as the use is in Model configuration, and the value is on the page it opens. A hub before #5806 leaves
  that use out of its catalog, so Settings › Model configuration asks for it by its ID after the catalog
  (`ModelCatalogModel`, `modelPurposesWith`) and lists it after the last use of its group; a hub without it, or one
  that fails that one request, lists the catalog as it is, and a hub that lists the use (#5806 on) is not asked.
- **Its reviews in the conversation**: a review it writes takes no turn of the conversation that handed the task
  out, so the hub marks the `review` feed entry `by: "background"` and the app draws exactly those entries in the
  conversation as the existing one-line `ReviewLine` (task · verdict · note), instead of leaving them in the
  background record (`AssistantTaskCards.isBackgroundSupervisorReview`, read by `isBackgroundEvent`). The mark is on
  the entry, so a line stays where it is whatever the switch says later and whichever conversation is the background
  supervisor then. Every other review, and every review from a hub without the mark, stays in the record as before.
  The whole line opens the task while the to-do list has it; the name is the list's, else the entry's own `title`.
- **Tests**: `BackgroundReviewsTest` (core/domain), `AssistantTest.theBackgroundSupervisorIsListedUnderAllChatsOnly`,
  `AssistantSettingsStateTest.superviseOwnTasksSwitchSavesAndComesBackOnFailure`,
  `HttpSettingsParityWireTest.mainSupervisesOwnTasksIsSentOnlyWhenSetAndTheBackgroundSupervisorIsReadOnly`,
  `DirectionNamesTest.theBackgroundSupervisorIsReadWhenTheHubNamesOne` and
  `.aConversationThatMayBeTheBackgroundSupervisorIsAskedAboutOnceItAppearsOrIsRetitled`,
  `ModelConfigurationStateTest.theBackgroundSupervisorsUseIsListedWhenTheHubHasIt`; `AssistantSettingsShotsTest`,
  `AssistantDrawerShotsTest` and `ReviewShotsTest` draw the section, the drawer row and the line in the three styles,
  light and dark.

#### Shared data contract

The only hub API addition is `task_cards` on the existing `GET /api/v1/assistant/conversations/{id}/feed?since=` response:
`{id,todo_id?,plan_id?,index?,at,updated_at,state,receipt_id?,report_id?}`. It is an additive **upsert page**, not a removal
snapshot. Identity is `plan:<plan_id>:<index>` before and after task binding, or `todo:<todo_id>` without a plan; `at` stays
at creation, not receipt time. A missing/null projection keeps legacy/cached data, and an empty page does not delete rows.
The full state and compatibility semantics are in [the protocol](../protocol/protocol.md#tasks-and-the-chat-feed).

Everything else reuses current endpoints and pushes:

| Use | Existing data and write path |
| --- | --- |
| Delegated tasks and PRs | `GET /api/v1/todos`, `Todo.origin`, `plan_id`, `pr_url`, `agents`, `done_at`, `supervision.state`, and live `todos` updates |
| Real progress | `Todo.supervision.progress.{kind,step,steps,text,at}`; otherwise the linked `Session.plan.{done,total,current}` and `activity` / `last_text` |
| Planning | Feed `plans` / `GET /api/v1/task-plans/{id}`; Start / Dismiss use their existing plan routes |
| Decisions | Current interactions and the existing `/interactions/{id}/resolve`; nontrivial questions/forms keep the existing Review flow |
| Draft replies | Feed message drafts and Inbox `suggestion.{kind,version,text}`; suggestion confirmation uses `/inbox/{id}/suggestion` with the captured `version`, `action:confirm`, and `client_id`; ordinary feed sends retain the existing message-send path |
| Quiet schedules | `GET /api/v1/assistant/tasks`, `repeat`, `every_minutes`, `next_run`, `last_run`, `last_status`, `last_error`, `run_machine_id`, `run_session_id`; refreshed by `assistant_schedule` |
| Agent rows | Existing session and computer snapshots, `state`, pending interactions, activity and completion-unread state |

No new database tables, model calls, approval bypass or draft-send endpoint are introduced. Android now decodes the
already-served progress/plan, PR and cancellation fields; Apple can use the same hub contract in its independent implementation.

For real-app synthetic captures, start `PORT=<unused loopback port> python3 scripts/mock/assistant_trays.py` and pair a
task-owned emulator through the existing DEBUG mock address. The fixture has four tasks (working, blocked and accepted),
a permission, a question and a versioned Inbox reply draft, with only fictional names and messages. Its
`GET /mock/assistant-trays?clear=1` removes pending decisions/drafts and pushes the new state without changing pairing;
`?reset=1` restores the fixture. `python3 scripts/mock/test_assistant_trays.py` verifies the stable rows and clear behavior
without opening sockets. JVM Native-rendered component captures are separate evidence, not emulator or handset captures.

## Pairing pages (#2481)

- **Manual entry**: an address typed without `://` gets a scheme before it is checked: `http://` when the host is this device or the local network (`localhost`, `*.local`, loopback and private addresses), `https://` otherwise. So `hub.local:18660` is tried over http, and `persona:18660` or `hub.example.com:443` over https. The rule after that is unchanged: http to a public host needs a fingerprint. This differs from the address editor in connection diagnostics ("Connection addresses" below), which always assumes https: that editor adds addresses to an existing pairing, while manual pairing is mostly for a hub on the same network. An address that still can't be read, or public http without a fingerprint, is reported under the address field and the form stays; nothing is sent. The reason goes away when the address changes, including when a pasted, scanned or opened link replaces it.
- **Failure page**: names the step it stopped at (`Stopped at "Read pairing details"`) instead of repeating the error chain, which stays in Diagnostics. From the second failed try in a row it adds "Tried N times, still can't connect." The count starts over when you edit the details, accept a new link or code, or tap **Next** on the manual form. When the details came from the manual form (**Next**) and the hub says the code is wrong or has expired, the page leads with **Edit address or code** (the form keeps what was typed), then Diagnostics, then **Scan again**: that is most likely a typo. Certificate and https failures, and details from a scanned, pasted or opened link, still lead with **Scan again**.
- **Back** follows the same route from the toolbar and Android: Confirm or failure returns to the manual form with the entered details, or to the connection options for a pairing link. The manual form returns to the connection options, and those return to Welcome when the flow started there. Settings › Pairing and Add hub return to their caller instead. Connecting and the required engine step have no Back action. On the first checklist, **Set up later** and Android Back both enter the app and leave the checklist pending.
- **Settings › Pairing** and Add hub start directly in the connection flow, even while the first checklist is unfinished. Only the root pairing page with no link reopens that checklist. A pairing link goes to Confirm, and successful pairing from any entry goes to the engine step or checklist.
- **Keyboard**: on the manual form, the address field's Next key moves to the code field, and the code field's Go key does what **Next** does.
- **QR scanner** centers the title between equal margins. Close and Android Back both return to the connection options.
- Tests: `PairingFlowTest` in `feature:settings` (Robolectric).

- **Connection diagnostics** opens over its caller. The toolbar arrow and Android Back dismiss it, keeping the pairing input or checklist scroll position.

## Aligning the Agents list and the Machines page (#3038)

- **Sorting**: the list settings have "Sort: **Status first** / **Recently updated**" (iOS `AgentSort`, stored on the device). With recently updated, sessions in each group sort only by time, and active spaces, repositories and computers sort by their newest session, first. With status first the sections stay the same; only the order inside a section is by time. The logic is in `AgentList.kt` in `core:domain` (`AgentListSettings.sort`).
- **Subtasks** (`CodexTaskForest.kt` in `core:domain`): a subtask is a session another one started: a Codex subagent, or a runtime's delegated session, such as a Paseo agent another agent created (#4494), whichever agent runs it; terminal panes never are. Its parent, on the same computer, is the session its `parent_session_id` names, of any source (a Codex thread's subagents sit under the runtime session that runs the thread); with no `parent_session_id`, the one Codex thread its `parent_thread_id` names; failing those, the session its `root_session_id` names. It sits under its parent's row in **Subtasks (N)**, folded until opened, finished ones included. One that the list of running sessions draws (Running, and the running part of All) hangs there only while that list draws a row above it too: a working agent whose creator was let go is a row of its own, where it would otherwise be drawn nowhere. No count includes a subtask, wherever it is drawn: quick filters, computer chips and headers, group headers, and the Machines page's card and **Running sessions**, which leave it out (`machineSessions`); the hub's fleet summary (ongoing notification, widget, tile) counts a runtime's the same way. Outside the Agents list (#4769): the floating capsule's **Unread**, its count, the badge and the completion notices leave it out as the Unread count does (`capsuleNotifiableUnreadSessions`; it is read under its parent in Agents). The flat lists of what goes on, the main chat's dock (**Needs you** / **In progress** and **All**, `dockAgents`), the capsule's activity and a knowledge project's **Agents** (`projectAgents`), leave it out while they show its parent, or a session above that, as `codexTaskForest` hangs a running one only under a drawn row (`foldedSubtasks`): the dock shows a task's agents as the task, so the agents one created are in the task's row, and the row's background line counts the ones still running where the host reports them (a Paseo creator does). A working one whose creator the list does not show (finished, or let go) stays an entry of its own there. The launcher's recent-agent shortcuts take the Running list's rule (`RecentAgents`). A subtask that needs the user is a row of its own instead, in Needs you and counted, until it no longer does (`isNestedSubtask`; `docs/design/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` ("Sessions that need the user")); the node already sends a runtime session that waits for the user without its parent, and the app's rule covers the rest. Opened under a quick filter and then answered, it stays where it was until the filter or the list is left, like any opened row (`openedRowsOfTheirOwn`). A row under Subtasks offers no **Pin**, since Pinned lists rows of their own only; one already pinned offers **Unpin**. Checked by `CodexTaskForestTest`, `CodexTaskTreeTest`, `AgentsHomeAffordancesTest`, `MachinesTest`, `TaskDockTest`, `CapsuleSessionsTest`, `KnowledgePresentationTest`, `ShortcutTextTest`, `CodexTaskTreeShotsTest` (three styles × light / dark, `build/runtime-subtask-shots/`), and for the dock and the capsule `AssistantTraysShotsTest.subtasksAreInTheirCreatorsRowInEveryStyle` (`feature/chat/build/assistant-trays-shots/subtasks-*`) and `CapsuleSubtasksShotsTest` (`app/build/capsule-subtasks-shots/`).
- **Origin filters and the botmux toggle**: the quick filters gain **Lark groups / Terminal** (#2526: botmux sessions / herdr and Tern panes, decided by the `pane_id` prefix, `agentSource` in `SessionSource.kt`). As on iOS / web, filters only show items that have Agents, and a selected item that becomes empty stays. When there are too many items for one row (all six have Agents), the Brief labels and the Board count cells switch to horizontal scrolling (each Board cell is at least 92dp wide and still splits the width evenly when it fits; when scrolling, a sliver of the next cell shows at the edge) instead of squeezing into ellipses (screenshot `AgentsParityShotsTest.sixFilters`, 360dp wide). The **botmux** capsule under the filters (web #2786) hides / shows botmux sessions on this device. It appears when there are botmux sessions or they are hidden; the number is the botmux session count under the current scope, computer and search (when not hidden it reuses the **Lark groups** count; when hidden it counts separately, without building the list again). Hiding while on **Lark groups** goes back to **All**; choosing **Lark groups** while hidden shows them again.
- **Origin label on session rows** (#3132, same as iOS / web #2698, placement per #3043): botmux sessions are labeled **Lark group**, herdr and Tern panes **Terminal**; other hosts and Persona's own chats get no label. The label sits at the top right, under the time, right-aligned, at the time's font size; the title starts at the line start and is not squeezed. The compact list has only one line, so the label goes before the time. When a Lark group has an https `external_url`, tapping the label opens that thread (through the shell's link handling, inside the App). Colors come from the design system's origin colors (`PersonaPalette.sourceFeishu` / `sourceTerminal`, same as iOS `Theme.sourceFeishu` / `sourceTerminal`; Brief uses ink only). Screenshot `AgentsSourceShotsTest`.
- **Three kinds of empty state** (iOS `ChatsView`): no search results is "Nothing found for “xx”", without **New session**; no sessions at all is "**Start your first coding session** / **No sessions on this computer**" + **New session**; an empty quick filter is "No XX sessions" + **Show all**.
- **Why herdr didn't start**: under the "herdr isn't running on this computer" card, a red line "Didn't start: reason" stays (known node errors turned into plain words, same as iOS `HerdrFailure.says`) until the next start or until herdr is read as running; long-press copies the hub's original text and the HTTP status.
- **Subscription usage**: the top of the machine monitoring page (overview) lists every account from `GET /api/v1/usage/windows` (of every paired hub group, see "Multiple hubs"), fullest first, expired last: a usage bar per window (turns the warning color from 80%), the reset countdown, the sample time, whether usage is running fast / slow, and a mark when the gateway can't be read. When the hub has no data (or an old hub lacks this endpoint) the whole section is hidden; while the page is in the foreground it reads once a minute, and a failed read keeps the last answer, aged by its `stale_at` (#1905). The logic is in `UsageWindows.kt` in `feature:machines`, with the same rules as the web's `lib/usage-windows.ts`.
- **What this computer can do**: after the computer card and the engine, the machine page reads the node self-check (`GET /machines/{id}/doctor`) and shows each feature as **Ready** / **Partly working** / **Broken** / **Off**, with **Check again**; when files Persona generated are stale or missing there is **Fix** (after confirming, `POST …/doctor/fix`); "See all checks" expands every item. Node too old, hub too old, offline and insufficient device permission each get one sentence; when the node is too old and can be redeployed there is **Redeploy**. The logic is in `NodeHealth.kt`, with the same rules as the web's `lib/node-health.ts`.
- **Browser (ego lite)**: when the hub's ego plugin is on (the machine has `browser`), a Mac's machine page has the **Let the assistant use this browser** toggle, install / uninstall, the first-time setup steps (open ego lite, import data from other browsers, finish setup, check again) and, once ready, its status (imports, usage stats), same as iOS `BrowserSection`. While a task is running or first-time setup is in progress it reads every 2 seconds. The phone has no live view of that Mac (the iOS "VNC" button); first-time setup is done on the Mac.
- **Not done yet**: "Control computer" (Cua) and "Browser sign-in" (importing cookies) on the machine page, "New worktree…".

## Settings navigation and search (#5233)

Settings keeps three scopes: the current assistant, this device, and Hub. This is a navigation reorganization;
model choices, notification defaults, permissions, pairing, update installation and assistant ownership do not change.
Search filters entry names and localized common aliases (including complete former labels from the existing
localization resources), case-insensitively with
Unicode width normalization. It never indexes credentials, account values or addresses. Clearing it restores the
three groups; a query with no matches shows an empty state. Extension-contributed navigation stays in its original
assistant scope and is shown when the search is empty.

| Previous home entry | New home entry and location |
| --- | --- |
| Model providers; Model configuration | Models; uses first, then Advanced › Model providers and Add provider |
| Assistant profile | Assistant profile (unchanged, current assistant) |
| Claude Code; Codex; Session triage | Agent › Claude Code settings, Codex settings, Session triage |
| Connectors; Extensions; Channels | Same current-assistant entries; plain home subtitles |
| Notifications; Floating capsule; Background keep-alive; desktop presence/muting | Notifications and background; notification preferences, capsule entry, one complete phone-check list, desktop-muting details |
| About; Check for updates | About and updates; version, manual check, recovery and verified APK installation |
| Hub status card; hub address; Connection diagnostics; Pairing | Connection; status, selectable wrapping address, pairing and existing diagnostics |
| Appearance; Language; Translation; Storage; native Inbox chats | This device, unchanged behavior |
| Hubs; Hub version; Assistants; Avatar; Devices | Hub, unchanged behavior |

The legacy `notify` and `ready` Settings destinations open the same Notifications and background page. `capsule`,
`desktops`, `providers`, `models`, `claude`, `codex`, `triage`, `diag`, `pair`, `about` and connector routes retain their
names and remain directly accessible. In-place navigation records the parent, so Back returns to the group page;
the navigation shell keeps its existing stack. Switching assistants closes the new Agent group as it does the
existing assistant-owned pages.

The device page reuses `ReadinessScreen(backgroundOnly = true)` without assistant or computer setup, checklist
completion/dismissal actions, or implicit writes to first-run pending state. System notification checks have one home;
notification channels, manufacturer battery/autostart controls, lock-screen and cellular tests remain there.
First-run onboarding still uses the original checklist behavior. When an older hub cannot supply phone-muting
preferences, its online-computer detail entry remains visible in this page rather than disappearing with the switch.

Android currently has knowledge-base browsing, searching and tagging, but no knowledge-base **selection** settings
(priorities, context-source toggles or per-chat selection). No misleading selection entry is added; that capability
remains separate work tracked by #2217.

Verification: `SettingsSearchTest` pins alias matching and stable destinations; `SettingsGroupsShotsTest` checks scope
order, assistant switching, real-page phone home/search/empty states in every style and light/dark; model tests check
Advanced folding and owner-aware selection. Captures produced by these tests are **Native JVM Compose renders**,
not emulator/handset screenshots, and do not prove system dialogs, background survival or live hub transport.

## Notifications (no push service)

Android shares the hub WebSocket through the foreground service in `:platform:notify` and does not depend on FCM or vendor push. Notification channels, restore on boot, network reconnects, keep-alive scheduling and the setup checklist for Chinese-vendor ROMs are implemented.

Device record (2026-10-31, ColorOS 16 / Android 16, single observation): with the foreground service running, the WebSocket was still cut about 6 seconds after the App went to the background, and reconnected automatically when it came back to the foreground. In system settings the App needs "Allow background activity" and "Auto-launch" turned on and battery set to not optimized (the checklist's "Background keep-alive" item guides this). How long it stays connected in the background after that, and how much battery it uses, are to be measured per acceptance item V03; not measured yet.

- "Finished" is decided by the hub with the same completion policy as iOS and delivered as a `notify` event; the client receives it after declaring `ws_notify` and no longer infers completion itself.
- A `notify` the hub selected for the phone while its socket was down (a network change, Doze, a frozen App) comes after the next `client_hello`, oldest first, for up to 30 minutes and 50 events; a card resolved meanwhile is skipped (`../protocol/protocol.md` ("Notifications over the persistent connection (ws_notify)"), #4043). Each one is posted under its usual tag, so it replaces rather than repeats.
- Questions, plans, permissions and screen options can be answered from the notification; allowing or approving requires unlocking, and denying or sending back can include a reason. Negative buttons go on the left and positive on the right (**Deny / Allow**, **Send back / Approve**), consistent with the ongoing notification and Android dialogs; questions and screen options keep their original order. When the result is unclear it says it's waiting for confirmation and never re-approves automatically. A second tap on a request this phone already answered (the first answer took it off the hub before its notification went away) says nothing, not "Handled on the computer"; one gone without an answer from this phone still says so (`NotificationActionResult.AnsweredHere`, `NotificationActionJournal.claimed`, #2483). Permission cards with `fields` (such as a password when adding mail; not the assistant's password card, which has its own Run) are a form in their conversation (#4641, as iOS `PermissionInputForm`): one field per declared field, secret ones masked (`JSecretField`), then **Deny** and **Fill In & Allow**, no Always and no deny reason; Allow stays off until every required field has something besides spaces, and sends `{"allow":true,"values":{…}}` with the declared keys that were filled (`AnswerDraft.values`, `AnswerBuilder`, `PermissionFieldsActions`). The values live only in the form (`rememberTransient`: emptied on sending, leaving the screen, going to the background or the card expiring) and never in the request draft. One-tap approval, request notifications, the ongoing notification and the Agents tab's card offer no Allow when a field is required, only Deny; the Agents card says to open its chat to fill them in (`needsFieldValues`, #4987). Tests: `PermissionFieldsCardTest`, `PermissionFieldsCardUiTest`.
- Tapping a notification and session deep links go through the same navigation entry (the same on cold start and when the App is already open). When the notification link specifies `tab`, it lands on Persona or Agents directly, without waiting for session metadata; when it doesn't, it waits for the metadata to arrive before routing. The terminal fallback action (`target=terminal`) opens that session's terminal, and back returns to the original chat. Cards not attached to a session (empty `session_id`, e.g. `personad ask` in a plain terminal, #4515) still raise notifications; opening one lands on the Agents page: first any chat open on the Agents page is closed, then if the card is in the list it opens directly (the link carries `machine` and `request` but no `session`, used only locally on the phone, #4808). There is no terminal button.
- The setup checklist offers system entries for notification permission, battery restrictions, auto-launch and so on, plus scheduling, showing and reporting a lock-screen test. Lock-screen survival, notification display, recovery after reboot and battery use on each ROM are all unmeasured.

<a id="连接地址设置--连接诊断"></a>

## Connection addresses (Settings › Connection diagnostics)

Requested 2026-11-01: "Current connection" and "Candidate addresses" must support adding and editing. The hub doesn't always publish all its addresses (for example, when a NAS's network card sits on a virtual switch, the hub treats it as a virtual interface, so its IPv6 and intranet https addresses are not sent out); in that case you add them on the phone by hand. The semantics match "manually add address" for computer links ([link.md](../operations/link.md)); the difference is that on Android these settings belong to the pairing.

Candidate addresses have three sources, each marked in the list:

- At pairing: `url` and `alt` in the pairing link, the https address built from `url` + `tls`, and `/api/v1/endpoints` read during pairing;
- From the hub: the authenticated `/api/v1/endpoints`, the WS `hello` and `endpoints` events ([protocol](../protocol/protocol.md#候选地址)). As soon as the hub's list arrives it replaces the pairing-time batch;
- Added manually: stored only on this phone; the hub's list never replaces them.

What you can do:

- Add: `https://domain:port`, `https://[IPv6]:port`, `https://IPv4:port`. Without a scheme it assumes https; the port is required; paths, `?`, `#` and user names are rejected; IPv6 needs square brackets. Validation does no DNS lookup and no networking (`checkEndpoint` in `core:hub`); errors are shown in Chinese under the input field, and when valid it previews the normalized form that will be saved (`scheme://host:port`, port always written out). Each pairing allows at most 10 manual addresses; duplicates of an existing candidate (compared by normalized form, so `https://host:port/` and `https://HOST:port` are the same) are rejected.
- Security rules are unchanged: https addresses are pinned to the public-key fingerprint from pairing, as at pairing time; when the pairing has no fingerprint (the hub has no TLS, or sits behind a reverse proxy), https addresses are checked against system-trusted certificates and the domain ([network.md](../operations/network.md#反向代理的子路径android)). `http://` must be on the local network: IP literals must pass `LocalAddressPolicy` (loopback, private, link-local, ULA), and names are only accepted as `localhost` and `.local`; at runtime `HubHttpClient` still checks again against the resolved peer address ([network.md](../operations/network.md)).
- Manual addresses can be edited (position and disabled flag stay) and deleted (with confirmation); pairing-time and hub-sent addresses can't be changed, only disabled and re-enabled; manual ones can be disabled too. At least one enabled address must remain. "In use" marks the one currently connected.
- Each one can be **tested**: a fresh connection pool with pinning requests `GET /api/v1/health`, checks hub_id, and shows the result and time taken. The time includes connection setup, and for https the TLS handshake; it is a single sample, labeled "single" in the UI, and is not a performance baseline. Results stay in memory only. Each row also shows the probe result from the connection's own route selection (labeled "auto-probe"); whichever is newer is shown.

**List order is the order they are tried** (requested 2026-11-02: "users must be able to drag candidate addresses; the display order is the trying priority"). However the list is ordered, the connection tries it in that order, with no hidden reordering: the old "prefer this" and "on reconnect, first try the one that last worked on the same network" are both gone.

- Reordering: drag by the handle on the left of a row, or long-press a row and drag; dragging to the top or bottom edge scrolls automatically. The row menu has "Move up" and "Move down", and accessibility services get actions with the same names. The number on the handle is this entry's position in the trying order (disabled ones are not numbered).
- Before any reordering, the default rules apply (`orderEndpoints`): public IPv6 → domain → local network → public IPv4 → other; within a kind, https before http; within that, manual before pairing-time / hub-sent; finally the original order (manual ones by when they were added, the rest in the hub's order).
- After reordering, the user's order wins (`arrangeEndpoints`): new addresses from the hub and newly added manual ones go to the end; addresses the hub no longer offers disappear from the list (an address whose IPv6 prefix changed counts as new and goes to the end). "Restore default order" (with confirmation) goes back to the default rules.
- Disabled addresses stay in place, grayed out and labeled "Disabled", and are skipped when connecting; they can still be dragged, and when re-enabled they're still where they were.

How connecting works: only enabled addresses are tried, starting strictly in list order. Each one starts after the previous one fails, or 1 second after the previous one started (`HubRouteSelector.STEP_MILLIS`), and whichever passes the identity check first wins; so a later entry can only win once the one before it has been tried alone for 1 second. Once connected, every 5 minutes (`OkHttpHubConnection.RECHECK_MILLIS`) it checks the addresses ranked above the current one, waiting at most 1 second each; if any answers, it reconnects (that round races everything and picks the first to answer). The connection never moves itself down the list. Dragging or restoring the default order only changes the order; it doesn't probe or switch right away: the connection follows the new order on the next reconnect or the next 5-minute recheck, and dragging doesn't drop the connection in use (decided by the user 2026-11-02); only disabling, deleting or editing the address in use reconnects directly. Both 1 second and 5 minutes are configured values, not measured. When no address passes, the failure shown is the most telling one: a pinned public address with another key is **The hub's certificate changed**, and that is not retried. A private address (LAN IPv4, ULA, link-local, `.local`) that answers with another certificate or another hub's id is only a route that does not lead to the hub on this network: away from home the same address can be a different device (an office network reusing the same private range). It counts as unreachable and is retried like any network error (`HubRouteSelector.worst`, code `private_address_mismatch`), unless private addresses were all there was to try.

Storage: manual addresses, disabled flags and the user's order are saved per pairing (`ScopedHubData.endpoints`, `endpoints.enc` in the pairing directory, encrypted with Tink AEAD like the credentials) and survive restarts; a new list from the hub doesn't change them; signing out, revoking and re-pairing delete them together with the pairing directory (`eraseScope`). If the file can't be read it is treated as "default order, no manual addresses" and does not block the whole pairing (if the credentials file can't be read, the original file is kept and an error is reported). A "prefer this" saved by the previous version is read back as an order where that entry is first and the rest follow the default rules.

Code: validation, normalized form, kinds and ordering are in `core/hub/.../HubEndpoints.kt`; racing is in `HubRouteSelector`, the recheck in `OkHttpHubConnection`; the settings page controller is `HubEndpointSettings` (implements `ConnectionEndpoints` from `core:contracts`) and is handed to Settings (`PairingModule`) by `ConnectionRuntime.endpoints`; storage is `core/data/.../EndpointOverridesStore.kt`; the page is `feature/settings/.../EndpointsSection.kt`, `EndpointEditorState.kt` and dragging `EndpointDrag.kt` (candidate rows use `JRow`'s slot style, so margins line up across all three styles). Demo mode has no such service, and connection diagnostics stays read-only there.

Screenshots: `MOCK_ENDPOINTS=1 python3 scripts/mock/mock_hub.py --tls-port 0` makes the mock hub also send two unreachable documentation addresses (`[2001:db8::10]` and `hub.example.com`), for looking at failed tests and disabling. Pairing with a fingerprint needs `persona://pair?url=…&token=mock&hub=<hub_id>&fp=<fp>&tls=<TLS port>`; read `hub_id` from the mock hub's `/api/v1/health`, and take `fp` and the port from the "TLS 端口=… fp=…" line ("TLS port=… fp=…") it prints at startup (`debug.mockAddress` only configures a plaintext address; without a fingerprint you can't add an https address).

## Composer (attachments, camera, dictation, "/" commands, @ mentions, quote cards, queue strip, refused messages)

Ported 2026-11-02 from the old App on the main line (inventory items C1–C4, D1, D7). The composer on the chat page and in Persona is the same `ChatComposer` from `feature/chat`, and the behavior below is the same in both (the queue strip is only in Agents chats).

- Photos and files: **+ › Photos** takes up to 4; each is uploaded to that computer as soon as it's picked (`POST /api/v1/machines/{mid}/files`), long edge scaled to 1600, JPEG quality 75 (same as iOS). Photos sit in a row of 64dp square thumbnails above the composer (design system `AttachmentThumb`, decoded in the background at thumbnail size), with an ✕ at the top right (**Remove this one**); uploaded ones have a pen at the bottom left, and a tap opens the markup editor (see "Viewing and marking up images"). While uploading they are dimmed with a spinner; on failure they get a red border and **Retry**, a tap re-uploads, and the reason is written in one line under the thumbnail; photos that can't be read, or that exist only on the computer after being restored by **Take back to edit**, show a placeholder icon. Files are still one per row (name, state, retry, ✕). When picking a file it pre-checks `OpenableColumns.SIZE`; anything over 200 MB is marked invalid right away ("“name” is over 200 MB and can't be sent") and is neither copied nor uploaded; when the size can't be read it still counts while copying. With only photos and no text or files, the body sent is "Please look at the photos", and the photos are still written as an `<attached_images>` tag. When coming back from the camera or file picker the connection is often still catching up, so uploads first wait up to 15 seconds for `readyToSend` (`UPLOAD_READY_WAIT_MILLIS`, configured value, not measured), then report "retry upload once the connection is back". Offline, photos are not queued with the message (decided: not doing it).
- Videos (#4339 C9, iOS `VideoAttachments`, [protocol.md](../protocol/protocol.md) "Sending videos"): **+ › Photos** picks pictures and videos, four together (`MAX_COMPOSER_MEDIA`, the camera and pasted pictures count too). A video is uploaded as it is through the files endpoint, shown as a row like a file's with the video mark and, once on the computer, "size · length" (`MediaMetadataRetriever`), and sent as a `<persona-file kind="video">` tag after the words (`ChatComposerState.message`); a video alone says "Please look at the video". Over the hub's 200 MB it is refused as any file is: iOS compresses such a video to 1080p / 720p / 540p H.264, Android does not. Edit and Take back to edit put it back as a video already on the computer; a queued one reads "[Video]". A stored draft keeps it (`DraftAttachmentType.Video`, its `duration`). Tests `ComposerMediaTest`, `ChatQueueStripTest`.
- Pasted pictures on Glass (#4339 C9, iOS `PasteImageChip`, `ComposerPaste.kt`): while the clipboard holds a picture the composer has not offered (read from the clip's description, which shows no clipboard notice; again when the window gets the focus back and on each change), the focused composer shows **Paste** and ✕ (**Don't paste**) at its top. Paste copies the picture into `cache/chat-captures/` (the clipboard's grant ends with the clip) and attaches it as a photo; either way the same copy is not offered again. The text field itself pastes only text: it is the `TextFieldValue` `BasicTextField`, which Compose's `contentReceiver` does not reach. Brief and Board have no chip. Tests `ComposerMediaTest`, shots `ChatMediaShotsTest`.
- Camera: the App declares CAMERA for QR pairing, and Android then requires the permission before it can launch another camera App. **+ › Take photo** first checks the permission and asks with `RequestPermission` if missing; on denial (including "Don't ask again") it shows **Camera access needed**, explaining that it can be turned on in system Settings › Apps › {App name} › Permissions, and **Open settings** opens this App's system details page. With no camera App it says **No app on this phone can take photos**. The original capture is kept in `cache/chat-captures` (FileProvider `${applicationId}.chat.files`) and deleted only when it leaves its draft (removed with ✕, or the composer is cleared after sending): the composer watches its own draft's changes by attachment id and deletes from that; it never scans the directory (drafts of other sessions may still reference files in the same directory). Code: `ComposerCamera.kt`.
- Dictation: the microphone (**Dictation**) is always there, same as iOS `ShellComposerBar` (#2976 G058): with content, the send key is to its right, and after typing you can keep dictating to append; when the Agent is working and there's no content, the right side is **Stop**; with no content and not working there's only the microphone. Tapping it opens the phone's own voice input (`RecognizerIntent.ACTION_RECOGNIZE_SPEECH`, `LANGUAGE_MODEL_FREE_FORM`, `zh-CN`, prompt "Go ahead; what you say goes into the message box"); any vendor's recognizer works, no Google services needed, and the App doesn't record or need the microphone permission. Without a recognizer the button is hidden (the `feature/chat` manifest declares `<queries>`; on Android 11+ it can't be found without it); a launch failure says **No voice input on this phone**. The recognized text is appended to the end of the draft with the cursor at the end, not inserted at the selection (`withDictation`: first trims trailing whitespace from the draft; if the draft ends with an ASCII letter or digit and the first recognized character is also ASCII, a space goes in between; Chinese is joined directly). Code: `Dictation.kt`.
- Send and Stop move as on iOS, on Glass (#4210 T3, iOS `ShellComposerBar`, `Theme.Motion.composerSwap`; `ComposerActionSlot` in `core:designsystem`, wired in `ChatComposer`, so Agents chats, Persona's conversations and New chat all have it): typing the first words grows **Send** out of the right edge, clearing a slight blur (Android 12 and later; none on 11), while it fades in; **Stop** shrinks away as send comes, and comes back the same way when the box empties and the Agent still works; **Send** leaving shrinks back into the right edge. The mic and the model pill beside the button glide as its slot widens. The button that is leaving takes no tap and TalkBack does not read it. **Send** shrinks while it is pressed (**Stop** and the Inbox's send do not). With the system's animations off, and in Brief and Board, the buttons swap at once and nothing shrinks. The slot keeps its swap going when the composer opens and moves its buttons to the second row. The values are `composerSwap` and `composerPress` in `spec/tokens/motion.json` (`DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` section 3.1). Tests: `ComposerActionSlotTest`, `SendButtonPressTest` (`core:designsystem`), `ChatComposerSwapTest`; frames in `ComposerSwapMotionShotsTest`.
- "/" quick commands: the command table `SlashCommand` (the same set as iOS: continue `/继续`, `/compact`, `/context`, commit and push `/提交`, report progress `/汇报`; the English UI shows e.g. `/continue`) plus **Clear context /clear**. In the "+" sheet a tap sends right away. When the composer holds only one "/word" (starting with `/`, no spaces or newlines), commands matching the prefix (case-insensitive) are listed right above the field (design system `ComposerSuggestions`: the word in bold, the description on the right, /clear in the danger color); tapping one puts its text in the composer (not sent, cursor at the end), and the list closes when the draft is exactly the only match; tapping /clear clears the composer and asks for confirmation. /clear is only for Claude in a terminal-engine pane (positive check `canTypeClaudeCommands`: Claude, can send, has a terminal, has a pane, not Persona), and only appears when it's not working and not already clearing (both in the list and in "+"). The confirmation: "{Agent} will start a new session, and this chat stays in history; the page jumps to the new session once it appears. Anything you haven't sent on the phone is kept." **Clear** is the danger button. After sending it waits up to 20 seconds (`CLEAR_WAIT_MILLIS`) and opens another running session if one appears in the same pane; on timeout it says "Sent /clear. Open the new session from the list once it appears." and offers /clear again (no message if this /clear hasn't gone out yet or was refused; the bubble says so itself); a failed request stops waiting immediately.
- /model and /loop (#2976 G052, web `AGENT_COMMANDS`): they only appear in the "/" list, not in the "+" sheet (where a tap sends). /model is for Claude in a terminal pane (`canTypeClaudeCommands` and able to change mode); tapping it clears the composer and opens the **Mode** sheet (which has a **Model** section, see "Model" below). This is a one-shot request (`openRequested`, cleared by `onOpenHandled` after opening), so when the composer is drawn again (back from a subagent's page), the sheet doesn't pop open by itself; when the draft is exactly `/model` the list doesn't close either, and only a tap opens it. /loop is for the same sessions; tapping it puts `/loop ` in the composer. When the draft is a /loop command, a line above the composer says what it will do (`loopHint` in `ComposerLoop.kt`, same copy as the web: "Repeats every 10 minutes: …", "Repeats at Claude's pace: …"; with no task given, it shows the format in the warning color).
- @ mentioning a file or session (#2976 G043, iOS `PromptItems`, web `ComposerPanel`): the @ button right of "+" types an `@` at the cursor (adding a space first if a character precedes it); **+ › Mention a file or session** does the same (#4339 C16, iOS's + panel, in an Agent's chat, Persona's conversation and New chat). When the text before the cursor is `@word` (at the start or after whitespace, so email addresses don't count), `ComposerSuggestions` lists right above the field: files that tools read or wrote in this chat (`file_path` / `notebook_path` of `tool_use`, newest first, at most 12), with the directory on the right (relative path for files in the session directory, "File this session used" for the root); tapping one replaces it with `@relative/path `. Then other sessions (excluding Persona chats and this session, by update time (parsed time, not string comparison: the hub's fractional seconds vary in length), at most 8), with "Agent · directory · as context" on the right; tapping one deletes the `@word` and puts that session as a context card above the composer (`persona://session/<machine>/<session>`, body "Another session: title (Agent) / directory / it last said: …"), sent with the next message as `<persona-context>`, as on web and iOS. Filtering is by file path, session title or "Agent · directory" (case-insensitive); with no match it says **No file or session matches**. Read-only sessions that can't send have no @. In an Agent's chat and in Persona's conversation (#4339 C16, iOS `ComposerMentionMenu`) the list goes on with the session folder's own entries, read from the session's computer (`GET …/fs`, the files pane's endpoint; each folder once while the list is open, after a 120 ms pause): folders first, then files, hidden ones only after a dot, at most 12, none a file the chat used already is, with "Folder" or "Session folder" on the right; and a word with a slash browses that folder relative to the session's (`@app/` lists `<directory>/app`, a folder keeps the list browsing it, a file types `@app/main.kt `; "Reading folder…" and "Nothing matches in this folder"). Tests `ComposerMentionsTest`, `ChatMentionFoldersTest`, `ChatMentionBrowsingTest`, shots `ChatMentionShotsTest`. **New chat's page** (#4339 C16, iOS `StarterComposer`) has @ too: no files or tasks, only the other sessions (every computer's), and a typed absolute or `~` path with a slash lists that folder on the assistant's computer (`GET …/fs`, read once per folder while the list is open, after a 120 ms pause): folders, then files, names starting with the word first, hidden ones only after a dot, at most 40; a folder types `@~/code/` and the list browses on, a file `@~/code/a.md `; "Reading folder…" and "Nothing matches in this folder" while browsing. A picked session waits over the composer as a quote card and goes in front of the first message as `<persona-context>` (the "Sending…" bubble shows the words alone); on Glass what needs the user over the composer steps aside while the list is open, fading and folding away in the state motion and back, the dock keeping the page's backdrop (`NeedsStepAside`; `starterMentionSource`, `StarterMentions`, `MentionFolders`; tests `StarterMentionsTest`, `NewChatMentionsTest`, shots `NewChatMentionsShotsTest`). In Persona's conversations the list starts with the conversation's open tasks (#4339 C15, iOS `TaskMentionList`: not done or failed, newest first, the state's dot and the computer · folder), under **Message a task**, the files and sessions following under **Mention a file or session**; picking a task takes the `@word` out and sends the next message straight to it, the same route a task's **Message** sets (`TasksStore.setRoute`). While a route is set, **Message “task”** stands under the tray bar right above the composer, in every style, and its × (**Hand to Persona**) gives the message back to Persona (`TaskRouteChip`). Code: `ComposerMentions.kt`, `TaskRoute.kt`; tests `TaskRouteTest`, shots `TaskRouteShotsTest` (`build/task-route-shots/`).
- Share as a picture on Glass (#4339 C9, iOS `MessageShareSheet` / `MessageShareCard`, `MessageShare.kt`): the long-press menu's **Share** opens **Message card**, a full-screen page with Light / Dark in its top bar (the app's appearance at first), the message drawn as iOS's card (who said it, "space › tab" and the time; the words, cut past 3,000 characters, with code blocks without their Copy bar; a quiet Persona mark; on a pale blue-to-lilac gradient) and **Share** (the picture through the share sheet), **Save**, **Copy** and **Share Text** along the bottom. The card is as wide as iOS's `ShareCardWidth` makes it (390 dp, wider for tables, code and very tall cards, at most 520 dp); the picture is the card's `GraphicsLayer` recording at the phone's density, shared through the chat's FileProvider (`MediaShare`). Brief and Board's Share still shares the text. A reply's cards are drawn as the chat draws them, never as their JSON (#7261, `ViewBlocks.picture` → `AssistantPictureBlock`): outside the conversation's card host so nothing answers, replies drawn as they look, an HTML card as its fallback; a card's JSON never widens the card and the 3,000-character cut never splits one (docs/assistant/assistant-views.md "Sharing a reply as a picture"). Tests `MessagePictureTest`, shots `ChatMediaShotsTest`.
