- A message only on this phone (#4645, as iOS and the web in #4626): an outbox bubble that is queued, waiting for the network, refused or turned down opens the long-press menu too, with **Copy**, **Select**, **Share** and, last and red, **Delete** (**Cancel send** while it waits, the words of its status actions). It takes the bubble and its outbox entry off (`MessageSender.cancel`) and sends nothing; the toast's **Undo** puts it back in the state it had, refusal included (`MessageSender.restore`, a waiting one then goes out once connected). Cancel send and Delete in the status line get the same Undo. A message the hub may have (sending, sent, unknown, verifying, needs review) has no Delete; unknown keeps Discard. Tests: `MessageMenuTest`, `ChatDeliveryStateTest`, `OutboxMessageSenderTest.undoPutsADeletedMessageBackAsItWas`.
- An unsent message on Glass (#4339 C9, iOS `PendingBubble`, `OutboxBubble` in `ChatOutboxBubble.kt`): no status line under it but a mark before the bubble, level with its last line: a quiet clock while it sends; a clock while it waits (queued, or for the network) whose small menu, and TalkBack's actions, are **Retry now** (the queue looks again at once, `DeliveryAction.RetryNow` → `MessageSender.retry`) and **Don't send**, with "Sends when connected" under the bubble; a red mark once it did not go (refused, turned down) with **Resend** and **Delete**, its reason ("Not sent: …") and the refusal's own actions (Clear and send, Open terminal, Edit) under it as before. Waiting or failed, the bubble is at 0.6 (`UserBubbleFrame`'s `faded`, `leading`). The long press ends in **Don't send** while it waits, the mark's word. Where one that did not go sits is the next item's rule, in every style. A delivery to check (unknown, verifying, needs review) keeps the status line with Check message and Discard, and a delivered one waiting for the chat history has nothing under it. Brief and Board keep the status line. Tests `ChatOutboxBubbleTest`, `ChatDeliveryStateTest.retryNowIsForAMessageStillOnItsWay`, `OutboxMessageSenderTest.retryNowOnAWaitingMessageTriesAtOnce`; shots `ChatDetailsMoreShotsTest` (`unsent`), `ChatInfoShotsTest` (`unsent-placed`).
- Quoting (#2976 G050): **Quote** in the long-press menu no longer stuffs the message into the draft with "> "; instead it adds a quote card above the composer (`ChatLinkContext` with `kind="quote"`; the title is who said it: your own messages are always written "我" ("me"; `ChatLinkContext.QUOTE_ME`, a wire value independent of UI language, iOS also only recognizes "我"; the card shows it per language as "Quoting your message"), an Agent's is its name, with message id `msg`). The draft is untouched and the cursor returns to the composer. The card says "Quoting Claude" / "Quoting your message" with two lines of the original text, and **Remove** drops just that card. Several can stack, and the same message is quoted only once (`withQuote`, same as iOS). On sending, like other context, it goes before the body: `<persona-context kind="quote" title="…" url="" msg="…">`.
- Why it stopped on an error / is waiting for you (#2976 G044, iOS `FailureNote` / `AttentionNote`): `Session` parses the hub's `failure_reason` and `attention_reason`. When the session needs you and has a reason, a warning-colored note sits above the composer. When the host stopped this turn on an error it won't retry by itself (a botmux notification) and the session isn't working, an error-colored note sits above the composer (with the leading ⚠️ removed) and **Retry** on the right: it sends a separate "继续" ("continue") without touching what's in the composer, and the notice you tapped is no longer shown. While the previous message is still submitting, **Retry** is disabled; if it isn't accepted (`send` returns false) it isn't hidden, and if sending fails the notice reappears (`hostRetried` is held by the chat page). When the session has a link on the host side (`external_url`, https only, `Session.externalLink`, same as iOS `externalLink`), both notes have **Open in Lark**, opened with the system. They are not shown while starting or when the start failed (there's `ChatStartingNote`). In both cases the Agents list row also shows the reason instead of the last sentence (`Session.reasonPreview`, same as iOS `listPreview`). Code: `ChatHostNotes.kt`.
- Reply suggestions (#2976 G057): tapping a next line Claude suggests (the chips above the composer) fills it into the composer; long-press opens a menu with **Send now** and **Put in the box**, and accessibility actions also include **Send now** (same as iOS `SuggestionPill`).
- Queue strip: messages sent while working and not yet picked up (the session's `queued`) are shown right above the composer (iOS `QueueStrip`), only while the composer is shown, in Agents chats and in Persona's conversations (below). Its line says "N queued · {Agent} will handle them in order"; each entry is numbered and at most two lines (design system `QueueStrip`). On Glass it works as iOS's strip (#4339 C6): folded to its line by default, a tap on the line opens or folds it, a new queue starts folded, and opened, each entry sits in a well and about three show before it scrolls (`queueListCap`). Brief and Board keep their always-open card and ticket with the height capped, without the fold or Send now (the alignment plan aligns Glass only). Tapping one opens a `JActionSheet` (titled with the message; iOS uses a menu, which Android keeps for two or three items): **Take back to edit**, then for Claude Code in a terminal pane (`Session.steersQueue`) **Move up** and **Move down** (greyed at the ends), then **Copy** and **Delete** (danger color). For such a session Glass's line also has **Send now**, folded or open, which at one press gives the agent the whole queue (#7170, as the web since #7154 and iOS #7169; `docs/design/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` 4.4; `ChatActions.sendQueuedNow`, `POST …/queue/send-now`). TalkBack reads the button's name and what the confirmation used to say (a command it runs goes on in the background, and a reply it writes starts again with the message; `stateDescription`), then the toast says "Sent to the agent now"; a refusal shows the hub's words for `nothing_queued` / `send_now_unsupported` / `send_now_unrecognized` / `send_now_in_doubt` / `node_outdated` (`sendNowFailure`). Pulling back, deleting and moving all go through `ChatActions.editQueue` (`POST …/queue {keep}`: every index except this one, or all of them in the new order, `queueMoved`; the node pulls back the whole queue, then puts the kept ones back in that order; an empty list is sent too). Pulling back to edit takes the original text for that index from the response and puts it back in the composer (if the draft is empty it becomes the draft, otherwise it's appended on a new line; its photos are restored as attachments), saying "Moved back to the input box. Edit it and send again."; delete says "Removed from the queue"; copy says "Copied"; failure says "Didn't change: reason". While an operation runs a spinner shows (in Send now's place on Glass) and the whole strip is disabled until the session refreshes; if the queue changes while the menu is open, the menu closes. Only sessions that can send (`session.canSend`) can be operated. In Persona's conversations (#4339 C6, iOS's strip with `own` and `persona`; every style, since seeing one's own queue is behaviour, each drawing its own strip) the strip sits under the Needs you / In progress entries and the route chip, right above the composer; its line names Persona ("3 queued · Persona will handle them in order"). The user's own messages come first and have the actions above, Move up / Move down included and on Glass Send now (`reordersQueue`: Persona's Claude Code takes the queue as an interrupt over stream-json), Send now at one press, TalkBack reading Persona's words ("Persona gets them right away instead of at its next step…"), and a refusal showing the hub's words for `assistant_control_failed` too. The last `queued_relayed` came from other conversations or the system (`Session.queuedRelayed`, `ownQueued` in core/domain): each says "Arrives when this chat is free" under its words (`QueueStrip`'s `notes`, in every style) and offers only **Copy**, and `keep` and the moves index only the user's own. Brief and Board draw it as their Agent chat's strip: always open, without Send now. Code: `ChatQueueStrip.kt`; tests `ChatQueueStripTest`, `ComposerPartsTest`, `SessionSourceTest`, wire fixture `sessions_assistant_queue`, shots `ChatQueueStripShotsTest` (`build/queue-shots/`), `AssistantQueueShotsTest` (`build/assistant-queue-shots/`).
- Refused pending messages: a message refused by the computer (`input_draft`, `dialog_open`…) or manually confirmed as not delivered typed nothing into the computer, so the button row under the bubble (`OutboxStatus`, also used by the session status sheet) gains **Delete** (`DeliveryAction.Delete` → `sender.cancel`; `OutboxMessageSender.cancel` now also accepts Refused); **Edit** puts it back in the composer and deletes the original bubble. Messages with an unclear result must still be checked first and can't be deleted directly. The bubble says why in words: the chat's own for `input_draft`, `dialog_open`, `op_id_reused` and `invalid`, the shared coded feedback for the rest (`node_unreachable` "Your computer is offline…", `stale_session`, `claude_missing`…), and "The computer didn't accept this message" for a code without words; never the raw code (`refusalText`, #2821).
- Where a failed message sits (#2769, iOS `ChatLines.placeFailed`): a refused, rejected or unconfirmed message goes right above the first message sent after it (`placeFailed` in `core/domain`, by when this phone wrote each send, `MessageSender.writtenAt`; a line typed elsewhere uses the computer's time less the clock slack). One with nothing after it or without a time, and every message still on its way, stays at the bottom. Because a bubble can sit between lines, the page maps list rows to lines by key, never by position: read receipts, the new-message count and the scroll after collapsing work. Tests: `PlaceFailedTest`, the replay case `send_failed_stays_above_later`, `FailedSendRowsTest` (also screenshots, three styles × light / dark, `build/failed-send-shots/`).
- A message that can't go yet (#2821, as iOS `SendSafety` / `OutboxWait`): a 502 `node_unreachable` with `delivered: none` (the computer asleep) is refused at once with its code, so **Retry** sends it under a new operation id. One that keeps coming back waiting while the hub link is up (503 `assistant_offline`, a 5xx without a code, a main chat that can't start yet) is tried again every 10 s and refused with its code after 60 s (`OutboxMessageSender` `StallRetryMillis` / `StallLimitMillis`, kept in memory; the link dropping starts the minute again). Waiting while the link is down has no limit.
- Read-only sessions: with `ChatComposer(enabled = false)`, "+" and send are disabled, the microphone and the "/" list are hidden, and the placeholder text is the `placeholder` passed in; with `canInterrupt = false`, there is no **Stop** even while working (see the next section).
- Who you write to, and who is getting ready (iOS `Composer` placeholder, `startingTurn`; every style): an Agent chat's empty composer reads "Message Claude" ("Message Codex", or the runtime's agent by the hub's name, `agentPlaceholder`); Persona's conversations keep "Message". Under your newest unsent message "Claude is getting ready…" (`TypingLine`, three dots that pulse) shows while it is still going in, no work line runs for it yet, and the agent is at work, starting up, or the message is on its way while the hub may not have caught up (`gettingReady` in `ChatStartingNote.kt`). It and, on Glass, a load error as a line of the chat (`ChatSystemLine`; Brief and Board keep it above the composer) are rows at the list's newest end, before the unsent messages (`tailRows`, counted in the list's row ids). While the first page loads, Glass draws iOS's skeleton rows where the newest lines will be (`ChatSkeleton`); Brief and Board keep the spinner. All of this is an Agent's chat's: Persona's conversations keep the spinner, the error above the composer and the pill's scroll (iOS does these there too, the open recap aside; left for a later batch). Tests: `ChatDetailsTest`; shots `ChatDetailsShotsTest`, `ChatPlanSheetShotsTest` (`build/chat-details-shots/`).
- The component catalog (debug build) has three sections, **Attachments**, **Composer suggestions** and **Queue strip**, and a composer sample with "dictation + stop". Unit tests: `SlashCommandsTest`, `ComposerParityTest` (@, quotes, host reasons, /model /loop), `DictationTest`, `ChatComposerStateTest`, `ComposerCameraTest`, `ChatDeliveryStateTest`, `ChatQueueStripTest`, `ComposerPartsTest` in `core:designsystem`; `OutboxMessageSenderTest` in `core:data` (refused ones can be deleted, sent ones can't).
- Screenshots: `MOCK_QUEUE=1` (the working session `macbook/a1f3c2d4-…0001` has two queued messages, `POST …/queue` edits by keep, `GET /mock/queue_edits` shows the log), `MOCK_SEND_REFUSE=input_draft` (refused bubble), `MOCK_UPLOAD_KBPS=8` (uploading), `MOCK_UPLOAD_OLD_NODE=1` (upload failure). Drafts are stored per pairing and survive `am force-stop`: type once or add one attachment in a session, then cold-start through each style × light / dark and take screenshots.

## Closeout, decisions and tasks nobody works on

Three things the hub already says, shown on Android (#3612, #3804, #3618; wire and rules in `docs/assistant/agent-work.md` "Closeout" and `docs/assistant/todos.md` "Nobody is working on this", "Decisions the owner can answer"). All fields are optional: an older hub's rows read as before.

- **Models** (`core:contracts`): `WorkState.closeout` (`Closeout`, `CloseoutChecked`) and `artifacts`, `Todo.orphaned`, the decision fields of `TodoAsk` (`question`, `why_owner`, `options`, `background`, `cost_of_waiting`, `default`, `deadline`, `responses`, `response`), `TriageSwitches.closeout`. Rules in `core:domain`: `sort/Closeout.kt` (which closeout shows, each claim against the hub's finding, the row's facts and mark, `stripCloseout`), `tasks/Decision.kt`, `tasks/Orphaned.kt`.
- **Closeout card** (`feature:chat` `CloseoutCard`): above the transcript of a stopped Agent's own page, never on an assistant conversation, a subagent or a workflow agent, and gone while the Agent works. The head says the status and "The agent's own report" and folds the fields: what changed, artifacts (a link is a small button, a pull request reads "PR #12"), merged and deployed, verified, still needs. Beside merged and deployed stands the hub's finding in three states that differ in words, mark and color: confirmed (check mark), contradicted (warning mark, the caution text color, the hub's own sentence) and not checked (quiet words, no mark). The fields scroll within 40% of the window's height. List rows are summaries, so the page reads the session's whole row once per closeout (`TasksStore.sessionDetail`, the `sessions_lookup` exact lookup) for what the Agent did, verified and produced; until it answers, and on a hub without the lookup, the card shows the row's part.
- **The reply without its block**: while the card shows, the last turn's answer (`closeoutReplyId`) is drawn without its last `<closeout …>…</closeout>` block, with a code fence that held nothing else; a reply that was only the block is not drawn. Nothing else of the transcript changes, and the block is back once the card goes.
- **List rows** (`feature:agents`): the line under the title is today's (`Session.workLine`; a turn the hub gave no line says the closeout's own words, not the raw block). Where the closeout stands is the first of the row's facts ("merged ✓", "not deployed", `Session.closeoutFacts`): at the end of the line it would be the part a phone cuts off. A "yes" the hub found untrue reads "✗ says merged, isn't" in the caution color and puts a warning mark beside the title; a turn that is not finished (an empty `work.phase` with a line) has a dashed-circle mark there. Every line stays one line.
- **Nobody is working on this** (`Todo.orphanedLine`): "Nobody is working on this · closed after an error" in the caution color where an open task says its state: the To-dos page's row, the task card and the task's page in the main chat, and the In progress tray's row. Not shown for a done task, a failed start, or while an Agent or the owner's ask says what it waits for.
- **Decisions** (`TaskDecisionAnswers`, the Needs you tray and the task's page): a `decide` ask with at least two options shows the question, the hub's background line, each option with what it leads to (the first marked **Recommended**), what happens when nobody answers and what waiting costs, then one line of answers (`DecisionAnswers` in `core:designsystem`): the options that fit as buttons (the first the only solid one), **Other…** (opens the task; on the task's page it routes the composer to the task) and a menu with the options that did not fit, the fixed answers the hub takes ("You decide", "This isn't what I asked for", "Tell me the background first") and **Finish now**. An option of an `irreversible` or `visible_to_others` decision takes two presses: the first turns it into "Confirm: <option>". Answers go through `POST /api/v1/todos/{id}/accept` with the round the owner read and `option` or `response` (`TasksApi.answerTask`); 409 reads "Already handled". An ask without options (an older hub) keeps **Follow recommendation**. A decision with three or four options is taller than the tray, so its answers are one short scroll away.
- **Settings › AI › Session triage**: **Ask for a closeout** under the actions, disabled while the main switch is off, with the same failed-save handling as the other switches; the row is absent on a hub that does not send `closeout`.
- Screenshots (Robolectric, every style, light and dark): `CloseoutShotsTest` (`feature/chat/build/closeout-shots/`), `AgentCloseoutRowShotsTest` (`feature/agents/build/closeout-row-shots/`), the triage page in `SettingsCoreShotsTest`. Not captured on an emulator.

## Rule suggestions, held decisions, recommended setup and purpose descriptions (#5802)

Four things the hub already sends, drawn on Android (the hub's rules: `docs/assistant/unified-entry.md` "A rule after the same answer three times", `docs/assistant/todos.md` "Decisions that can wait", `docs/assistant/assistant.md` "Recommended setup", `docs/assistant/model-config.md` "API"). Every field is optional: a hub from before them, or one field of another shape, leaves the card, the row and the page as they were (`RuleSuggestionContractTest`).

- **Models** (`core:contracts`): `RuleSuggestion` / `RuleScope` (`RuleSuggestion.kt`) on `TaskPlan.suggestion`, `TodoAsk.suggestion` and `Interaction.suggestion`; `TodoAsk.forBriefing` (`for_briefing`); `BriefBody.setup` (`BriefSetup`, decoded row by row in `Brief.kt`, a row without an id or without words is skipped); `ModelPurpose.description` (`about`: the sentence when it says something). `TasksApi.ruleAlways` / `ruleNotNow` are `POST /api/v1/assistant/rule-suggestions/{id}/always` and `…/not-now` (`HttpTasksApi`, the id as one escaped path segment; `RuleSuggestionMade` is what Always answers).
- **Rule suggestion** (`feature:chat` `RuleSuggestionLine`): one quiet block under the card that carries it, never inside it and never a card of its own. Under an open plan card (`PlanCard`), under a to-do's decision in the Needs you tray and on the task's page (`TaskDecisionCard`, both the answers on the row and an older hub's recommendation button), and under a question a supervisor passed up in the chat's panel (`InteractionPanel`'s `suggestion` slot, only in a Persona conversation and only while the card is not folded). It says, in this order: the hub's own line (what was noticed, in the user's language), **Applies to** the computer and the directory, exactly what Always stores, then two one-line buttons, **Always** (secondary) and **Not now** (quiet). What Always stores is worded by the hub's `creates`: `tasks.trust` remembers the kind of task so Persona starts ones like it without a plan card; `standing_authorization` names the action (merge, push, deploy to the test environment; another action keeps the hub's word), the conditions (`ci_green`, `no_conflicts`; another keeps the hub's word) and the quote kept as the owner's words; an empty `creates` (a `delegate` suggestion) says that no rule is stored and, when the hub names a `setting` (`agents.delegate`), that it is turned on in the assistant's configuration file on the hub's computer, the words the hub's own recommended-setup line uses (the setting is the assistant's, not `hub.json`'s). A `creates` this build does not know gets no line of ours: the hub's text and the scope stand alone, and the app never says that nothing is stored unless `creates` is empty.
- **After Always** (`TasksStore.ruleAlways`, `RuleOutcome`): the block becomes one line with a check mark that says what was made and where it is listed and removed. This app has no page for either list, so it names the web app's: **Remembered auto-start rules** and **Standing authorizations** in Persona's settings there; for `delegate`, that nothing was stored and which setting to turn on; for a rule this build does not know, only **Rule made.** The hub sends the card again without its `suggestion` once it is answered, so the store keeps the answer with the suggestion for the card it was given on (`ruleAnswers`) and the line stays with it, on a started plan too. It is kept in memory only: it is gone after the app restarts.
- **An answer is the card's own** (`RuleAnswer`): the keys are `plan:<id>`, `card:<id>` and, for a to-do, its decision round (`todoRuleCard`: the to-do's id with the ask's `asked_at`). Nothing is kept by the suggestion's id, because the hub's ids name a kind and a scope and come back: on the next card once the answers earn the rule again, and, for the delegate (one id for every assistant), on another assistant's card. So what was made on one decision is not drawn under the to-do's next decision, a stale suggestion does not hide the line on a later card, and Not now for one assistant's question says nothing about another's.
- **Not now, stale, refused**: Not now removes the block at once on the card it was tapped on; another open card with the same suggestion loses it when the hub sends that card again, and tapping there meanwhile is answered 404. 404 (the answers do not earn it any more, or it was answered on another device) removes the block the same way and shows no error. A line put away is settled once the hub sends the card without it (`ruleSettled`), so the same card can carry it again later. Any other refusal is shown as the page's notice and leaves the block to answer again: 403 without the full-access role, 409 when the standing authorizations changed meanwhile, 503. One of the two actions at a time per card, and the button that was tapped is the one that shows progress. The requests name the plan's conversation's assistant, the to-do's, or the card's (`docs/assistant/managers.md` "Clients").
- **Held for the next briefing**: a decision whose `ask` says `for_briefing` has a quiet clock mark and **Comes with the next briefing** on its decision card (`ForBriefingMark`, under the background line, both card variants), and its row on the To-dos page says **Decision comes with the next briefing** under its activity line (`TodoTriageRow`). It is answered as any decision; the mark goes once the hub clears the field.
- **Recommended setup on the briefing card** (`BriefCard` `BriefSetupSection`): on a briefing whose `brief` item carries `setup`, the card ends with one more section, **Recommended setup**: the note that the numbers come from Persona's own records of the last 7 days and that nothing was changed, then one row per mechanism with the hub's line as it came. The line takes the card's width and its button sits under it, as the card's other rows are laid out. A row has **Open settings** only when its mechanism has a page in this app (`BriefSetup.page`): `triage_decide`, `triage_auto_continue` and `triage_wake` open Settings › Session triage, `supervisor_tier` opens the background supervisor's model purpose (`ModelPurposeRoute("assistant.supervise")` for the conversation's assistant). The other mechanisms (the delegate, standing authorizations, remembered kinds of task, leaders, the two reviews) and one a newer hub names have no button and say where in their text; the hub's absolute `link` is not used. Opening is `ChatChromeBindings.onOpenSetup`, wired in `:app` (`ShellChat`); it calls nothing on the hub. No section without `setup`.
- **Purpose descriptions** (`feature:settings`): in Settings › Model configuration a purpose's row leads its subtitle with the hub's `description`, before what is in effect (`purposeSubtitle`; such a row may take six lines instead of three, `purposeSubtitleLines`), and the purpose's own page says it under the assistant row (`model-purpose-about`). A task's, an extension's and an older hub's purpose have none and read as before. The app has no sentence of its own for any purpose.
- **The background supervisor's row** (#5806): a hub that lists `assistant.supervise` in its catalog is not asked for it by id, and the row is where it was, last of its group, because the hub lists it last of the built-in purposes and this page groups by `group` in the hub's order. The request by id stays for a hub from before (`ModelCatalogModel.withBackgroundSupervisor`).
- **Not done here**: the Agents tab's request cards (`AgentRequests`) and the Needs you tray's one-line rows for an Agent's card do not draw the suggestion; it is under the card once the card is open in the conversation. An Agent's own chat page does not draw it either (it has no tasks store). There is no page for the remembered kinds of task, the standing authorizations or `agents.delegate`.
- Tests and screenshots (Robolectric, every style, light and dark; native JVM captures, not an emulator's): `RuleSuggestionShotsTest` (`feature/chat/build/reports/tests/rule-suggestion-shots/`: the three cards with their lines, after Always, after Not now / 404 / 409, a to-do's next decision, a rule this build does not know, and without the fields), `BriefCardShotsTest.theWeeksRecommendedSetupOpensOnlyThePagesThisAppHas` and `largestTextKeepsASetupLineAtTheCardsWidthInEveryStyle` (`feature/chat/build/reports/tests/brief-setup-shots/`: `brief-setup-*.png`, and `brief-setup-large-*.png` with text at 200%), `TodoForBriefingShotsTest` (`feature/agents/build/reports/tests/todo-for-briefing-shots/`: the To-dos row), `ModelConfigurationScreenTest.aPurposeSaysWhatItIsForInTheListAndOnItsPage` (`feature/settings/build/reports/tests/model-described-shots/`); these folders are under `build/reports/tests/` so that the Android CI run uploads the pictures with its reports; `RuleSuggestionStoreTest` in `core:domain`, `HttpTasksApiTest.aSuggestedRuleIsAnsweredOnItsTwoRoutes`, `ModelConfigurationStateTest`.

## Session controls, session origin and capabilities

Ported 2026-11-02 from the old App on the main line (inventory items C5–C7, C9, D2–D5). The rules match iOS; the logic is in `core:domain`: `sort/SessionSource.kt` (capabilities, origin wording, resume commands), `sort/AgentClose.kt` (close confirmation), `answer/RequestPresentation.kt` ("Always allow").

- Session origin and capabilities (`docs/protocol/protocol.md` ("Codex sources and capabilities")): `Session` carries `source_kind` (`codex_app_server` / `codex_history` / `identity_conflict` / `runtime`), `source_thread_id`, `read_only_reason`, `capabilities` (read / send / interrupt / approve / terminal / attach, plus optional `modes`, `close`, `rename`, `model`, `rewind` and `rewind_code`), `attached`, `agent_label`. Old sessions without `source_kind` can do everything; sessions with an origin only get capabilities that are explicitly true (a missing field means no, except that missing `modes` keeps the older agent-based fallback, and a missing `close`, `rename`, `model`, `rewind` or `rewind_code` keeps the terminal rule below: an older node; a declared one wins, the same rule on every client, #6294); `canAttach` = has an origin, `attach` is true, and not yet `attached`. Before session metadata arrives the phone doesn't block anything (the hub / node will still refuse); read-only and capability checks use the metadata that is known.
  - Chat page: when it can't send, the composer is disabled with the placeholder **This session is view only**, and a line above shows the origin (`JTag`) and the reason (the node's "View only:" prefix isn't doubled); the web card's "Use as context / AI edit" are not accepted either. Without the terminal capability there is no terminal entry (⋯'s Terminal, the work line's mark, the header's button on Brief and Board); without interrupt there's no **Stop** and no **Interrupt** menu item; without approve, the interaction panel still shows the request but no button can be pressed (not even **Leave to computer**), and the note says "Attach live to answer this request on the phone…" (when live attach is possible) or "This session can't be approved on the phone. Approve it on the computer." The top bar subtitle starts with the origin ("Codex live / server / directory").
  - ⋯ menu: **Join live** (`canAttach`, `ChatAction.Attach` → `POST …/attach`; when the result is unclear it checks `attached` in the session list; once attached the node lets it approve). **Rename** and **Close agent** go by `capabilities.rename` / `capabilities.close` where the node says (a runtime's session, which has no terminal, #6294: its rename dialog has no note about a tab or Claude's /rename, and the close confirmation only says the session ends), else they need a terminal (`Session.canRename`, `Session.canClose`; shots `RuntimeSessionActionsShotsTest`, `build/runtime-session-actions-shots/`); **Copy resume command** is only for sessions with a terminal (Codex uses `codex resume <source_thread_id>`).
  - Agents list: rows with an origin show it in their facts ("Codex live / codex-desk"), plus **View only** when read-only; Agents the App doesn't know show the hub's `agent_label` ("DSH") instead of always calling them Claude. The long-press menu gains **Join live** (`AgentOperations.attachSession`); rename and close (the swipe too) go by `capabilities.rename` / `capabilities.close` where the node says (#6294), else they need a terminal, as the resume command always does, and **Stop loop** needs send. Request cards, request details and `AgentsViewModel.answer` never answer on behalf of sessions that can't approve, and request details offer no **Open terminal** without a terminal. Origin sessions opened from History don't offer **Continue this session**, and the read-only reason is shown.
  - Notifications: allow / approve on a notification first looks up the session the request belongs to (on cold start it refreshes the session list once first); if the session can't approve, nothing is sent back and it says **Can't approve on this phone** (`NotificationActionResult.ReadOnly`); if the session can't be found, the hub decides.
- Agent runtimes (DSH): `Session.agentLabel` follows iOS: Codex origin → Codex, `traex` → compatible Codex forks, empty or `claude` → Claude, anything else uses `agent_label` (or the raw `agent` if there is none). `isClaude` is a positive check; Claude-only controls (mode, `/model`, `/clear`) are only for it. `Interaction` carries `agent` / `agent_label` / `always_label` / `reason`: cards from other Agents carry its name in the title ("Codex needs your permission", "DSH has a question"), and permission cards show its own stated reason first. **Always allow in this session** is only on Claude's cards; other Agents' cards get **Always allow** only when they carry `always_label`, with "Always allowing lets through: …" under the button (required by `always_offered` in `ApprovalPolicy`; shared fixtures `spec/fixtures/approval/unified.json`, `answer/unified.json`).
- The model pill and the unified model panel on Glass (#5055, the web's #5032, #5033, #5035; `docs/web/web.md` "Model pill and model panel"): the pill (`JModelPill`) sits in the composer of an Agent's chat, of a Persona chat and of New chat's page, before the mic and Send while the toolbar shows (`ChatComposer`'s `modelPill` slot, `ChatModelPill.kt`). An Agent's panel lists its computer's launchers with their /model lists (`AgentModelPanel` in core/domain): a pick on its own launcher sends `POST …/modes {model}` then `{effort}` (Claude Code of the user's own, and a runtime's session whose row says `capabilities.model`, #6294: the node's word wins, as the web's `canSwitchModel`); on a hub with `launcher_switch` a Claude Code Agent in a terminal (no source: the node moves no runtime's session to another launcher) may pick another launcher of its engine, recorded with `{launcher, from, model?, effort?}` and drawn at once (`DrawnSwitch`), and the user's next message from the chat goes with `apply_switch` (`MessageSender.sendApplyingSwitch`, kept in the outbox), so the hub restarts the agent there; the pending switch comes from the session's complete row (`ChatActions.completeRow`, `sessions_lookup`) where the list holds summaries. A Persona chat's panel lists what its computer runs Persona chats on (`?purpose=assistant`, the unavailable ones off with the hub's reason) with a strip saying where the setup comes from; a pick is `PUT …/conversations/{id}/model` and Follow default / Undo pick a `DELETE` on a hub with `assistant_model_pick` (an older hub keeps the pick on the page and sends it with each message, as #5586's chip did), and summary rows keep the chat's details (`keepingDetails`). New chat's pick goes with the start (`startConversation(ModelPick)`). Favorites, Recent and per-model setups are kept on the phone (`PanelPrefsStore`, apart for Agents and Persona chats). Where the replies' model changes the chat draws `JModelChangeDivider` (`ModelChanges.mark`, the items' `model`). Brief and Board keep the Mode sheet, the launcher chips and the header's mode button, pixel-identical. Not on Android yet: the switch to the other engine (#5054), Codex's Fast, quota bars and reference prices, forgetting a typed id. Tests: core/domain `ModelChangesTest`, `ModelPanelTest`, `AgentModelPanelTest`, `PersonaModelPanelTest`, `SummaryRowsTest`; `ModelPillTest`, shots `ModelPillShotsTest`, `NewChatModelPillShotsTest`.
- The header's permission mode (#3957, iOS #3719): on Brief and Board an icon-only button before ⋯ (`HeaderModeButton`, the design system's `JModeButton` and `ModeTone`); on Glass the same button sits in the composer's toolbar beside the model pill and the header has none (#5055: decision 11 of the alignment plan, and the web, whose top bar dropped it for the composer's permission picker in #5421; iOS still draws it in its header's second row). The button shows the mode's symbol in its tone, always: Ask First the quiet gray, Plan Only teal, Auto-Edit and Auto purple, Allow Everything the danger red; a mode not reported yet the plain shield, never a guessed Ask First; none for Codex or an assistant conversation in the header (`SessionModes.showsInHeader`); Glass's composer also shows a Persona chat's (`showsInComposer`). The words are its description and its menu (the switchable modes, the current one checked, Allow Everything greyed with its reason where the agent can't take it). The header says the state once, in the title's line (as iOS, which shows no state above its composer): the line above the composer leaves the state out and shows only what the header doesn't, the queued / loop / background / draft facts (on Glass not the loop and background, which its header shows; next bullets) and a message whose delivery isn't confirmed, and is gone without them (`StatusLine` `sayState`); on Brief and Board the header's title opens the status sheet it opened (on Glass it opens Session Info, as iOS; next bullet). Board drops its mark before the title. Checked by `HeaderModeTest` and `HeaderModeShotsTest` (each mode, the menu, both confirmations; three styles × light / dark).
- The header's first words (#4339 C4, iOS `ChatView.stateWords`, every style): **Working N min** while a turn runs (**N hr M min** past an hour; counted from the newest turn's start, `ChatHeader`'s `turnStart`, and only while the agent works and its turn is live, `workedSince`), the state's name otherwise, **Working (Background)** once the turn ended and a workflow keeps the agent going and **Waiting in the background** when all it runs only waits (`AgentSession.stateLabel`, core/domain; a turn that ended while busy background commands go on says **Working**). The status line's own "Waiting" (a loop or background work on a seen chat) is not a state of the session, so the header does not say it (`headerStatus`): a seen chat with a loop says nothing in words and has Seen's dot, as iOS. On Glass the wait draws the clock for its dot and its words in the lighter tone (`JStateDot`, `ChatTopBar`'s `waiting`), in the folded slim bar's dot too; Brief and Board keep their dots and colors. Checked by `ChatHeaderWordsTest`; shots `ChatHeaderWordsShotsTest` (`build/header-words-shots/`).
- The folding header (Glass, #4339 C4, iOS `ChromeHeader` and `compactChrome`): an Agent's chat header (the title row, the space's tabs, the ports and the goal) folds into a slim bar under the status bar: back, and in the middle a capsule with the agent's mark, the state's dot and the title (while the hub is out of reach, a spinner and the connection's words), nothing on the right. It follows the chat list's own scrolling (`NestedScrollConnection`, never the app's jumps): toward the newest it folds as far as the list moves, back into the history it opens; when the finger lifts (and its fling ends) it is never left half way, folded at the newest end, else the nearer end. Left 3 s without a touch on the list or the header it folds by itself, not while a sheet, menu or the files / terminal cover is up, and never while animations are off (the system's animator scale 0: then the fold and the opening jump instead of moving). A tap on the capsule, a request the page had not seen, and coming back from the terminal or the files open it again; the messages never move (the list is upside down: only its oldest end's room changes). Folded, the open header stays composed (what it opened stays up) but takes no touches and leaves TalkBack. The title opens Session Info, or while the hub is out of reach why. Persona's conversations, Brief and Board keep their header (`HeaderFoldRules`, `ChatHeaderFold`, design-system `JFoldingHeader` and `ChatSlimBar`). Checked by `ChatHeaderFoldTest`; shots `ChatHeaderFoldShotsTest` (`build/header-fold-shots/`).
- A loop and background work (Glass, #4339 C8, iOS `ActivityChips`, `ActivitySheet`, `LoopStrip`, `BackgroundTasksStrip`): an Agent's chat with a /loop or background tasks (`Session.backgroundTasks`, workflows aside) shows one pill at the end of the header's second row, after the tabs and their ⋯, or in a row of its own without tabs: the loop's arrows in the loop color and its short words (the next wake-up's time, Running once it is due, else Loop), a divider, a neutral gear and the number of tasks (`JActivityPill`; with both at font scale 1.6 and up the loop is its arrows alone). TalkBack reads it in full ("Looping, Next 2:05 PM, 2 background tasks, tap to view"). A tap opens the sheet, titled Loop, Background Task, or Loop & Background Tasks: the loop's card ("Looping · Every 30 minutes", "Next wake-up 2:05 PM (4 minutes left)" or "Running this round", the task and the agent's reason when open) with **Stop Loop**, which asks first ("Stop this loop?") and then sends the agent iOS's message asking it to stop scheduling (`ChatActivity.STOP_LOOP`, the same words as iOS's `AppModel.stopLoop`; nothing outside the agent can cancel its wake-up), greyed where the chat can't send; and the background tasks' card, each task with what it runs, how long it has run and, with an output file, View Output, which opens the file in the files pane (`ChatChromeBindings.onOpenPath`). The line above the composer then leaves out its Loop and Background chips; the status sheet still lists both. The folded slim bar has no pill (iOS dropped its marks). Brief and Board keep their chips, which open the status sheet. Checked by `ChatActivityTest`; shots `ChatActivityShotsTest` (`build/activity-shots/`).
- Permission mode and reasoning effort (`ChatModes.kt`): the **Mode** button left of the composer is for every running Claude session that can send, Persona chats included (when the assistant process isn't running it applies from the next message); not shown for Codex / compatible Codex forks. A runtime gets it when `capabilities.modes` is explicitly true (including agy); explicitly false hides it for Claude outside Persona's terminals, and an older hub that omits it keeps the old agent-based rule (`Session.canChangeModes`). The sheet's **File permissions** has four options (Ask first / Auto-edit files / Plan only / Auto, each with a one-line description); a running session picks **Allow all** only when its process can take it (`bypass_available`, as on iOS and the web; otherwise it shows greyed, saying it is a start's choice), and when that's the current mode (or a mode this version doesn't know, shown by its raw value) it is still listed and selected, and the **Mode** button uses the danger color. Going into Allow all asks first, and so does leaving it when the agent can't come back to it from here (`SessionModes.confirmation`, `ModeConfirmDialog`). **Reasoning effort** has five levels (Low / Medium / High / Very high / Max). Changes go to `POST …/modes`.
- ultracode (#582, `Ultracode` in `ChatModes.kt`, the same rules as the web's `lib/ultracode.ts` and iOS `Ultracode`; `docs/protocol/protocol.md` ("Claude Code ultracode")): a switch under **Thinking effort** in the same sheet, shown when the hub lists the `ultracode` feature and the session is the user's running Claude Code that can change its modes (not Persona, no source). It sends `{"ultracode": bool}` alone and stands where it was put while the request is out, while the node says the change is queued (`ultracode_pending`, "Applies next turn") and until the push with the result arrives; the node's `ultracode_note` replaces the line (without the caution color the web uses: `JSwitchRow` has one subtitle color). While it is on, "ultracode" joins the mode hint above the composer. Under the switch, the Claude subscription's use: "Claude quota: 5h 86% · weekly 41%" (`UltracodeQuota` in core/domain `usage/UsageRules.kt`, the same account rules as iOS `Ultracode.quota`: a fresh sample first, then one read on the computers, then the fullest; drawn only with a Claude account's plan windows), "Out of date · 2 hr ago" when the hub says the numbers are old, and at 80% "Quota is running low; ultracode uses it up faster" in the caution color while the switch is on or can simply be turned on (`Ultracode.quotaWarnShown`). It reads `GET /api/v1/usage/windows` of the chat's own hub (`ChatAppBindings.usage`, `LocalUsageRead`) each time the sheet opens. The session info card has an **ultracode** row (on / off / unknown) for the user's Claude Code that isn't offline, on a hub with the feature (`Ultracode.infoShown`); Codex's **ultra** effort carries the same workflow mark in the sheet. New Agent, the list mark and the resume notice: see New Agent below. Tests: `SessionModesTest`, `HttpChatActionsTest`; shots `UltracodeSwitchShotsTest` (`build/ultracode-shots/`, three styles × light / dark).
- Model (`/model`): for Claude in a terminal-engine pane (not Persona), the same sheet has an extra **Model** section: Default (account default) / Opus (strongest) / Sonnet (balanced) / Haiku (fastest). The current one is decided by whether the session's `model` name contains opus / sonnet / haiku (gateway models and no model count as Default). Picking another sends a separate `/model <alias>` (the same path as quick commands; the composer content is kept). When the chat already has a cache, Claude first asks "Switch model?"; that's a dialog on the computer, and on the phone it becomes a pending card.
- Tab strip for the same space (`ChatSiblingTabs.kt`, iOS `SiblingTabs`, #477): a row under the top bar of an Agent chat that lists the running Agents on the same machine and in the same herdr space (one pane counts once: when /clear or a resume briefly shows two session ids, it takes the one currently open, otherwise the most recently updated). They are in tab order; several Agents in the same tab sit together, ordered by pane position. Names are herdr tab names, default numbering shows "#n", and when one tab has several Agents the Agent name is added ("#2 Codex"); the lead is marked "lead". Each is a `JChip` with a status dot, the current one selected, and on open it scrolls to the current tab. Tapping another tab opens that chat (`onOpenSession`); ⋯ on the right pops up "N agents" listing all of them (the lead's subtitle says **Lead**), and ⋯ gets a red dot when another tab needs you. Hidden when the space has only one tab, for Persona chats, and when there's no switch entry.
- Several Agents in one tab (#2515, web #2386, `docs/protocol/agent-states.md` ("Several panes in one tab")): when an Agent splits its own tab and starts other Agents in new panes, those Agents form a group.
  Ordering matches the web (`AgentTab.kt` in `core:domain`: `PaneOrder`, `launcherOf`, `agentTabs`): by the session's `pane_index`, or if missing by the number in the pane id (herdr's base-36 `p2E`, Herdr App's `t1`), then by session id; `pane_index` 0 is the lead; when the top-left is a shell the Agents are peers and none is marked.
  - The Agents list groups only "by machine" (same as the web; by repository / state / to-do still put one Agent per row): when a tab has several Agents there is first a tab row (`AgentTabRow`: tab name, the title / state / latest line of the one most in need of attention, "N agents" at the end to collapse / expand; tapping the row opens that Agent), and below it the lead comes first, marked **Lead**, with the panes it split off indented one level and hung on a hairline. The whole group sits where its most urgent member would have been, and if one member is shown the whole group is shown (no longer folded into "N more idle"). Expanded by default; the collapsed state is stored on the device by `machine/space name/tab number` (DataStore `folded-tabs`, without the tab name, so it still matches after a rename). The space header number still counts Agents.
  - The space page orders by tab and pane position the same way, with the lead marked and branches indented one level.
  - "Replaced by a new session" on the chat page (`SessionRuntimeState.sameSlot`): another still-alive pane in the same tab is not a stand-in for the session that exited; when both sides have a pane id they must be the same pane.
- Session info (`ChatSessionInfoSheet.kt`): ⋯ **Session info** (`GET …/info`): title, "status / machine / space"; **Open in Lark** for a session its host shows in a Lark topic (`external_url`, handed to the system); an Agent's **Questions the assistant answered** (`GET /api/v1/interactions/history`, its resolved questions with `answered_by: assistant`, newest first: when, each question and the answer, "Why: …"; none from a hub that refuses or lacks the history, a failure with Retry; changing an answer is not offered here); run (model, launcher, **How it runs** for an assistant conversation on the Agent SDK or fallen back to the CLI (`runtime`, `runtime_fallback`), Claude Code / Codex version, **Connect** for a Codex (`codex_route`: "app-server (structured)" or "Terminal (screen reading)" with `codex_route_note` under it), permissions, reasoning effort, ultracode), usage (context N tokens, **Context remaining** "61% left" over a bar from the session list's `context_tokens` / `context_window`, both in the caution colour from 80% used (iOS `ContextUsage`), compacted N times (only when > 0), N turns, N tokens generated in total, start time), location (directory, branch; tap to copy), copy (session id, resume command, only for sessions with a terminal). The new rows are in every style (#4339 C9; tests `ChatSessionInfoTest`, `HttpChatActionsTest.interactionHistoryReadsTheSessionsCards`, shots `ChatInfoShotsTest`). Tokens count in the language's compact unit, as the compaction divider: 950 / 12.3K / 1.2M in English, 1.2 万 in Chinese. When it can't be read it states the reason and can retry. Tapping the title still opens the new **Session status** sheet (delivery, queue, loop, background tasks, resume). The two entries split the job: status shows "how it is now", info shows "what it is and where it runs".
- Closing an Agent: the long-press menu, swiping a list row left (only for running sessions that have a terminal and aren't Persona; the row springs back before the confirmation; `SwipeAction(destructive = true)` paints the danger color, and no tone fills it) and the chat page ⋯ (not for Persona chats) share one confirmation: **Close this agent?** + "“title” / machine / space › tab" + which Agent on which machine will quit, which tab closes, that work in progress stops, and that the chat stays in History. On confirm it checks again, and if it was already closed elsewhere it just dismisses.
- Background task notices (`TaskNoteLine.kt`, iOS `TaskNoteRow`, #2602): a background command, a Monitor event or end, and a subagent reporting back are drawn as one line in the tools' look (`TaskIndex.note()`: the starting call's description or the event, with "kind · result · duration" under it). A tap shows the command / event / result, with **View output** (`output_file`, through `ChatRowActions.openPath`) and **Jump to the step that started it** (finds the call by `tool_use_id`, `callRecordTarget`; a call folded in a work line opens it first, one still in an unloaded hub fold opens the folds before the notice one by one; it uses the same positioning and **Back** as the task page's record link, `ChatCallJumps`). A notice whose call can't be identified (no `tool_use_id`, and no task id in a result) has no jump.
- Deleting a chat (#1749, aligned with web #801): sessions not running (`Session.canDelete`: not running / done / seen (idle) / unknown / interrupted, not starting, not the assistant's) have **Delete chat** in the long-press menu (for a not-running row, next to **Open read-only**) and in the chat page ⋯, with one shared confirmation text (core:domain `deleteConfirmation`: it's only removed from every device's list, the record on the computer is not deleted, and it reappears when used again). After confirming, `DELETE /api/v1/sessions/{mid}/{sid}` (the list uses `AgentOperations.deleteSession`, the chat page `ChatAction.Delete`; when the result is unclear it checks that the session is gone from the session list); the list hides it immediately and an open chat page exits with it. When the hub refuses (409, it's running again) it says so and doesn't retry.
- Connection state in the chat page title slot: when the hub can't be reached, the line under the top bar title becomes "Waiting for network / Connecting… / Updating…" (with a spinner beside the title while connecting and updating), and switches back to the session state as soon as it recovers; the 1.5-second delay and the 8-second catch-up cap live in `OkHttpHubConnection` / `ConnectionRuntime`, consistent with titles on other pages.
- Pending requests above the composer (#4210 T6, #4339 C10, iOS `PendingCards` and `TaskQuestions`, every style): the cards stack over the composer, the queue strip and the source note, which all stay; a message typed while a card waits, a permission included, is sent as any other. While they fit in about half the page (less what an open tray and the composer need; with the keyboard up, what the conversation has above the rest of the footer, `PendingStack.cap`) they stay in the order they came, the one opened from a notification first; past it they scroll under "N pending", permissions first and then the newest (`PendingStack.order`). Each folds on its own from its header to one line, its title and what it asks (`RequestFolds`, `foldPreview`), and opens to its full page from ↗. In Persona's conversations iOS's footer order holds: the oldest question of its tasks is a card of its own above the Needs you / In progress entries, under the task's name with "N more" for the tasks' other questions (`taskQuestion`, iOS `TaskQuestions`); the entries and the route chip; then the stack, which holds the conversation's own cards alone, so its "N pending" counts only them; the queue and the composer. A tray row opens what iOS's rows open: the conversation's own card is right below, so its row closes the tray onto it (unfolding it); a task's row opens that task's detail and an Agent's its own (`openTask`, `TaskSheet.Agent`), where Open chat brings its page and its cards. Review never brings a card into the stack. The two places of cards share the room the footer leaves above the composer (iOS draws the question uncapped, here the composer must stay on the page): each takes at most half the page, and with both on screen each has at least half of that room, a card that needs less leaving the rest to the other (`PendingStack.cap` with `other`: null when the other place has no card, 0 while it is not measured, taken then to hold its half; each frame reads the other's height of the frame before). Design system `DecisionStack` / `DecisionDock` (`docs/android/android-design.md` 2.2). Tests `PendingStackTest`, `PendingStackUiTest`, `DecisionSemanticsTest`; shots `PendingStackShotsTest` (`build/pending-stack-shots/`).
- Interaction panel details: question options are numbered, and the question's header and "Multiple choice" are shown as tags; the footer (**Previous**, "1 / N", **Next** / **Submit**) is drawn only for several questions or a multiple choice, as iOS `needsFooter`: a single-choice question is answered by tapping an option and gets one **Submit** only once "Other answer" has text (#2821); after **Leave to computer** the notice says "Handed back to the computer" (`AnswerReceipt`), not "Your choice was sent back", in the chat and on the Agents page; the Agents page's request sheet (a request card's title) closes once the answer given there is delivered, while one opened on an already-answered history item stays open, read-only (`RequestDeliveries.closesSheet`, screen test `AgentsRequestSheetTest`); a terminal dialog's header is shown above the question and options carry the terminal's keys; the option that goes back to the chat (marked `kind: chat` by the hub, e.g. "Chat about this") becomes a **Say it in the chat** button (choosing it sends that option to the terminal).
- After answering this Agent's last card while other Agents still have cards waiting (#4325, same as web #4091): a line appears under the panel's note, "N more need you · Next: <Agent> · <what it asks> ›". It counts the same list as "What needs you now" on New chat's page (`needsYou`, minus this chat) and takes the first in that order; tapping opens that chat with that card first (`InteractionRoute`). It never jumps away by itself and disappears after 20 seconds (`NEXT_WAITING_MILLIS`). It only shows after a real answer or "Answered already"; not after the screen changed, expiry, withdrawal or handing back to the computer (a changed screen brings a new card right away). Not shown while the keyboard is up or in read-only sub-Agents, same as the web. Persona's chats show it too, as iOS's `NextNeedsYouStrip` does (#4339 C18): after a card of theirs or of their tasks' Agents was answered there (in the panel or with the tray's Approve), and in the Assistant tab the shell provides the list and the way to open it (`LocalWaitingElsewhere`). The rules are pure functions `nextWaiting` / `offersNext` / `showsNextWaiting` (`feature/chat/NextWaiting.kt`), unit test `NextWaitingTest`, screenshots `NextWaitingShotsTest` (three styles × light / dark × zh / en / ja, `build/next-waiting-shots/`).
- Answering a request that has already ended (#2906, same as iOS #1688): when the hub returns 200 `already`, the panel says "Answered already: <the answer that took effect>" (`feature/chat/AnsweredText`, the same wording as the web's `lib/answered.ts`); for 410 it follows `status`: "it expired / the computer withdrew this request / this request was handed back to the computer, so the answer wasn't delivered"; without `status` it's still "Handled on the computer". `HubInteractionCenter` remembers request ids that ended on this device (at most 512) and doesn't bring them back even if later snapshots still list them, nor when the pending-card snapshot after a resumed connection sends them again as `interaction` events (#2767, as the web's `endedHere`). A card the hub withdrew (`interaction_resolved`) is different: it comes back when the hub raises the same id again, since resumable ids are deterministic. For 20 seconds after answering (or the hub saying it was already answered), if this Agent is still needs_you with no other cards, and that needs_you is the one the answer ended, the session list merged for pages treats it as working first (`core/data/AnsweredSessions.kt`, #4525, same as web #4186): list rows, the "Needs you" filter count, to-do rows, task cards, the chat status line and the Persona page change right away. The one the answer ended (`AnsweredMark.ended`, #709, as the web's `answered-lookup.ts`): any needs_you until the hub withdraws the card (`interaction_resolved`, seen before or after the answer's reply; an `already` answer counts as withdrawn), since a push sent before the hub took the answer can carry any time; after that, the same `state_since` as when it was answered, or one that started before the answer (the hub's clock against the phone's). When a new card comes or the hub pushes a needs_you that started after the answer, it goes back to "waiting for you" immediately. **Leave to computer** doesn't count as answered, also when tapped twice (the hub answers `already` with `status: "dismissed"`); the Agent is still waiting on the computer. An `already` with another status (answered elsewhere) counts as answered.
- An answer the hub refused (#1881, `InteractionSubmission.Refused`): a 4xx (the node could not type it into the terminal, a device paired to answer only sending something else) or a 503 `assistant_offline` once the transport's retries end means nothing was done, so the card stays answerable and the line under the panel gives the hub's reason ("The hub didn't take this answer: …"). Only a lost receipt (no answer, a 5xx without that code) keeps the card **Result unconfirmed** until the hub says more. Shots: `RefusedAnswerShotsTest` (`build/refused-answer-shots/`, before / after, three styles × light / dark).
- Mock hub: `MOCK_SOURCES=1` adds three sessions: "整理部署脚本" ("tidy deploy scripts"; Codex live, can send, can join live, can't approve before joining, with a Codex permission card that has `always_label`), "迁移日志采集" ("migrate log collection"; Codex history, read-only, with `read_only_reason`) and "生成季度报表" ("generate quarterly report"; DSH runtime, `agent_label: DSH`, no terminal, with a permission card without `always_label`). `POST …/attach` sets `attached` to true and enables approval, and returns 403 `codex_read_only` for sessions that can't attach. `/info` returns Codex's model and version for Codex sessions. `MOCK_SCREEN_CHAT=1` adds a terminal dialog with a header ("计划模式", "plan mode") and a back-to-chat option ("照片同步脚本支持 HEIC", "photo sync script supports HEIC"), to look at the interaction panel's tags, numbering and **Say it in the chat**. To see the Mode button under **Allow all**, `POST /api/v1/sessions/{mid}/{sid}/modes {"permission_mode":"bypassPermissions"}` to the mock hub directly.
- Unit tests: `SessionSourceTest` in `core:domain` (cases from the old `SessionSourcesTest` + runtime, resume commands), `AgentCloseTest`, `AlwaysOfferTest` and the answer / approval fixtures; `ModelsTest` in `core:contracts` (new fields, `agentLabel`, `SessionInfo`); `HttpChatActionsTest` (attach, queue, info) and `HttpAgentOperationsTest` (attach) in `core:hub`; `SessionModesTest` (old `SessionModesTest`, `ModelPickerTest`), `ChatSessionInfoTest` (old `SessionInfoTest`) and `InteractionOptionsTest` in `feature:chat`; `AgentsListInfoTest` in `feature:agents`; `NotificationExecutionTest` in `platform:notify` (sessions that can't approve send nothing back).

## Agent public sharing

The Agent session menu opens `AgentShareSheet`, backed by the authority-bound `HttpAgentShares` adapter and the
management contract in #3604. All history, From a message and individual selection use the complete sealed catalog;
the UI shows the selected count and a full sanitized preview. Future messages and execution default off, with a
three-day lifetime; the choices are 1, 3, 7 and 30 days, capped at creation plus 30 days.

Creation and widening require an exact preview and sensitivity acknowledgement, plus a separate transport-risk
acknowledgement on HTTP. A paused publication with its future preference still on has an explicit Resume action
that requires fresh preview consent and sends `include_future:true`. The list describes effective publication,
not just that preference: only active, live, future-enabled links add new messages. Sealed history remains available
for inspection, narrowing and revocation without the source; revocation has a destructive confirmation.

The bearer link is shown once, stays only in sheet memory until the user leaves or closes it, and is never persisted
in app storage or returned in the management list. A lost creation response is inspected through tokenless metadata, not retried.
`AgentShareModelTest`, `AgentShareShotsTest`, `HttpAgentSharesTest` and `AgentSharesWiringTest` cover policy consent,
selection, publication state and real application routing. For #3606, the maintainer directly authorized real
Compose/Robolectric native UI previews instead of emulator/device screenshots on the build machine without KVM;
the captures cover all three styles in light and dark and are explicitly JVM UI previews, not device evidence.

## Connection details and haptics (#3054, aligned with iOS)

- **Connection details**: when the hub can't be reached, the titles of Agents, Inbox, Lark chats and the Lark document reader turn into the connection state (the Agents page used to always show the machine name), and a tap pops up a **Connection details** sheet in place (design system `ConnectionDetails.kt`, modeled on iOS `ConnectionDetails`) instead of jumping to the Settings tab. The sheet gives the current state and reason (Waiting for network / Connecting… / Updating…, plus local network permission, certificate changed, pairing invalid), followed by a few facts: the last failure's reason and "Retrying in N seconds" (only while connecting), "Last connected: N seconds ago / N minutes ago / time", "N messages send once connected". States where retrying helps have **Retry now** (`HubConnection.reconnect()`, then refresh this page) and **Connection settings**; for certificate, pairing and permission, retrying is useless, so only **Connection settings** is offered. Online, the title isn't tappable (the Agents page used to jump to Settings even when online).
  - Data: `ConnectionDiagnostics.nextRetryAt` is the next attempt time `OkHttpHubConnection` writes while waiting between two attempts; "Last failure" reads `lastAttemptFailure` (localized text per failure kind, the same set as candidate address results in Settings; cleared on the first successful connection; like iOS, shown only for the most recent failure; the diagnostics page in Settings still shows the raw `lastDisconnectReason`); "Last connected" uses `lastReceivedAt` (the last message received from the hub); the pending count is the number of outbox entries sending / queued / waiting for network. The shell provides `LocalConnectionDetails` for the selected group (`Root.kt`); demo mode has no diagnostics and shows only the state.
  - Screenshots: `ConnectionDetailsShotsTest` in `core:designsystem`, connecting in three styles × light / dark, plus waiting for network and pairing invalid, output to `build/connection-details-shots/`.
- **Haptics** (design system `Haptics.kt`, four kinds matching iOS `Haptics`: Tap → ContextClick, Success → Confirm, Attention → GestureThresholdActivate, Error → Reject; the system haptics setting still applies):
  - Answer / approve (same on the Agents page and the chat page): sent successfully is Success; "the dialog on the terminal changed" is Attention; already handled elsewhere doesn't vibrate; unclear result is Error; tapping while the Agents page is offline is Attention.
  - Inbox (iOS `InboxStore`, #4210): one item filed or put back, by swipe, menu or the button on the Lark chat page, is Tap, and so is a conversation row, which counts as one item; many at once (Mark all as seen, Clear all, the picked rows' Mark done) are Success when filed and Tap when put back (`doneHaptic`).
  - Failed operations: notices shown through `AppFeedback.failure` are Error when online and Attention when the hub can't be reached (`FeedbackNotice.haptic`, played when the shell shows it).
  - Tapping the connection-state title and **Retry now** is Tap.

## Multiple hubs (connected to several groups of hubs at once)

#295; the design and the interface contracts between lanes are in [plan-hub-mesh.md](../archive/operations/plan-hub-mesh.md) sections 4 and 2.6; iOS is #256. The phone can pair with several hubs:
hubs that reach each other (linked hubs) form one **group**, which keeps a single connection and switches routes inside the group; different groups (environments that must stay apart, like work / personal) each have their own connection and don't affect each other.
Agents, machines and requests that need you are shown merged across groups; Persona, Inbox and hub-related settings belong to each group and use "the selected group".

### Places that assumed a single hub

Before the change (2026-11-02, integration branch `679b227f`) the App assumed a single pairing everywhere. Item by item, with what it does now:

| Place | Before | Now |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `HubScope` in `core:contracts` | One `hubId/generation` per pairing, data directory `hub-data/<hubId>/<generation>/` | Unchanged; each group's data lives in one such directory (the group's storage); credentials and candidate addresses are still stored per pairing |
| `EncryptedCredentials` / `HubDataStore` | `active.enc` points to the only pairing; `pair()` drops the old one before pairing the new one; `restore()` returns the only scope | A new group registry `hub-data/groups.enc` (`HubGroupRegistry.kt`) opens every group's scope at once (`openGroup` / `closeGroup`); `restore()` returns the selected group; the signatures of `pair()` / `unpair()` are unchanged |
| Credentials, candidate addresses, pinned fingerprints (`credentials.enc`, `endpoints.enc`) | One copy | One per paired hub (in its own pairing directory) |
| `ConnectionRuntime` | One `OkHttpHubConnection`, one `ScopedHubData`, one compatibility `Hub` | One `GroupRuntime` per group (`di/GroupRuntime.kt`); the old public API is unchanged and points to the selected group |
| `OkHttpHubConnection` / `HubHttpClient` | One hub's id, fingerprint, token | Several hubs per group: `HubConnectionConfig.members`, each with its own id, fingerprint, token and candidate addresses (`HubMembers.kt`) |
| `PairingModule` | Pairing means replacing | The original pairing page still replaces; Settings › hub › **Add hub** is `WiredPairingService.addHub` |
| `ChatDataOwner`, `AgentsWiringModule`, terminal / files / actions / machine monitoring in `AppServices` | Built from the only scope | One per group (`GroupStacks`), then merged (`MergedServices.kt`) |
| `InboxDataOwner` (Inbox, Persona session list), device settings, update check, lock-screen test, sharing, phone data tools, knowledge base | The only hub | The selected group |
| Machine management in `MachineManagementModule` (reconnect, redeploy, launchers, engine, botmux toggle, Claude Code / Codex settings) | Current pairing | The group named by the machine id's suffix (`mergedMachineManagement` + `MachineManagement.inGroup`, #634): machines from other groups used to be asked about at the selected group's hub, which couldn't find the machine or changed the wrong place; the machine whose engine is picked after pairing also comes from the merged list and routes by suffix the same way |
| Subscription limits on the machine monitoring overview (`MachineManagement.usageWindows`) | Current pairing | Every open group, each asked on its own hub at once and listed in the groups' order (`GroupUsage`, #1845, as iOS / Mac): the same account in two groups shows once in each; an old hub without the route (404) has none, and a group whose read fails keeps its last answer while the others show theirs |
| Model configuration (`MachineManagementModule.models`), voice (`VoiceExtensions` in `AppServices`) | Current pairing | The selected group: both are hub-level (one configuration and one voice extension per hub), and like Inbox and Persona they show one group at a time (decided in #634); machines in the model configuration are reported by that hub itself and use its ids as is |
| Candidate addresses in connection diagnostics | The only pairing | The selected group's own hub (the first paired hub in the group); candidates of other hubs in the same group also take part in route selection and are listed in diagnostics too |
| Whether request cards and request details on the Agents page can be answered; whether pin, mute, rename, close, delete, join live and stop loop on sessions and spaces can be sent; whether a computer's herdr is checked and its "herdr isn't running" notice shown and started | The selected group's connection | The connection of the group named by the request's, session's or computer's machine id suffix (`AgentGroups.stateFor`, #4291, #4702, #5726): one group going offline no longer blocks requests, operations and herdr notices in other groups; unchanged with only one group. A request card whose group is offline keeps Allow and Deny greyed out and says why, "Connect to the hub to send your choice", as the request detail does; in the Board style, whose cards are collapsed in the list, the reason (this one, or that the computer must approve it) sits above the quick buttons (`DecisionCard` `note`, #2483) |
| `Notify.install(connection.events)`, foreground service | Events from one WebSocket | Events from all groups merged into one stream (ids already suffixed, see below); the ongoing notification's state is the selected group's, and Agent counts add up across groups |
| `ScopedListPreferences` (Agents list settings), chat reading positions | The only scope | The first group's scope: switching the selected group doesn't touch them |

### Grouping and group ids

- The group registry (`GroupsDocument`) records each group's id, name (as renamed by the user), storage scope, paired hubs (`hubs`; the first one is the group connection's own hub),
  hubs discovered through linking but not paired (`mesh`: address, fingerprint, name), plus `primary` (the primary group) and `selected` (the selected group). It is encrypted like the credentials (Tink AEAD); if it can't be read, the original file is kept and an error is reported.
- **Grouping**: the authenticated hello carries `mesh.hubs` (plan 2.6), all hubs in the connected component this hub belongs to. Each time one arrives it updates each hub's peers: hubs in the list become each other's peers, and hubs outside the list drop the list members they had listed (so a new hello can correct stale information other hubs left behind).
  Two paired hubs are in the same group as long as either one's peers include the other; a group is a connected component (`learn` / `regroup`, pure functions, `HubGroupRegistryTest`). Hubs without the `mesh` field (hubs from before L1 merged) only update their name and form a group of their own.
- **Group ids** are generated on the phone (`g` + 8 hex digits) and are stable: a merge keeps the oldest id (the side containing primary-group members gets the primary group's id first); on a split the side containing the original group's first hub keeps the id, and the side left without one gets a new id and a new empty scope.
- **Merging loses nothing** (required by the maintainer 2026-11-02): the merged-away group's data is not deleted at first; the registry records a `PendingMove` (its scope, and which group it merged into). Once it's closed and the merged group is open, its **unsent messages** (everything except shown and cancelled ones; one interrupted mid-send is recorded as "result unclear" and checked with the hub before deciding to resend) and **non-empty drafts** are moved into the merged group: messages keep their original `client_msg_id` (the hub deduplicates by it, so delivery semantics don't change), machine and session ids don't change (after linking, machine ids are stable within the group, plan 2.6), and a non-empty draft for the same session already in the merged group isn't overwritten. Only after the move is that scope deleted (its paired hubs keep their credentials); caches aren't moved but fetched again. If the move fails (e.g. the database can't be read) it stays and is retried on the next registry change or the next launch, never cleared silently; if it crashes halfway, moving again doesn't duplicate anything (messages dedupe by `client_msg_id`, drafts aren't overwritten). Groups the user disconnected and revoked groups are not moved (the disconnect confirmation says local data will be cleared). Code: `GroupMerge.kt`, `HubDataStore.unsentAt`, `ConnectionRuntime.moveUnsentLocked`.
- On a split (one group becomes two), the split-off group uses a new empty scope; messages still in the original group's pending queue that belong to computers of the split-off hub are not moved; sending them fails and is shown (they don't vanish silently).
- **Pairing a hub that's already in some group** (it's in some group's `mesh`, or some hub's peers include it) merges it into that group automatically, without showing it twice; pairing the same hub again only replaces its credentials, and the group and data stay.
- **Migration**: on the first launch of the new version, the old single pairing becomes the primary group in place: its storage and its only hub are both the original pairing directory, and no files move. Database tables were already split by pairing scope (the `scope` column), one directory per group, so **there is no Room schema migration**, and the version numbers of `ItemsDatabase` / `PersistentDatabase` are unchanged; the migration tests cover the registry (`HubGroupRegistryTest`, instrumented test `HubGroupsDataStoreTest`).

### Keys: the group suffix on machine ids

Android's take on plan section 4 ("session keys must tell groups apart"), decided by the maintainer 2026-11-02:

- For the **primary group** (the group migrated from the old single pairing, or the first group paired), machine ids, session keys, routes, deep links and notifications are byte-for-byte what they are now. **Every other group's** machine ids carry a stable `~<group id>` in the merged list (`GroupKeys`, e.g. `macbook~g0a1b2c3d`), so a session key becomes "group + machine + session". Only valid group ids are recognized as suffixes; the hub's own collision suffix `@<hub>` is part of the machine id and is kept as is.
- The suffix **does not follow the selected group**: switching the group used for Persona / Inbox changes no key, so open pages, the back stack, notification deep links, drafts, pins and caches never get crossed. When the primary group is disconnected, the other groups stay as they are and no new primary is promoted; once all are disconnected, the next group paired becomes the primary again.
- Interaction ids (things for you to decide) are chosen by each hub, and two hubs may use the same one (the fixtures of two mock hubs are identical, and the list once crashed outright on duplicate LazyColumn keys): interaction ids in non-primary groups carry the same suffix, stripped when answering; `interaction_resolved`, `interaction_id` in `notify`, and the interaction ids and the machine part of entry ids (`machine/session`) in fleet entries change together.
- Each group's own repositories, database and transport use only unsuffixed ids. The facades (`GroupNames`, `MergedServices.kt`, `GroupTranslation.kt`) add the suffix when passing data up to lists and strip it when handing calls to that group. This way the signatures of `Session` / `Interaction` / `SessionKey` / `ChatActions` don't change, and feature pages, routes, deep links and notifications barely need to change.
- Trade-off: unlike iOS, no field was added to `SessionKey`. That would require every place that builds a key from a route, deep link or notification to carry the group, and missing one would cross groups; the suffix encodes the group in the machine id, so it's never lost wherever it travels. The cost is that the facades translate interface by interface (lists, events, preference paths, draft keys, results of creating sessions…), and new interfaces carrying a machine id must be added to `GroupNames`.

### Connections and switching routes

- One `OkHttpHubConnection` per group. The first paired hub in the group is its own hub; the other paired hubs and hubs discovered through linking are `members`: their candidate addresses come after its own, all through the existing route selection rules (strict order, 1-second steps, the 5-minute recheck only moves forward, `HubRouteSelector`). When the connected hub drops, the next round of route selection lands on another hub in the group; when it recovers, the recheck moves the connection back.
- Each candidate is pinned to its own hub's fingerprint and its hub_id is checked; members' fingerprints come from the authenticated hello (trusted), and another hub's certificate in the group can't impersonate this one (checked by host:port during the handshake; OkHttp doesn't provide `handshake.peerCertificates` for self-signed certificates). Requests and the WebSocket use the credentials of the hub on the current route: paired hubs use their own, hubs discovered through linking borrow the group credentials (plan 2.6, "credentials are shared within a group").
- When a hub rejects its own credentials (401), only that hub is removed from the group (its pairing is deleted from the registry and the group reopened); the other hubs keep working; only when none are left is the group invalid. A hub that only rejects borrowed group credentials is just no longer tried, and nothing is deleted. When the last group becomes invalid, it's the same as with a single pairing before: all data is cleared and the state is "pairing invalid".
- Different groups have one connection each, and these are what the foreground service keeps open. Disconnecting a group closes only its connection and clears only its data; other groups aren't affected. The exception is the ungrouped "opened hub files" cache (`cache/files-audio`, `cache/files-share`, `cache/shared`), which is deleted entirely when any group is disconnected (and on unpairing) (#4990, `clearOpenedHubFiles`); other groups fetch again when they need it.

### UI
