- Differences from iOS: this page cannot access the chat's last item, so it cannot distinguish thinking from working; any running turn shows working. The needs-you ring uses the Android design system's needs-you color (orange), not iOS red. Mochi keeps its App icon colors in every style. iOS adds a small pale-red dot at the bottom-right of the face when the Inbox has items; Android's Inbox is a separate bottom tab with its own indicator, so the face doesn't carry one.
- Selection: Settings › Hub › Avatar (`PersonaSettings` in `feature/settings`) shows Mochi and Milk Frog on an unextended hub, plus any valid installed custom packs. `LocalPersonaDesign` passes the default drawing to every `PersonaFace`; a selected pack supplies its cached images. `AppStyleStore.personaId` caches the choice; retired local IDs normalize to `persona`, and unknown or unavailable pack IDs display Mochi. Retired IDs remain reserved in `PersonaManifest`, so extensions cannot reclaim them. The native Compose picker regression renders all three styles in light / dark with real bundled frog assets, selects the frog, verifies download and hub sync, then returns to Mochi.
- Hub avatar packs (`docs/apple/persona.md` ("Pack format"), same rules as iOS `PersonaStore`): `PersonaStore` in `core/designsystem`, one per device, not tied to the hub.
  - List: fetch `GET /api/v1/personas` (`HttpPersonaPacks` in `core/hub`) each time the hub connects and the avatar page opens, store in `files/personas/index.json`, and show that first on the next launch. The phone checks the hub's validation rules again (`PersonaManifest.problem`); an old hub without this endpoint says "This hub doesn't support avatars yet: update the hub and check again".
  - Download: the whole pack is downloaded only when selected, first to `files/personas/<id>/.<rev>.part`, then renamed as a whole to `<rev>` once complete, so a partial pack is never used; unselected packs download just an avatar thumbnail for the card. A changed rev downloads a new version; the old one stays in use until the new download finishes. Unused old revs and thumbnails are deleted after refresh. Rev must be the hub's 16 hex digits (used as a directory name). If the selection changes during download, the latest choice wins.
  - A selected pack already on the phone is displayed straight from disk without waiting for the hub (including offline startup); if it disappears from the hub, the default Mochi is used but the selection (`AppStyleStore.personaId`) is kept, and restored when the pack returns.
  - Playback: Android only decodes the first PNG frame, so `decodeApng` splits APNG itself: each frame's data is packaged as a separate PNG for `BitmapFactory`, then composited on the canvas using its offset, dispose and blend (APNGs from the generation script have a full first frame, then partial frames). Only currently used states are decoded, with at most 6 cached; frames use file durations, falling back to `fps` when missing. With "Reduce motion" or battery saver, playback stays on frame 0. State colors are drawn into frames, so the App adds no ring.
  - **New from photo** (#2407, `AvatarUpload.kt` in `feature/settings`; `docs/apple/persona.md` "Uploading an image"): with `look_uploads` in the hub's features (`PersonaStore.canUpload`; not in demo mode), it ends the avatar list, with a line under it on how to make and delete them.
    - It opens the system photo picker (no permission). `decodeAvatarImage` decodes the image upright, in sRGB, at most 4096 px a side (`ImageDecoder` applies the EXIF orientation and takes a GIF's first frame). An image under 64 px a side is refused here.
    - The crop sheet shows the picture under a square with a circle: drag to move, pinch to zoom, and TalkBack zoom actions (`AvatarCrop`: never under 64 px of the image or past 8×). It also has a name (required, 40 code points) and a description (optional, 80).
    - **Upload** sends the square at most 1024 px as PNG (JPEG above 1.5 MB without transparency) with `choose: true` (`PersonaStore.upload`). The store lists again and takes the hub's choice, so there is no PUT of its own.
    - A package that didn't come keeps the sheet open with **Try again**, which only fetches it (`PersonaStore.fetch`); the image is never sent twice. A refusal shows the hub's words; no answer shows "Couldn't reach the hub…".
  - **Deleting** an avatar made from an image (`uploaded` in `GET /api/v1/looks`, or `source.kind` `upload`): its row has a trash button and a TalkBack action, and one confirmation names it.
    - `DELETE /api/v1/looks/uploads/{id}`; a 404 counts as deleted. When it was chosen here, the default comes back here; the hub chooses it for the others. The list is read again after.
    - Its row's second line is its description, or "From your image".
  - Tests: `HttpPersonaPacksTest`, `PersonaStoreUploadTest`, `AvatarUploadTest` (crop bounds, names, PNG / JPEG, the sheet's outcomes and Try again). `AvatarUploadShotsTest` writes `avatar-upload-*` (the list, the delete confirmation, the crop sheet, made but not downloaded) in three styles × light / dark into `build/looks-shots/`.
  - With a pack selected, the Persona top bar, chat list header, menu name and composer "Ask xxx…" switch to the pack's `name` (`LocalPersonaName`); product entries such as the bottom tab and launcher shortcuts remain Persona.
  - Pack `colors` are read into `PersonaManifest.colors`; selecting **Match avatar** for theme color uses its `accent`, `bubble`, `bubble_text` (see "Appearance: theme color and light / dark"); `primary` is not used yet.
- Tests: `ApngTest` (partial frames composited by dispose / blend, duration fallback to fps, single-frame PNG, non-PNG), `PersonaManifestTest` (hub-list parsing, packs the hub rejects are also rejected by the phone, choosing avatar size, missing states use idle). Device test: put the demo pack from `make_persona.py demo` into the hub's personas directory and select it on the avatar page.
- Screenshot inspection: the **Avatar** section of the component catalog (debug package `--es debug.openPage catalog`) draws one row per avatar with five states side by side.

## Appearance: theme color and light / dark (#3048)

Aligned with iOS `ThemeColor` / `ColorSchemeChoice`. These are new options; at default values the UI remains pixel-identical.

- Storage: `AppStyleStore.color` (`style` / `persona` / `blue` / `coral` / `lavender` / `teal` / `lark`) and `AppStyleStore.scheme` (`system` / `light` / `dark`), in the same preferences as interface style, not tied to the hub.
- Theme colors (`ThemeColors.kt` in `core/designsystem`): presets and avatar packages provide an accent and optional outgoing-bubble colors. **Match avatar** appears when the pack has `accent` or `bubble` in `colors`; a bubble-only pack retains the style's accent. The selection is applied through `LocalThemeAccent` / `PersonaTheme`, after extension role themes. In Glass a preset is used as it is and a package's accent is held to iOS's floors (white words on it 3.5:1, a link 3:1; `ThemeColors.iosMeeting`), as on iOS; in Brief and Board an accent used as text is moved toward black (light) or white (dark) until it reaches 4.5:1. `ThemeColorsTest` covers every preset × style × light/dark. Since #6184, cards and message bubbles draw neutral surfaces rather than themed fills; the stored bubble colors remain compatible, but no longer recolor `UserBubbleFrame` or its words. Brief buttons stay ink-colored. Selecting a preset clears the extension theme; extension themes have their own section.
- Light / dark and theme color apply to every App window: `AppStyleStore` writes the scheme to `AppAppearance.scheme`; `PersonaApp` writes the theme-color-derived accent to `AppAppearance.accent`; `PersonaTheme` reads both by default (`appDarkTheme()`), so windows not going through `MainActivity`—share page, floating capsule, "Approve first" dialog, health permissions page—follow too (`AppAppearanceTest`). Android 12+ also sets the App's night mode with `UiModeManager.setApplicationNightMode`, so WebView pages and system dialogs change too; on Android 11 they still follow the system. `MainActivity` additionally uses `AppearanceOverride` to change Compose's Configuration; screenshot debug argument `debug.theme` still takes precedence.
- Screenshots: `SettingsParityShotsTest` in `feature/settings` (`build/parity-shots/`).

## APK update discovery

The paired app checks its hub's APK feed on launch and when returning to the foreground. Automatic attempts, including failed ones, are limited to one per six hours per hub; Settings › Check for updates bypasses that interval. No resident background timer is required or promised. Demo mode and onboarding do not check automatically.

A nonmodal notice offers Download and install. Closing it remembers that version for that hub; the update remains available in Settings, and a later version can show a new notice. Last-check time and discovered metadata survive process restarts. Switching hubs uses separate state and rejects results or downloads whose source changed. Automatic failures stay quiet; manual checks and download failures provide retry feedback without blocking other app features.

Checks and installation preparation share a nonqueuing gate. Downloading continues if its notice is closed or Settings is left. APKs use the existing authenticated, pinned hub transport and are verified by SHA-256, package name and increasing version code. One verified APK is retained in app cache for retry after cancelled installation; pending installer sessions prevent another download, and abandoned uncommitted sessions are cleaned before retry. Installation always opens the Android system confirmation and may require allowing installs from this app first. Publishing an APK does not install it on a phone.

Regression coverage: `AppUpdateStateTest`, `AppUpdateNoticeShotsTest` (including lifecycle events and all three styles in light/dark), `AppUpdateTransportTest` and `AppInstallerTest`.

## Other Settings parity items (#3048)

- Model providers (Settings › <name>'s settings › Model providers, `ProvidersScreen.kt` / `ProvidersState.kt`, aligned with iOS `ModelLaunchersView`): `GET /api/v1/launchers`, grouped into **Managed by Persona / From ~/.zshrc / Hidden** (collapsed by default, expanded during search); search matches name, model, gateway and computer name. Long-press a row: hide / restore ~/.zshrc entries (no confirmation), delete Persona entries (confirmation, deletes the key too). Top-right + chooses **Add provider** (reuses `NewProviderPage`) or **Import from ~/.zshrc** (choose computer → preview merged providers, all selected by default → import; the hub reads keys from that computer itself; if its personad is too old, 409 `node_outdated`, it says to redeploy from Settings › Computers). The detail page (`ModelProviderRoute`) lists settings, availability testing (tests ~/.zshrc providers on all their online computers at once, with independent results per row; Persona providers and direct APIs are tested on the hub), raw function text, hide / restore / delete. Interface: `ProviderSetup` (`HttpProviderSetup`). Machine ids belong to the hub itself; with multiple groups, names are matched using `GroupKeys.raw`.
- Storage (Settings › This device › Storage, `StorageScreen.kt`, aligned with iOS `StorageView`): measures `cacheDir` and the App's directories as **Pictures in conversations / Lark pictures and files / Web pages / Other caches / App data**, with per-category or clear-all actions. The active `items.db`, unsent attachments (`chat-uploads`, `chat-captures`) and files open elsewhere (update APK being installed, `persona-update-*.apk`; open PDFs, `files-pdf`; shared files, `shared` / `files-share`) count as App data and are not deleted. Files are removed through data-layer `CacheClearing`, holding the cache lock, skipping active readers (`readPinned`) and in-progress `.part` / `.tmp` downloads, so deletion does not race `BudgetFileCache` writes. Web cache is cleared only through `WebView.clearCache`, not direct file deletion.
- Signed-in Macs and muting the phone (aligned with iOS `DesktopPresenceRow` / `DesktopPresenceSheet`): the hub's `desktops` event (`DesktopsEvent`) is stored separately per hub group (`GroupDesktops`; the event itself carries no group); Settings shows only the current group's, clears it when that group disconnects, and sends sign-out to the current group's hub (`GroupDesktopsTest`). When a desktop is online, the Settings home's **This device** group gains **Persona is signed in on a Mac** (iOS places it above the Agents list; Android's Agents page is unchanged here). Notifications gains **When a computer is online › Mute phone** (`NotifyPrefs.mute_while_desktop`, hidden if an old hub lacks the field). **Signed-in Macs** lists login time and sign-out per Mac (revoking that device's credentials).
- Quiet hours (#1863, aligned with iOS `NotificationSettingsView`): Settings › Notifications has **Quiet hours** after **When an agent finishes** (a switch, then **From** / **Until** on a 24-hour time picker), only when the prefs answer has `quiet`. Each change sends `quiet` alone with this phone's time zone (`DeviceSettings.saveQuietHours`, optimistic, put back if the hub says no); hours set in another zone: the footer names that zone until they are changed here. Inside the window the hub sends no "agent finished" and marks cards and reply reminders `quiet: true`; the phone posts those without a sound (`NotifyEvent.quiet`, `setSilent`).
- Notification page details (aligned with iOS `NotificationSettingsView`): if the system disables notifications, the page starts with **Notifications are off** and **System notification settings**; **When you are needed** has a subtitle and explains that notifications allow direct allow / deny; the footer says "Change sound, lock-screen display and Do Not Disturb in system settings".
- Claude Code settings › Blocked config directories (`ClaudeDirs.kt`, aligned with iOS `ClaudeDirsSection`): `GET /api/v1/machines/{id}/claude-dirs` lists the current computer's configuration directories, block / unblock (unblocking removes the rule that blocks it); rules matching no directory are listed separately and can be deleted; manual paths or wildcards use iOS's validation; `PUT …/claude-dirs/blocked` sends the complete rules.
- Assistant settings › Task notifications › Notify only in the main chat (`main_tasks_quiet`, hidden if the old hub lacks it).

## Assistant settings (Settings › Assistant)

Requested 2026-11-02: "There is no setting at all for which device runs Persona's chats, memory and the rest." Code is in `feature/assistant/…/settings/`: page `AssistantSettingsScreen`, state and requests `AssistantSettingsState`, using `config` / `updateConfig` / `launchers` from `AssistantManagement` (`GET` / `PUT /api/v1/assistant`, `GET /api/v1/machines/{id}/launchers`) and the existing memory API.

- Entry: **Assistant** in the Settings list's AI section (#3048, aligned with iOS `SettingsPage.assistant`; `AssistantSettingsRoute`, registered in `assistantEntries`, opens in the Settings list's right pane on wide windows). #2196 removed the row formerly labeled Persona (then treated as a duplicate "Meet Persona" entry); this row is now Assistant, below model configuration, like iOS. **Details** in the Persona tab title menu, the top-right **Assistant profile** button and **Launcher** in the drawer open the same page in place in the Persona tab (the drawer's old profile page moved to Settings like iOS's "Meet Persona", #2003). Demo mode has no page and shows an explanation.
- Runs on: machine (online state; `kind: local` says "the hub machine"), working directory (shortened using that machine's home to `~/.persona/assistant`). Launcher and model are not here: both are selected in Settings › Model configuration › Main chat (#1291), which also shows provider offline / missing states.
- Changing machines: confirm first; the dialog states "Memory and chat records stay on the original computer; they won't move with you", with an extra warning when the target is offline. On confirmation, `PUT {"machine_id":…,"main":"-"}`. The main chat is a transcript on the old machine and cannot be resumed on the new one, so it is cleared too; the next message starts a new main chat on the new machine. Old chats and memory stay there and are still present if you switch back (the former main chat becomes a regular chat). Then refresh Persona's chat list and reload launchers and memory.
  - The outbox stores the main chat's bound session locally (`main_conversation` table). After switching, call `MainConversationSender.mainChanged()` to ask the hub: if the hub no longer recognizes it (machine changed, another device created a main chat), forget it and resolve or create the target of the next main-chat message according to the hub. Otherwise messages keep going to the old machine's session, now a regular chat (user feedback 2026-11-05). Also check once when the hub connection catches up (`OutboxMessageSender.onReady`) and when opening the Persona tab, to repair phones already bound incorrectly; failure to reach the hub or timeout keeps the original binding. Changing machines in place in the Persona tab returns to the new-chat page.
- Skills (#2755, aligned with iOS `SkillsPage`): **Runs on** gains a **Skills** row, opening a read-only list in place. Each opening reads `GET /api/v1/skills` (`machine`, and name / description / source in `skills[]`), listing name, description and "Built-in / Mine". No skills shows an empty state (put them in that machine's `~/.persona/skills/`); failure shows the hub's reason (409, no assistant machine; 502, node did not reply; via `AssistantRefused`), or the page's own wording if there is none. A failed refresh keeps the existing list. Unit tests: `AssistantManagementTest` in `core:hub`, `AssistantSettingsStateTest` in `feature:assistant`.
- Data locations: chat records and memory are on the assistant's machine; About me, scheduled tasks and settings are on the hub; knowledge base is on the hub machine (`~/.persona/data`).
- Learning about you: **About me** edits `notes`. Before any App save it opens the default from `assistant.notes` (the manager's `manager.json`) and explains that saving makes this page authoritative; clearing also saves (telling the assistant to write nothing). Memory lists `memory/*.md`, newest first; titles come first from the `MEMORY.md` index line, then front-matter name; types are user / feedback / project / reference. Open the body, edit the whole file (including front matter), or delete (the hub also deletes its index line). `MEMORY.md` is read-only.
- Time zone: defaults to "Match phone", like iOS. Opening the Persona tab or this page sends `PUT timezone` if the hub's `timezone` differs from the phone's. Turn it off to choose from a list (current and phone zones first; searchable by city, Chinese name and UTC offset), with no further automatic sync. The choice is kept on the hub (#1855, `timezone_picked`): turning Match phone off sends the zone in effect with `timezone_picked: true`, picking a zone sends it with `true`, turning it back on sends the phone's zone with `false`; a write the hub could not take puts the switch back. The switch shows the hub's value when it has one (a zone picked on another phone stays and the switch is off here too), kept in SharedPreferences `persona-assistant` for hubs from before; a zone picked here before the hub kept it is sent to the hub once with `true`. While the hub says picked, no device (the Apple apps, another phone on Match phone, apps from before) changes the zone.
- Proactive work: a section below **Runs on** has **Scheduled tasks, follow-ups and run history**, opening `AssistantProactiveScreen` in place (back returns here); see "Proactive work" below. **Scheduled tasks** in the Persona drawer opens the same page in place in that tab; old `AssistantProfile` is deleted.
- Screenshots: `debug.assistantSettings` jumps to a portion of the page (notes, memory, memory-file, memory-edit, memory-delete, machine, launcher, timezone, move for machine-change confirmation, proactive for proactive work, skills for Skills), passed with `debug.openLink 'persona://open?target=settings&page=assistant'`. The mock hub supports `PUT /api/v1/assistant` and `PUT` / `DELETE /api/v1/assistant/memory/{name}`; like a real hub, new main chats start on the assistant's machine and are recorded as `main`; `GET /mock/sent_to` lists each message's target machine and session. `MOCK_MACHINES=1` adds an offline machine for inspecting offline states.
- Existing-chat launcher and model (#4663, `ConversationLauncher.kt`): an assistant conversation that can send has a chip above its composer. It reads `GET /api/v1/machines/{id}/launchers?purpose=assistant`, never the generic New Agent list or a client-side eligibility filter, and reads the effective launcher's `/models` when the sheet opens. **Use the default** clears the conversation's pick; otherwise the selected launcher/model go with every following message, including outbox retries and process restoration, until changed. The sheet says this lifetime and that Assistant settings stay unchanged. Missing model routes, loading and failures leave disabled status rows. The old Chinese `默认` placeholder in a launcher's model field is treated as no model in every UI language. Screenshot test `ConversationLauncherShotsTest`.
- New-chat launcher (#2976 G049, iOS `StarterComposer`, web: the composer's model pill, `AssistantModelPill`, #5033): a launcher chip above the composer on the Persona new-chat page defaults to "Default: <launcher from settings>"; tapping lists Default and the launchers returned by `GET /api/v1/machines/{id}/launchers?purpose=assistant`, with model names and no client-side agent-kind filter; loading, failure and empty each get a line. Like web, every opening reloads: always reload the default launcher to reflect settings changes, and retry failed lists on the next opening. During sending the chip ignores taps but keeps its appearance and size (a `JChip` without a tap handler becomes a shorter tag). Selected, it says "<name> · Just this once": this message does not enter the main chat; first create a new chat with this launcher (`POST /api/v1/assistant/conversations {"prompt":"","main":<when no main chat exists>,"launcher":…}`), then send the message through the outbox and open it; the chip returns to default. The default launcher still changes in Settings › Model configuration › Main chat. If chat creation succeeds but sending fails, retry sends to the same new chat instead of creating another: `StarterStart` records which launcher created which chat (`rememberSaveable`, survives rotation), reused only for the same launcher (`starterTarget`); choosing a different launcher or Default clears it, as does changing hubs. Without a choice, the first message on **New chat**'s page starts a new chat on the default launcher (next item); on the page that starts the main chat (there is none yet), it goes to the main chat. No chip in demo mode. Code `StarterLauncher.kt`, screenshot test `StarterLauncherShotsTest`.
- **New chat** (top bar, drawer and wide sidebar; #2804): opens a blank page and starts nothing on the hub yet. That page has no "What you send goes to the main chat" note: the first message sent there starts the new chat (`startConversation`, or the picked launcher's path above), then goes to it through the outbox, and the chat opens. It never goes to the main chat. If the chat was started but the message failed, the retry sends to that same chat. Back on the blank page returns to the conversation New chat was tapped on (the main chat when it was tapped on none) and leaves nothing on the hub; opening another conversation or switching manager leaves the page as well. The top bar's New chat is off on the page itself, as iOS's. (New chat used to start an empty working chat at once, which stayed in Recent even when nothing was sent.) With no main chat yet, New chat's page is the one that starts it: its first message goes to the main chat, with the note. Test `NewConversationStartTest`.
- **No home page** (#4339 C11, alignment decision 10, as iOS): New chat's is the only blank page. Back from a conversation embedded in the Assistant home opens the main chat (a notification's or a link's too); Back on the main chat is the shell's and leaves the app. A conversation opened from an inbox item is instead pushed above that item and Back returns to it. Archiving or ending the open embedded conversation opens the main chat as well. The Assistant tab's menu has **Back to main chat** off the main chat in place of the old **Back to home**, and the sidebar's **Back to home** row (Brief and Board, and the page's own sidebar outside the shell) is gone. Without a main chat (none started yet, or one just replaced by **New main chat**), the blank page that starts it takes the place of the main chat. What the home showed is on New chat's page (the greeting, What needs you now, the suggestions and the online devices); a session that needs the user is also on Agents (its tab's badge and Needs You) and in the Inbox. Pure rule `assistantBack`; tests `AssistantBackTest`, `AssistantArchiveNavigationTest`; screenshots `NoHomePageShotsTest` (`build/no-home-shots/`, three styles × light / dark, the menu on Glass).
- **A new chat's first message** (#4339 C18, iOS `FirstMessagePending` and `FailedStart`): sent from New chat's page (or with a launcher picked for it), it leaves the composer for your bubble at the page's foot, with "Sending…" under it while the conversation starts; the conversation then shows it from the outbox with "Persona is getting ready…" (`gettingReady`, Persona's name) until its turn's work line comes, and its composer reads "Ask Persona…". A start that failed keeps the message as a failed bubble: "Not sent: <why>", **Resend** (the same conversation when it had started, with the same message id) and **Delete** (Undo puts it back). The page takes no other first message meanwhile, and the failure is kept on the device per assistant until it is sent or deleted (`FailedStarts`). The blank page with no main chat yet still sends to the main chat's queue as before. Tests `FirstMessageTest`, `NewConversationStartTest`; screenshots `AssistantDetailsShotsTest`.
- Unit tests: `AssistantSettingsStateTest` (config read/write, machine-change warning and clearing main chat, notifying outbox before refresh after switching, missing launcher, phone-matched timezone, a picked zone kept on the hub and left alone by other devices, About me, memory read / edit / delete), `AssistantConfigTest` (`PUT` sends only changed fields, including empty About me and `timezone_picked`).

## Settings › Managers ([managers.md](../assistant/managers.md))

- **Where**: Settings › Hub, after Hub version (hidden in demo mode); the roster the sidebar's switcher opens has **Manage assistants…** to it too. One row per manager: name and Default badge, status with its dot (online / offline · last synced … / installing · code expires in n minutes / code expired), and where it runs (host · OS · deployment, worded as on the web). A row opens the manager page (`ManagerRoute`, in the detail pane on wide screens): where it runs, version, connected since / last synced, Rename, Make default, Install elsewhere (New install code for a pending install, without the warning) and Delete (Cancel install while installing; the hub's own and the default manager can't be deleted, with the reason under the button). New manager and Install elsewhere open the install code sheet: the code and its countdown, New install code once it expires, and when the hub sends `commands`, Install as a service and Use Docker, each with Copy. An old hub without `/api/v1/managers` (404) gets a page saying to upgrade the hub.
- **Code**: `HubManagers` (API and models) and `ManagersEvent` in `core:contracts`; `HttpHubManagers` in `core:hub`. The app wires it to the selected hub in `MachineManagementModule`; `client_hello`'s `features` carries `managers`, and the hub's `{"type":"managers"}` makes an open page reload the list. Text and model are in `feature/settings/ManagersState.kt` (`ManagerText`, `ManagersModel`), the screens in `ManagersScreen.kt`.
- **Tests**: `HttpHubManagersTest` (MockWebServer checks paths, bodies, error codes and the event's decoding) and `ManagersStateTest` (where it runs, status line, countdown, error wording, model). Screenshots: `./gradlew :feature:settings:testDebugUnitTest --tests '*ManagersShotsTest*'` renders the settings row, list, manager pages (another computer's, the hub's own, installing), the code sheet with commands and expired, and an old hub, in three styles × light and dark, into `build/managers-shots/`. `android.sh shots` also shoots the mock hub's list and two manager pages (`debug.openPage managers` / `manager:<id>`).
- **Known**: in Robolectric a text field in a dialog keeps Compose from going idle, so the UI tests don't drive the name dialog; creating a manager is covered by the model's unit tests.

## Settings › Connectors › Records sent as you ([connectors.md](../extensions/connectors.md) "Sent as the user")

- **Where**: Settings › Connectors, a single-row entry after the connector list (#2521), opening a secondary page (`SettingsPage.Sent`, page name `connectors/sent`; hidden in demo mode, and where the services have no `sent`). The Settings home shows only the Connectors category, not the ledger. The old `sent` page name and `persona://open?target=settings&page=sent` still open this page; Back reaches Connectors. Hub links `/settings?tab=sent`, `/settings/sent` and `/settings?tab=connectors&connector=-sent` resolve to the same native destination, and restored serialized `SettingsPage.Sent` keys retain their identity with a connector parent inserted. It lists what Persona wrote in your name through the connectors (`GET /api/v1/connectors/sent`), newest first, as the web page (`web/src/views/settings/SentSettings.tsx`) and iOS (`SentView.swift`) do; the wording is theirs, in sentence case as Android has it (`docs/protocol/i18n.md`). A row has the connector, where it went (mail the recipients and subject, GitHub `repo#number`, Lark the conversation else method and path, others the common fields it named, at most three, else a dash), how long ago (as the web's `ago`: minutes, hours, days by the local calendar up to six, then the date), the first two lines of the text (the one-line summary when there is none) and a badge for how the gate let it through: **By a rule**, **Always allowed in this chat**, **Confirmed by you on a card**, and in orange (the caution tone) **Answered by the assistant**, **Answered by <name>** and **Sent by the extension itself · <setting>**; a kind it does not know is shown as it is. Under it, **Open chat** (the app's `ChatRoute` by the row's machine and session ids, so the assistant's chats open in Persona and an agent's in Agents; named "Open chat: <connector> <target>" for TalkBack), **Extension-reported** with the extension's id for a row an extension recorded, or **No chat**. A row opens a sheet with the time, the action, every field of the target (the destination fields first, then the rest by name), how it was let through (the rule, the card's id), the chat, the message id, when it was recalled, and the whole text with **Copy text**, and a note when the text is exactly 4000 characters (the most the ledger keeps). **Recall…** (#3100) is at the end of the sheet only when the row's `can_recall` is true: a `JDialog` asks first, then `HubSent.recall` posts `{"confirm": true}` with a 70-second read timeout (the hub can take about 40 seconds). `SentModel.recall` drops first-page reads already on their way, puts the row the hub answers with in the list (the open sheet follows it by id) and reads the first page again whether it worked or not; the refusal is said under the button in plain words (`SentText.recallProblem`), with the hub's own message for `recall_refused` and codes it doesn't know. A recalled row has a **Recalled** tag. **Load earlier** asks with `before=next` and adds rows by id; the first page is read when the page opens and on pull to refresh, keeping the older pages it connects to. The connector chips are Lark, the connectors the loaded rows show and the chosen one; choosing it again is All. States: nothing recorded yet (what is and is not recorded, 90 days), nothing under a chip (**Show all**), 503 `no_database`, 403 (a device without full access) and a 404 with no code (an older hub: update it), each said before the usual "Couldn't load: …" with **Retry** (only that failure is red, as on the web and iOS; the other three read plainly). Not built: per-chat rules, other filters, opening the original message.
- **Late answers** (as iOS): an answer for an earlier chip is dropped; an older page read for a cursor the list has moved on from, or under an earlier chip, is dropped; for the first page the newest answer taken wins, so a cancelled newer read doesn't strand a good older one.
- **Code**: `HubSent` (the call), `SentPage` / `SentItem` and their lenient reading (`SentWire`: a field of the wrong type is empty, an element with no whole-number id is skipped, JSON that is not an object is an empty page) in `core:contracts`; `HttpHubSent` in `core:hub`. `MachineManagementModule` wires it to the selected group's hub through `SelectedGroupSent`, which gives the rows' machine ids in the group's form (`SentPage.inGroup`, GroupKeys), so a chat opens in a hub group too. Words and rules are in `feature/settings/SentState.kt` (`SentText`, `SentModel`), the page in `SentScreen.kt`, the strings in `strings_sent.xml`. `debug.openPage connectors` opens the connector list; `debug.openPage connectors/sent` and the legacy `debug.openPage sent` open the ledger in a debug build.
- **Tests**: `HttpHubSentTest` (MockWebServer: the query, escaping, lossy rows, empty and non-object pages, the hub's refusals, the recall request) and `SentWireTest` in `core`; `SentStateTest` (the words and rules, merge and refresh, chips, the hub's refusals, the model over a fake hub with every late-answer case, ported from iOS's `SentModelTests`, the cancelled-read and double Load earlier cases, and recall: the row replaced, a refusal's words, the list read again) and `SelectedGroupSentTest` in `app` (a row's chat opens by the selected group's machine id, from the hub's own id through `SettingsScreen`, for a non-primary group and the primary one). `./gradlew :feature:settings:testDebugUnitTest --tests '*SentShotsTest*'` renders the row among its neighbours, the list, a send's details (also a cut text, Recall offered after a refusal, a recalled row), the empty, empty-under-a-chip and no-database states in three styles × light and dark into `build/sent-shots/`, and drives the page over a fake hub (a row and its chat, also through `SettingsScreen`'s route, details with no way to open a chat, the chips, Load earlier, the refusals with only a failure red, Retry, the settings row's place).

- **Navigation acceptance**: `SentNavigationTest` checks canonical and legacy app links, the old serialized key, parent restoration and deduplication; `HubLinksTest` checks both old web URLs and the canonical web URL. `SentShotsTest` drives the real Settings → Connectors → ledger path and Back in all three styles × light/dark, and retains the ledger's existing behavior tests. For actual emulator screenshots, install an isolated debug APK, run a mock hub with `MOCK_SENT=1`, forward its port, then run `python3 scripts/test/android_sent_e2e.py --adb <adb path> --serial <owned emulator> --mock <forwarded mock address> --out <screenshot directory>`. It tests the hierarchy and cold/warm legacy links, saves Settings/Connectors/ledger images, and ends with `ANDROID_SENT_E2E PASS` or a failure verdict. JVM-native images supplement, not replace, emulator acceptance.

Verification image removed; see the associated PR.

## Several managers on the pages ([managers.md](../assistant/managers.md) ("Clients"))

Managers are **assistants** to the user (助手, アシスタント; the code and the docs keep "manager"). Every page shows the **current assistant**, picked in the sidebar; with one assistant the pages are as before and the sidebar still has its switcher.

- **Roster**: `ManagerRosterSource` (app) reads `GET /api/v1/managers` of the selected hub on connect and on `{"type":"managers"}`, and gives it to the pages as `LocalManagerRoster` (with `LocalManagerGroup`, the group it is of). `ManagerRoster` (`core:contracts` `HubManagers.kt`) has `installed` (default first), `isMulti`, `shownUnder(manager)` (the installed manager a thing is listed under: its own, else the default) and `waiting(cards, sessions)` (the switcher's badges). A new hub is set first (`AssistantManagerChoice.atHub`), so what its lists note before the roster lands is kept under it.
- **Switcher** (`feature/settings/AssistantSwitcher.kt`, drawn at the sidebar's head through `AssistantSwitcherSlot`; design system `Managers.kt`): the head shows the current assistant's monogram (`JManagerAvatar`, its presence dot at the corner: green online, grey offline, orange installing), its name with a caret and "Online · <computer>" / "Offline · last synced …" (`JAssistantSwitcherHeader`), in all three styles and also with one assistant. A tap opens the roster in a menu under it (`JMenu` with the rows in its content slot: as wide as the sidebar, closed by a tap outside, scrolled when it does not fit, at large text sizes too): one row per assistant (`JAssistantSwitcherRow`: monogram with its dot, name, Default only with more than one installed assistant, the same status line, a tick on the current one, else its badge; an offline one's row faded as a whole), the installed ones first (default first), then the ones still installing ("Installing · code expires in n minutes" / "code expired"), then **New assistant** (name, then the install code sheet) and **Manage assistants…** (Settings › Assistants). Picking an installed one makes it current, offline or not; picking one still installing opens its install commands (a new code, as on its page). The monogram is the name's first character on a colour every client shares (`managerHue`): the default's is the AI purple, another's is the sum of its id's UTF-8 bytes mod 5 of blue, green, orange, pink, indigo. **Badge** = the agents waiting for the user at that assistant (`ManagerRoster.waiting`, through `ShellCounts` in the shell): its sessions that are neither quiet nor muted and have a pending card or are in Needs you, each once however many cards it has, a card on no listed session once by itself (counted from the pushes the app already has), plus its inbox's Needs you from the roster's `inbox_needs_you` (absent: 0); the same rule as every client (`docs/assistant/managers.md` "Clients"). When another assistant has something waiting, the sidebar button on the home pages carries a dot. The choice is `AssistantManagerChoice` (process-wide), kept per hub on this device (`AssistantSelectionPreferences`) and back to the default once the roster no longer lists it. TalkBack reads the head as one ("name, status", and that something waits at another assistant while its dot shows; the top bar's sidebar button says the same) and each row as one, its badge as "n waiting for you". Switching lets go of the Agents page's computer filter when the new assistant has no such computer (the shell's saved computer follows), of the inbox's selection, and reads the new assistant's Done history when Done is open; a card opened from a notification reads its session and computer from every manager's lists. Demo mode, and a hub without a roster, keep the sidebar's head as it was. The hub client (`HubHttpClient`, `isCurrentAssistantPath`) adds `X-Persona-Manager` for a current assistant other than the default to `/api/v1/assistant…` (not `?space=`), the knowledge base (`kb…`, `kb-search`) and a new to-do (`POST todos`), except under a conversation (`assistant/conversations/<id>…`): those go by the conversation's own manager, noted when a list showed it, since a named manager overrides the hub's ownership lookup. The conversations listed are the chosen manager's (sessions carry their computer's `manager`); live updates that name a conversation (feed items, cards, plans) go by it in `TasksStore`, whichever manager's they are (another manager's carry `manager`, which the models leave aside), so another manager's conversation open elsewhere stays live; the assistant repository caches each manager's conversations apart.
- **An offline manager's computers**: the card says "Its manager “<name>” is offline. It comes back online once the manager is back." (`offlineManagerOf`, `machines_manager_offline`) instead of the hub's words, and the page has no Reconnect row (hidden, not disabled). Only a machine of the group the roster is of counts (`GroupKeys.namespaceOf(id) == LocalManagerGroup`), so another group's computer with the same manager id keeps the hub's words.
- **Offline**: its row in the switcher's menu is faded and its dot grey; on its pages the composer is off and reads "<name> · last synced …", the empty page says it can't take messages now, nothing is sent or queued; an open conversation is read-only. The hub's merged lists leave it out, so the inbox and to-dos, and Agents and Computers when the hub keeps none of its agents or computers, show `JAssistantOffline` (design system `ManagerOffline.kt`: its monogram with the offline mark, faded as in the switcher, "<name> is offline" and "Last synced <time>. This updates when it's back online.") in place of their empty state or first-run hint (whose New to-do and Add computer buttons go with it); the inbox then drops its "All caught up" line. Only with several assistants (`CurrentAssistant.offline`); one assistant, or an online one, reads as before. A conversation opened from elsewhere (a notification, the Agents page) goes by its own manager: when that one is offline its composer is off with "<name> · last synced …" (`managerOfflineNote`), whichever manager the switcher shows.
- **Messages keep their manager**: the main queue (`AssistantMainConversationResolver`) always asks the default (`?manager=`, empty); a message for another manager goes to that manager's main conversation (`mainConversationOf`, asked of it alone with `?manager=<id>`) and through the outbox to that session, so retries stay there. If it can't be found the send fails and the words stay. **Hand to AI** → Persona, and Summarize / Draft reply on an item's page (`askPersona`), do the same for another manager's inbox item: it goes to that manager's main conversation (`RealInboxRepository`'s `mainConversationOf`), not the default's, naming the item by its id on the hub.
- **Pages show the current assistant** (`CurrentAssistant`, `currentAssistant()`): the assistant page, Agents (its sessions and computers, and their cards: `AgentsViewModel.show`), Computers (the list; one computer's page opens whichever assistant it belongs to), the inbox (`InboxSnapshot.ofAssistant`; Done's history, Muted and Rules are read from it alone), to-dos (each carries the `manager` its merged list stamped, `Todo.manager`; requests for one name it, and a new one is the current assistant's), scheduled tasks and the knowledge base. The hub's lists stay every manager's and are filtered here by each thing's `manager` (none, or one the roster no longer lists: the default's), so the sidebar's counts, the tab badges and "Needs you now" are the current assistant's too, and an item of another assistant still opens from a notification or a link, by its own manager, without switching. No page has per-manager headings any more, Settings included (below). Row keys carry the manager, since the same id can be under two (a built-in extension is listed under every manager).
- **A page keeps its assistant**: an entry of the knowledge base opens with the assistant whose list showed it (`KnowledgeDetailRoute.manager`; a route restored from before keeps the one current when it opens again), and its page pins it on its own reads and saves (`ManagerPins` over `kb` and `kb-search`, which `HttpKnowledgeRepository` puts on the URL), so switching while it is open leaves it that assistant's; a question about it goes to the main queue when its assistant is the current one (the Persona page shows it), else to that assistant's main conversation, the default's included (`mainConversationOf`, then the outbox), which opens in place. The other pages about one thing already go by it: an inbox item's pages by its record's manager, a to-do's by its noted stamp, a conversation by its own manager, a computer by the hub's ownership; the assistant page's own sub-pages (scheduled tasks, its settings in place) close on a switch. **Create and link** on an agent (and a project's new to-do) makes the to-do under that agent's assistant (`AgentsViewModel.todoOwner`, a `RequestManager` on `POST todos`; `""` the default's), and the to-do sheet offers that assistant's to-dos only (`todosFor`). A plan's Start and Dismiss (`task-plans/<id>/…`, routes that name no conversation) name the manager of the conversation's own session row (`AssistantTaskHost.manager`), else the one a list registered it under (`TasksStore.planOwner`), so a restored back stack or a notification still names it. An offline notice shows only when nothing is kept: the unfiltered inbox / to-do list is empty, and Agents has no computer either. Nothing but the switcher (`AssistantManagerChoice.pick`) and the roster check (`AssistantSelectionPreferences`: restored per hub, back to the default when the roster no longer lists it) changes the current assistant.
- **Settings in three groups** (`SettingsScreen.kt`; the same groups and order on every client, `docs/assistant/managers.md` "Clients"):
  - **<name>'s settings** (`settings_group_assistant`): the head is the current assistant's monogram with its presence dot and the title (`JAssistantSettingsHeader`), and a **Switch** text button that opens the sidebar's roster (the same `AssistantSwitcher`, drawn through its `head` slot: switch, new, manage; the badges come from `LocalAssistantWaiting`, which the shell provides). With one installed assistant it shows the same way, its roster that one, New assistant and Manage assistants…, and no Default tag; an assistant still installing is listed too but does not count as another installed assistant or bring back that tag. The sidebar's head follows the same rule. Without a roster (an older hub, demo mode) there is no assistant to name: the heading is "Persona", with no Switch and no footer. Then Model providers, Models, Assistant profile, Session triage, Connectors, Extensions, Channels, Claude Code settings and Codex settings, and the footer "Only <name>'s own settings are shown. To change another assistant's, switch to it first." The extensions' slot cards and nav pages sit above the rows only while the current assistant is the default (they are the default's).
  - **Its pages read and write the current assistant alone**: for an assistant other than the default, `SettingsServices.forCurrentAssistant()` hands them providers, models, triage, connectors, channels, the messaging bot and the sent ledger through `forManager` (`ManagerScopedApis.kt`: each call carries the `RequestManager`) and extensions (with their source subscriptions) pinned with `ManagerPins`; the default's, one assistant's and an older hub's requests name none, as before (model configuration's `namingOwner` likewise). Merged lists (connectors, extensions) are filtered by `CurrentAssistant.owns`; Claude Code and Codex settings, and Model providers' Import from ~/.zshrc, list only its computers; what a page opens carries it: a model use (`ModelPurposeRoute`: Session triage's, the Lark connector's agent model), a market's channel (`ExtensionCatalogRoute`), a channel's More settings (`ExtensionRoute`, `ChannelText.settingsDestination`). The rows' values (the extensions' count, the channels') are each assistant's own. No page of the group has assistant chips or per-assistant headings. While the current assistant is offline (several assistants), each of its pages shows `JAssistantOffline` ("<name> is offline", when it last synced) instead of a list that would look empty or failed.
  - **This device** (`settings_group_device`): Interface style, Language, Open native chats by default, Notifications, Floating capsule, Keep running in background, Persona is signed in on a Mac (while one is), Translation (its model and connector stay the default's), Storage, About, Check for updates; then the update card.
  - **Hub** (`settings_group_hub`): the hub card (its connection and computers online), Hubs, Hub version, Assistants, Pairing, Avatar (the hub keeps it, `/api/v1/looks`, for every device whichever assistant is current), Devices, Diagnostics, and the footer "These two groups are the same whichever assistant is current."; then Disconnect. Inbox rules and scheduled tasks have no row of their own on Android (the inbox's Rules and the assistant's proactive page hold them), and there is no Health page.
  - **A switch with a page of the first group open** goes back: `CloseAssistantSettingsOnSwitch` (in `Root.kt`) closes everything from the first such page (`isAssistantSettingsDestination`) on the Settings sheet's stack and on each tab's (a link or a readiness card opens settings there), and a page shown in place goes back to the list (`SettingsContent`), so no page shows the new name over the old assistant's content, or sends a half-filled form to the new one.
  - **Tests**: `SettingsGroupsShotsTest` (the three groups in their order in every style, light and dark, Switch opening the roster and the group following the pick, one assistant with its Switch and roster, no roster, an offline assistant's page, a switch while a page is open, English, Japanese, the largest text size; `build/settings-groups-shots/`), `SettingsAssistantScopeTest` (the group's services name another assistant on every request, and none for the default, one assistant or an older hub), `SettingsAssistantEntriesTest` (a market's channel, a channel's More settings and the Lark agent's model open the current assistant's; the shell's stacks close its pages on a switch, not before), `SettingsNavigationTest` (what a switch closes), `ManagerScopedApisTest`, `ConnectorOwnershipShotsTest` and `ExtensionsCurrentAssistantShotsTest` (only the current assistant's, without headings), `ModelManagerScopeShotsTest` and `AssistantSettingsManagerTest` (model configuration and the profile follow a switch).
- **Item routing**: an inbox item or conversation a list showed as another manager's is noted (`AssistantManagerChoice.noteItem`, keyed by hub and path; only a picked manager's conversations, so a placeholder default is never sent); requests under its path (`inbox/<id>…`, `assistant/conversations/<id>…`) carry its `X-Persona-Manager`. The inbox repository names an item's manager on each request it makes for it (`asItem`: the `RequestManager` coroutine element, which `OkHttpHubConnection.request` and the actions transport turn into `?manager=`; `""` for the default's names none over any note), so a request that goes out later keeps it; with no record known it names nothing and the page's pin decides (a chat's send uses the one item of that chat, if there is one). An answer about one item comes back unstamped and keeps the record's manager and id. Every item of a manager other than the hub's own assistant has the app id `InboxItemKey.of(id, manager)`, whatever the lists' order, so two managers reading one source are two records and a draft or queued reply never moves to the other's copy; rows and local state go by it, requests take the hub's id back (`hubItemId`), and an id from outside the lists (a notification, a link) finds the first record with that hub id (`inboxRecord`). A page that knows its item's manager pins it on its own requests only (`ManagerPins`, a coroutine context element: the page hands its children the repository and APIs wrapped with it, `InboxRepository.pinned`, `ExtensionManagement.pinned`, `MailTransport.pinned`, and the hub client puts it on the request as `?manager=` where the request is made; a call that moves to another scope takes it along, as the app's shared inbox does into the pairing's scope with `awaitAsCaller`), so what the rest of the app asks for meanwhile names nothing by it; an item's `RequestManager` wins over it, and it over the notes: the inbox item, mail, Lark, Moments and connector thread pages pin their record's manager for the detail calls (and `inbox/<hub id>` for the item itself), and a Lark chat opened that way keeps its manager for its later pages, pictures and downloaded files (each manager's cached apart; the default's keep their names), cards, threads and replies; an extension's page and UI pin the manager from the route (`ExtensionRoute(id, manager)`, `ExtensionUiRoute`, which a page's push and a place's card keep: `ExtensionViewText.pushed` / `opened`), and Settings › Extensions selects a row by (manager, id), since a built-in has the same id under every manager (rows are keyed by manager and id, also before the roster loads). An extension's page shows the connectors it contributes from its own manager's list and pins `connectors/<id>` for their switches. An item page an extension draws has its body drawn and worked, buttons and controls included, by the item's manager's extension under the item's hub id (`rememberInboxItemBody`), and opens its links as that manager (`ManagerLinks`; the shell's `ChatLinkHost` is a `ManagerUriHandler`, and a link inside the document or post it opens natively opens as the same manager); a Moments post's pictures from the hub carry `?manager=` (`LarkMoments.imageUrl`; none for the default's). The main Inbox list shows one row per item; the capsule intentionally remains conversation-based because it presents the message window and composer. The capsule's conversation rows and the main list's read controller (`InboxConversations`, including optional prefetch) read and acknowledge each chat as their row's manager. A chat's composer is one per chat and sends in the pairing's scope, so the chat page and the capsule pass their pins to `ReplyComposer.send(context = …)`: when two managers list the chat, a send goes as the copy it was sent from, and a read of shown messages goes through that copy's window. A chat page also reads where to open (the first unread) from its item's manager's chat list, and a mail page keeps its attachment downloads under its pins (`pinnedForPage`).
- **Extension pushes keep their manager** (#1834): `extension_auth`, `extension_operation` and `extension_ui` read the top-level `manager` (none: the assistant the hub starts, `pushManager`). A sign-in push changes only its own manager's row in Settings › Extensions and only the page of that manager's copy (`ExtensionModel` gets the route's manager); Settings' nav-page badges (the default manager's `…/-/ui`) and the market's catalog page (the default manager's installs) leave other managers' pushes out, as the web does.
- **Bulk inbox actions** (mark all seen, clear all, mark several done): split by each item's manager, one request per manager naming it (`bulkByManager`, `?manager=`), the answers merged, as the web does. A manager that fails (offline) leaves the others' done: its items go back and are the result's `failed` (the page says some were put back); only when every manager fails does the action fail.
- **Settings › Managers › one**: Notifications switch (`ManagerPushModel`, `PUT …/prefs {"muted_managers"}` alone, optimistic). Delete says how many computers go back to the hub's own manager and, in the caution colour, that its sources are deleted with it (`ManagerDeleteDialog`; not while installing). New install code for a never-installed manager that left the roster (`404 no_manager`, 7 days after its code expired) makes it again under the same name. A manager the hub marks `needs_update` has a **Needs update** tag (`Tone.Caution`) in the list and on its page, with a note to update personad where it runs.
- **Move to…**: a row in a computer's page controls (`machineControls` `move`), not for a linked member hub's computer (`kind` `hub`) or another group's; the sheet lists the installed managers (current checked and disabled, offline marked) and calls `MachineManagement.moveToManager` (`POST /api/v1/managers/{id}/machines`); the `machines` push shows the new stamp.
- **Tests**: `ManagerRosterTest` (what is shown under which manager, the current assistant's ownership, the badge's tally: each agent once, quiet and muted ones not), `ShellCountsTest` (app: the shell's counts are the current assistant's, the dot is another assistant's badge), `InboxSwitchHistoryTest` (Done's history follows a switch), `HubManagerHeaderTest` (the current assistant's routes, to-dos keep their manager), `ManagerAvatarTest` (the monogram's colour and initial), `AssistantSwitcherShotsTest` (app: the real sidebar with the switcher closed, open, switched, offline and with one assistant, alone or with one still installing, three styles × light / dark, and the open roster in English, Japanese and at 200 % text (on a short screen too, where the menu scrolls to New assistant and Manage assistants… and a real tap works), with the mock hub's roster, into `app/build/switcher-shots/`; it also picks, opens an installing one's code and Manage assistants…), `HttpDeviceSettingsTest`, `HttpMachineManagementTest`, `AssistantManagerPinTest`, `ManagerMainTargetTest`, `FeedEventTest`, `ManagersStateTest`, `InboxRepositoryTest` (stamps kept, bulk per manager and a manager failing, the same id under two managers), `HubHttpRoutingTest` (a request names the manager it was made for), `InboxHandOffRepositoryTest` (Hand to AI to the item's manager), `KnowledgeOwnerTest` and `KnowledgeRepositoryTest` (an entry's page keeps its assistant after a switch, its question to that one's main conversation), `AgentsCurrentAssistantTest` (Create and link under the agent's assistant, the sheet's to-dos, the offline Agents and To-dos pages), `PlanOwnerTest` (a plan's buttons to its conversation's manager), `InboxManagersShotsTest` / `MachinesManagersShotsTest` (an offline assistant's inbox and computers say so; online and one assistant as before; `inbox-offline-*`, `machines-offline-empty-*`, English and Japanese in `inbox-offline-en.png` / `-ja.png`). Shots (three styles × light / dark, into each module's `build/managers-shots/`):

```sh
./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest --tests '*AssistantSwitcherShotsTest*'
./gradlew :feature:assistant:testDebugUnitTest --tests '*AssistantManagersShotsTest*'
./gradlew :feature:machines:testDebugUnitTest --tests '*MachinesManagersShotsTest*'
./gradlew :feature:inbox:testDebugUnitTest --tests '*InboxManagersShotsTest*'
./gradlew :feature:settings:testDebugUnitTest --tests '*ManagerGroupsShotsTest*' --tests '*ManagerDeleteShotsTest*' --tests '*ManagersShotsTest*'
```

## Inbox AI drafting and handoff; knowledge base categories, entries and tagging

Behavior ported 2026-11-02 from the old main-line App (`81dc6928`), using the corresponding iOS features as reference (`apple/Persona/Views/Inbox/InboxActions.swift`, `KnowledgeView.swift`).

Inbox (`feature/inbox`):

- **AI draft / AI edit** (`InboxAiDraft.kt`): one instruction line and button; without a draft it says **AI draft** (placeholder "How to reply (optional)"), with a draft **AI edit** (placeholder "Ask AI to change: …"). Requests `POST /api/v1/inbox/{id}/draft {"instruction"}`; instruction is two lines, the requirements and "在这版上改：<current draft>" ("Edit this version: …"), either optional (`draftInstruction`). The hub waits until the model finishes, so this request has a separate 120-second read timeout (configured value, not measured, `HttpInboxActionsTransport.DRAFT_READ_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`). The result replaces the reply draft (new `client_id`, `ReplyComposer.replaceWithAi`); not replaced while sending or awaiting result review. Failure says "Couldn't draft: <reason>" (uses the hub's reason when provided; connection issues use shared operation feedback). Entries: **AI draft** above the Lark composer (opens `InboxDraftSheet`, same sheet as **Edit** on the AI twin draft card), inside the mail **Reply** sheet and the bottom **AI draft** button. Old **Summarize / Draft reply** shortcuts are removed: summarizing is the first handoff preset, and drafting now happens in place (the same two actions as iOS).
- **AI-written drafts always require confirmation before sending** (decided): drafts carry `ReplyDraft.ai` (AI twin drafts and AI draft / edit results; changing a few words still counts, deleting everything and rewriting is your own text), stored in the reply log (`InboxStoredDraft.ai`). Sending an AI draft first asks "Send as you?", states "Send to Lark “<title>”" or "Reply to email “<title>”" and shows the full text (`SendAsYouDialog`, `InboxSendConfirm.kt`), the same for inline list cards and draft sheets. Manually typed replies (bottom composer on the Lark chat page) send as typed with no extra confirmation. Mail replies already required confirmation; the wording now shares this dialog and names recipients, warning when the reply target changes. When the result needs review the button is **Check result**, which only checks and does not ask again.
- **Draft card** (`AgentDraftCard.kt`): one status line while the AI twin is processing / queued, "The AI twin couldn't handle this; you can reply directly" on failure, and a card with **Edit / Send** when an AI draft exists. List rows create a reply editor only when the twin has drafted or a stored AI draft exists (creating one per row reads all drafts at once); manually typed drafts still show "Draft · …" inline. Lark chat and mail cards are created on page opening, so an AI draft result appears immediately. Opening mail from a deep link before the list arrives waits for the item before filling the twin draft (only replies untouched and without a stored draft, `offerAiDraft`).
- **Mail page**: below metadata are an AI summary (`JNotice`, title **AI summary**) and AI twin draft card. **Edit** opens the reply sheet; **Send** uses mail's own confirmation and reply-target check, both first switching to the latest email. The reply sheet includes the twin's rationale (while the draft is still its version) and the AI draft line.
- **Row swipe** (`InboxItemRow`, `InboxConversationItemRow`, `inboxRowSwipe`; #4210 T1, alignment decision 5, iOS `InboxView`'s trailing swipe; every style): one swipe from the end, **Done** (the check mark, positive tone) or, on a done item and in the Done section, **Move Back** (the undo arrow, accent tone); none towards the start, none while the row's write is out (`busy`) or while picking items. Glass fills the revealed action with the tone's color (green, blue), Brief and Board keep their neutral backing. TalkBack has the same action in the row's Actions menu (`swipeActionSemantics`, applied by `MessageItem`). The long-press sheet shows the same icons (`doneIcon`), and the label is iOS's **Move Back** (en; 移回, 戻す) wherever the Inbox says it.
- **Hand to AI** (`InboxHandOffSheet.kt`, logic in `InboxHandOff.kt`): a list row's long-press menu (an open row's long press starts a selection, and the picked row's More holds it; no swipe since #4210 T1, as iOS), Lark chat and mail **Hand to AI** open the same sheet: "What should it do" (empty uses the first preset) and three presets: "Summarize, tell me what I need to do, and suggest a reply", "Find the background and tell me", "Handle it for me; ask before replying". "Hand to": Persona (main chat, decided) or the 8 most recently active online Agents (not assistant or offline, newest first; read-only sessions excluded once batch B's capability fields merge), each with machine name; a note when no Agent is online. **Also mark done** is off for every target, including a remembered Agent, and changing targets keeps an explicit user choice (hidden for already-done items). The message matches iOS: `<persona-context kind="context" title="飞书 · <title>|邮件 · <title>" url="">` ("Lark · …" / "Mail · …") + body (hub item context, otherwise "sender: preview"; the mail page uses the full headers and body) + annotation with Lark feedId or mail account_id / mailbox / uid + blank line + instruction; attributes escaped, `</persona-context>` stripped from the body. Persona goes through `POST /api/v1/inbox/{id}/handoff` on the item's manager (`HttpInboxAssistantTransport.handOff`), which records the hand-off so the main chat can write back (`docs/assistant/inbox.md`, "Write-back to the item"); an older hub (404 / 405) falls back to `MainConversationSender.sendToMain` or another manager's main conversation, while offline, a 5xx or an answer without `session` fails without a second send. Agent uses `MessageSender.send` with the same outbox as Agents (`InboxModule` injects `data.messages`). After the Agent accepts the message, the app returns immediately and records the hand-off in the repository's background scope through the same endpoint with the Agent session, `kind: "agent"`, its display title and the operation id, but no text; an old hub's 400 or any other recording failure is ignored without resending or failing the hand-off; the next inbox refresh carries any item update. The same text to the same target reuses the operation ID (`HandOffAttempt`), so retry doesn't queue twice. Once accepted, close the sheet, mark done if checked (failure only shows a notice), then open the main conversation the hand-off answered (the Persona tab after a fallback) or that Agent's chat; there the message is a context card plus an instruction bubble. Failure says "Couldn't hand it over: <reason>" and leaves the sheet open.
- **List**: type labels follow the old App: @everyone shows **@everyone** (`lark.at_all`); mail only copied to me shows **Cc** (omitted `mail.direct` means false). Empty states: "Your inbox is empty" (explains configuring Lark or adding email); a search with no match says "No results"; a source or kind filter that leaves nothing open says "Nothing open matches these filters", with the filter mark and **Clear filters** (it clears the source, the kind and the search, and the Inbox outside those filters may still hold open items). A bay with nothing in it, and no source or kind filter, still says "All caught up" with the check mark. `InboxFilteredEmptyShotsTest` draws the filtered empty state in Glass, Brief and Board, light and dark (`build/inbox-filtered-empty-shots/`).
- Set `LocalGlassSource` to null when putting `ComposerFrame` in a sheet (see "Lessons").

Knowledge base (`feature/knowledge`):

- **Six categories**: Projects / Chats / People / Documents / Meetings / Events, horizontal chips with count on the selected category. Chats include private chats (old App, iOS and `docs/archive/redesign/parity.md` 3.1 all include every chat; previously only groups). Chips hide during search (search covers all categories). Projects are split into Focus / Other active (Active when there is no focus) / Completed / Not a project; Not a project is collapsed by default, with why entries are here and how to restore them. Other categories use one "By importance and relevance" section (named #tag when tagged). Reloading the same view (pull, refresh, after saving tags) keeps old content instead of flashing a loading spinner.
- **Search results**: merged by entry, recognizing entries even from `docs/<id>.json`; a sentence appears once, profiles and timelines before reference material, grouped by category. Each entry shows at most two hits: source (Profile / Timeline / Reference / Chat log · date) and the matching line with list markers, JSON keys and quotes removed; every query word bolded. More than two says "N matches". Entries not seen in lists initially use the category as their name (the category list loads in the background to fill it).
- **Entry page**: header shows importance, project short-term / long-term and state, relevance, all tags, one sentence, **Ask Persona** (existing), and **Start Agent** only for projects. "Agents working on this project" uses the hub's `agents`, only online non-assistants and no subtask of one listed there (`projectAgents`, see **Subtasks** in "Aligning the Agents list and the Machines page (#3038)"; `AgentRow`, tap opens that chat). Timeline newest first, each row a "time · source" line and content line, newest highlighted; initially 6, **Show all N / Collapse**. Profile drops the title, lines repeating the one-sentence summary, and "importance … relevance"; hidden if nothing remains. Relations grouped by category (related projects / chats / people / …), initially 4 each, **All N / Collapse**. Code directories show trailing path components and machine; "Messages in the last 7 days" is collapsed; "Directory" explains "Persona, the AI twin and Agents on your computers can read this directory directly."
- **Recording minutes** (#2738, `KnowledgeMinutes.kt`; `docs/assistant/meeting-minutes.md`): a recording's page shows `detail.minutes` above its transcript, as iOS does: when · where and who was there, then Key points, Decisions, Action items and Open questions, each item with the time it was said as a chip that scrolls the page to that transcript line. The transcript leaves out Persona's echo (`echo: true` segments, #5905, `docs/assistant/meeting-minutes.md` "Persona's echo"), as the web does, so a time never opens one (`KnowledgePresentation.kt` `transcript`). Copy and Share, under who was there as on iOS, send the minutes' Markdown. Make minutes (none yet, transcript complete) or Regenerate posts `POST /api/v1/recordings/{id}/minutes`; while `meta.minutes_state` is `pending` the page reads the recording again every 5 s (Writing… without minutes, Updating… over older ones). Each action item offers Add to to-dos, and Add all when more than one is left (`POST …/minutes/todos` with the version shown); an added one says Added. After each write the page reads the recording back before another is allowed; `409 minutes_stale` says the minutes changed and shows the new ones. A failed write says why with Close. A meeting whose transcript is incomplete says minutes come after it; an expired transcript (`trimmed_at`) keeps its minutes and offers no Regenerate. `KnowledgeMinutesTest` covers the states, a time's transcript line and the writes' read-back; `KnowledgeRepositoryTest` the wire; `KnowledgeMinutesShotsTest` the page and the states (`knowledge-minutes-*`, three styles × light / dark, English and Chinese) and a time scrolling to its line.
- **Tagging** (`KnowledgeTagEditor.kt`): top-right # opens "Tag · <name>": importance P0–P3 (P0, P1 have state-color dots), "Relevance to me N" slider (0–100, step 5, new design system `JSlider`), space-separated tags; projects also have type Short-term (request) / Long-term (mission) and state Active / Paused / Completed / Not a project, explaining "Manual tags are locked and won't be overwritten by the hub's automatic organization." Save uses `PUT /api/v1/kb/{kind}/{id}`, only set fields (an empty tag list must be sent); while saving **Saving…**, failure "Not saved: <reason>" stays in the sheet; success reloads entry and list (`KnowledgeEdits`). Relevance retains its original value until the slider moves (not rounded to 5), so changing other fields doesn't alter it.
- **Start Agent**: for projects, with the first code directory (`<machine>:<path>`) and "project background + blank line" as the first message (`KnowledgeAgentStart`). The shell wires `knowledgeEntries(…, onNewAgent = …)` to the prefilled new-Agent entry (batch E's `NewAgentRoute`); without wiring it falls back to copying the background, switching to Agents and prompting to paste it into a new session.

Mock hub: `MOCK_DRAFT_SECONDS` (AI drafting wait, default 1.5 seconds), `MOCK_DRAFT_FAIL=1` (draft fails, 502), `GET /mock/inbox_drafts` (each draft's instruction); `MOCK_INBOX_LABELS=1` adds an @everyone group message and an AI twin draft and rationale to the Tuesday-sharing email; `MOCK_NO_LIVE_AGENTS=1` makes all Agents offline (handoff only offers Persona); `MOCK_KB_SAVE_FAIL=1` fails tagging save (502); `MOCK_KB_MANY_LINKS=1` relates "天气 App" ("Weather App") to 6 people (inspect **All 6 / Collapse**). Existing knowledge data has an empty Events category for inspecting its empty state; `GET /mock/kb_updates` shows tagging requests.

Unit tests: `InboxHandOffTest` in `feature/inbox` (iOS-matching handoff message, recent online Agents, operation ID, row labels), `InboxDraftTest` (instruction composition, AI flag lifecycle, no replacing drafts during sending, twin drafts only fill untouched replies, confirmation wording); `InboxHandOffRepositoryTest` in `core/data` (`at_all` / `mail` / `direct` parsing, AI and manual draft row states, which outbox handoff to Persona / Agent uses, the hand-off route and its 404 / 405 fallback, drafting through actions), `InboxHandOffShotsTest` (target defaults, retry with the same operation; sheet shots in `build/inbox-handoff-shots/`); `InboxActionsTransportTest` in `core/hub` (draft path, request body, empty draft and failure), `KnowledgeRepositoryTest` (agents online non-assistants only, PUT only set fields); `KnowledgePresentationTest` in `feature/knowledge` (project sections, six categories, hit entry / source / text, merge by entry, word-by-word bolding, timeline, profile dedupe, code directory, new-Agent prefilling, tag form and body), `KnowledgeTaggingFlowTest` (save failure stays in sheet, successful save reloads entry and list, online non-assistant Agents only); `SliderTest` in `core:designsystem`.

## Inbox: new items waiting for triage (#1773)

The shared three-client specification (placement, wording, fallback, source naming, setup navigation, mock hub `MOCK_INBOX_TRIAGE`) is in [inbox.md](../assistant/inbox.md), "AI triage" → "Waiting-for-triage banner". Android specifics:

- **Data**: on every Inbox refresh `RealInboxRepository` reads `triage` into `InboxSnapshot.triage` (`InboxTriage.kt` in `feature/inbox`); old hubs without the field produce null and no banner.
- **Banner**: a separate list item after the filter/search item (`item("triage")` in `InboxScreen`), using design system `JNotice`, the needs-you state color (amber border in Glass, amber strip in Board, Brief's own notice style), and `Clock` icon (waiting, like web). Copy in `InboxTriageText.banner` matches web `triageBanner` case by case.
- **Sources not read this round** (#1849): `InboxTriageSource` also reads `read_error` / `read_reason`. Any source with a `read_error` (its items are the last round's) gets one line, "name: the hub's `read_reason`" (or "Not read this round; the list may be incomplete" without one), in a quiet `JNotice` (`item("triage-unread")`, `InboxUnreadNotice`) under the banner, separate from it: a failed read changes neither triage's state nor its waiting count. It goes with the next good read. Shots: `InboxTriageShotsTest.saysWhichSourcesWereNotReadThisRound` (`inbox-unread-*`).
- **Set up**: opens `ModelPurposeRoute("inbox.triage")`. The shell (`InboxTriageSetup` in `Root.kt`) decides whether settings can be changed: when the banner needs a button, read this purpose once (`GET /api/v1/model-config/purposes/inbox.triage`), offer the button only if readable, remember a successful read. Answer-only devices (403) and hubs without this purpose show text only. Not in demo mode.
- **Screenshots (no emulator)**: `./gradlew :feature:inbox:testDebugUnitTest --tests '*InboxTriageShotsTest*'`, Robolectric native rendering of the whole Inbox page, three styles × light / dark × three cases (no model with **Set up**, backlog, multiple sources with a hub reason), PNGs in `build/inbox-triage-shots/`.
- **Unit tests**: `InboxTriageTest` (parsing, old hub, one / several sources, hub reason and fallback, unknown state, setup only for missing model), `InboxTriageShotsTest` (copy present, setup appears only without a model and opens settings, text only for devices unable to change settings, hidden for ok and old hub), `InboxHandOffRepositoryTest.refreshCarriesTheHubsTriage` in `core/data`.

## Inbox: events, suggestions and handling chain (phase 1)

Behavior and API follow the first-phase Inbox specification shared by all four clients: `status` / `urgent` / `recap` / `handling` / `suggestion` / `chain` / `related`, and `POST /api/v1/inbox/{id}/suggestion`. This section covers Android only. Old hubs omit these fields, so sectioning and navigation keep their fallbacks; the rows take the shared three-line shape below.

- **Data**: `InboxEvents.kt` in `core:contracts` defines `InboxHandling`, `InboxSuggestion`, `InboxChainEntry`, `InboxChainLink` and `InboxRelated`, also added to `WireModels.InboxItem`. `inboxEventFields(item)` reads fields independently; a malformed field or chain entry is dropped on its own. Unknown `status` falls back to tier; unknown chain kinds remain, showing the hub's `text` with a generic icon. `position` accepts strings or numbers. `RealInboxRepository` stores them in `InboxRecord.event`, also putting `status` and `urgent` into `core:domain`'s `InboxItem`. The cache retains raw hub JSON, including these fields on cold start.
- **Sections** (`buildInboxList` in `core:domain`): one hub item is one row, including separate items from the same source conversation; Android does not regroup them client-side. When present, `status` determines `InboxItem.section`: Needs you, In progress, FYI, Collapsed, then Done. **In progress** sits between Needs you and FYI and is always expanded. In-progress items with action tier do not count as needing you or toward `actionCount`. Shared fixture: `spec/fixtures/inbox/status.json`. Old hubs' AI-twin-drafting rows join that same section exactly once; its legacy heading remains **Persona is handling it**, while hubs sending `status` use **In progress**. Reading never moves an item into a separate read-pending section.
- **Rows**: in-progress rows use working state and show who is handling the item (AI reviewing / Agent working / Waiting for their reply). Urgent items still being reviewed (`urgent`, `handling.state == looking`, no suggestion) say **AI is preparing a suggestion**. Suggestions show their kind and first line, such as **Suggested reply: first line**. Failed handbacks say **AI couldn't finish; please take a look** (`eventMark` in `InboxEvents.kt`). With `suggestion` or `handling`, rows, Lark chats, mail pages and extension item pages hide the old AI twin draft card/status: the hub mirrors reply suggestions into `agent` for older Apps and they must not be shown twice. AI drafting and your own drafts remain unchanged.
- **Follow-up display (#6611)**: optional `where`, `follow.who` / `next` / `check_at`, `now` and chain `via` are decoded independently and never make an item open the event page by themselves. Every `InboxItemRow`, with or without `where`, draws title + time / AI summary / one source-specific latest line, rather than making the source conversation or sender a second title. With `where`, the third line is `where · sender: preview`, with a direct chat named once. Without `where`, it follows the existing iOS / web `from` projection: document type · preview, a Moments sender · preview, connector reference · sender · preview (or reference · preview), translated mail sender · translated preview, direct-chat preview alone, group / mail sender · preview, or preview alone without a sender. A summary equal to the preview is not drawn twice only for the assistant's own source; other sources keep their third line even when those strings match.
